# Ecosystem Marketplace — Changelog

One dated entry per implementation task, in the order tasks land. Each entry: what changed, why, files touched, and a pointer to the relevant `HLD.md`/`LLD/` section. Newest entries at the top.

---

## 2026-09-30 — Connectors + Plugins phase (Stages 0-5)

New `item_type`s `connector`/`mcp_server`/`plugin` go from inert placeholders to real, flag-gated behavior. Branch `feature/ecosystem-connectors-plugins`; plan docs `docs/ecosystem/CONNECTORS_PHASE_PLAN.md`/`PLUGINS_PHASE_PLAN.md`; contract `CONTRACTS.md` §19/§20; manual-testing detail `TESTING_GUIDE.md` §14; team try-it-out guide, new `TEAM_TESTING.md`. Every new flag defaults off; the whole phase is additive.

- **Stage 1 — credential broker + tool-calling core**: `store/ecosystem_secret_store.py` (per-org secret CRUD, AES-256-GCM via `core/ckms`'s `KeyService`, modeled on `store/credential_vault.py`'s shape but not its flat instance-wide key) + `services/ecosystem/credential_broker_service.py` (wraps the existing `connectors/oauth2.py` `OAuth2Handler` rather than reimplementing PKCE/refresh/revoke; read-through over `connectors/registry.py` for native connectors; new remote-MCP protected-resource-metadata discovery, SSRF-guarded). Additive `tools` param on `models/model_router.py`'s non-streaming `generate()` path + the 4 fixed-signature gateway adapters (`gateway_claude/openai/gemini/local_llm.py`) plus the 2 already-`**kwargs`-shaped ones — a *separate*, thinner, single-turn passthrough from the pre-existing `generate_with_tools()` multi-round loop, not a replacement for it. New `mcp/tool_annotations.py` (read/write/destructive classification, defaults conservatively to write). Regression-proved byte-identical for every existing caller when `tools` is omitted (dedicated test suite + confirmed the one pre-existing unrelated test failure reproduces identically on the pre-change baseline).
- **Stage 2 — Connectors**: `routers/ecosystem_connectors_router.py` (connect/disconnect/reconnect/oauth-callback/admin-oauth-apps/tool-calls-pending-approve-deny — every handler derives org/user from the authenticated caller only, closing the impersonation gap noted against the existing `/connectors/execute`/`/connectors/status-for-user` endpoints), `services/ecosystem/tool_approval_service.py` (server-side approval enforcement + mandatory audit row on every path), gate stage 7 (`mcp_connector_stage.py`) filled in with real SSRF/HTTPS/OAuth-metadata/tool-classification checks for `connector`/`mcp_server` items. `packages/ecosystem-ui/src/components/Connectors/*` + `ai-ui` chat cards (built, not yet wired into `Chat.jsx` — disclosed). **Real integration bug found and fixed during review**: the frontend's `connect()`/`reconnect()` send a body-less POST, but the route handlers' Pydantic body params had no default, so FastAPI 422'd on exactly the request shape the real client sends — caught by a new regression test that posts with no body at all, not a `json={}` stand-in.
- **Stage 3 — Advanced: MCP servers**: `services/ecosystem/mcp_runtime_service.py` (local/stdio server lifecycle — health-check/exponential-backoff-restart/idle-shutdown on top of `sandbox/docker_executor.py`'s existing container-security posture, for long-running rather than one-shot containers), new `workers/start_workers.py --mcp-runtime` flag (its own dedicated process, never combined with `--gate`/`--gate-sweeper` per the 2026-09-28 fork+lock incident), admin runtime status/logs/restart endpoints. **Real gap found and fixed**: `services/ecosystem/create_service.py`'s `create_via_write()` silently dropped `connector_url`/`server_url`/`oauth`/`tools`/`endpoints` from a created item's manifest, so gate stage 7 had nothing real to check — no new creation endpoint was needed once this was fixed, the existing `POST /ecosystem/items` pipeline already worked.
- **Stage 4 — Plugins**: `services/ecosystem/plugin_manifest.py` (composition validation — namespace existence, no cross-part duplicates, license compatibility), install fan-out (`EcosystemInstall.managed_by_plugin_install_id`, new column) — a plugin install creates tagged child installs, a managed child refuses direct uninstall (same shape as the existing `scope="required"` refusal), the parent uninstall frees/reassigns children correctly including the shared-child case, new-version diffing frees/adds parts without disturbing an unrelated standalone install. New `POST /ecosystem/items/{id}/plugin-compose`. **Two real bugs found and fixed, the same regression class `CONTRACTS.md` §9 already documents once for `Install.version_id`**: `items_service.py` never included `managed_by_plugin_install_id` in an item's summary dict, and `ecosystem_router.py`'s response models didn't declare it either — so the field never survived the wire even though the service layer computed it correctly. Fixed both; added a real HTTP-level regression test (every other test in this stage had asserted against the DB/service layer directly, which is exactly why this slipped through).
- **Stage 5 — catalog + security + E2E**: new `McpServerRepoSource`/`ActivepiecesSource` crawler config (loader code only — `docs/ecosystem/catalog/stage5-sources-candidates.md` proposes 2 MCP repos + 12 Activepieces pieces, real live-verified license/neutrality data, `sources.yaml` itself untouched pending sign-off). Offline bundle export widened to connector/mcp_server/plugin metadata. **Real security bug found and fixed**: `tool_approval_service.py`'s auto-approve check didn't distinguish write from destructive classification, so a destructive tool with an org auto-approve policy row (for any reason — added for a different tool, added in error) could skip human approval entirely; fixed to gate auto-approve on `classification == "write"` only. New security test suite (token isolation, no-tokens-in-logs, cross-org 404s, SSRF, the destructive-approval regression itself) and one new E2E spec (written, not yet run against a live flags-on backend).
- **CI**: the "Ecosystem OpenAPI/TS-enum contract conformance" check (task B-17) genuinely failed on the Stage 4/5 commits — `docs/ecosystem/openapi.json` had drifted after Stage 4's schema field additions and never got regenerated. Root-caused via the real GitHub Actions job logs, fixed by running the existing generator scripts for real and reverifying `--check` passes clean.

Files: see `docs/ecosystem/CONTRACTS.md` §19/§20 for the full new-endpoint contract; `porting-pack/CHANGESET.md`'s Connectors+Plugins delta section for the complete file inventory.
Tests: 710/710 passing in the full `tests/services/ecosystem` directory (clean container, post-fix), plus dedicated suites per stage (14-15 tests each) and a 9-test security suite — all confirmed for real against the shared `ainxt-gateway` container's real Postgres/Redis, not carried over from any sub-task's own unverified claim.

## 2026-09-29 — Porting-pack round: provider-agnostic signer + offline catalog bundle mode

Built for re-implementing this feature in a different, GitLab-hosted, air-gapped codebase (no internet/GitHub access) -- both changes are also real, standalone improvements to this repo, not porting-pack-only code.

- **Provider-agnostic signed-index verification**: `services/ecosystem/catalog_crawler/signing.py`'s `TrustedSigner` matched on GitHub-specific Fulcio certificate extensions (`GitHubWorkflowRepository`/`GitHubWorkflowName`) -- a GitLab CI signer would never satisfy it. Redesigned to match on the provider-agnostic extensions every OIDC issuer populates (`OIDCIssuer` + `OIDCSourceRepositoryURI`, optionally `OIDCBuildConfigURI`) -- confirmed empirically both before and after against this repo's own real, checked-in GitHub Actions signed-bundle fixture (decoded its actual X.509 certificate extensions directly rather than assuming). `ECOSYSTEM_CATALOG_TRUSTED_SIGNER`'s JSON shape changed from `{"issuer","repository","workflow_name"}` to `{"issuer","source_repository_uri","build_config_uri"?}`.
- **Offline catalog bundle mode**: new `ECOSYSTEM_CATALOG_SOURCE_MODE` (`online` default | `bundle`) + `ECOSYSTEM_CATALOG_BUNDLE_PATH` (a local filesystem path or an internal `https://` URL, deliberately NOT run through `import_adapters/ssrf_guard.py`'s public-address-only check -- that guard is for attacker-influenceable pointer data, not this operator-set trusted config). New `sync_catalog_from_bundle()`/`_read_content_from_bundle()`/`run_configured_sync()` (`services/ecosystem/catalog_sync.py`) -- same signature verification, upsert/stale-marking, and content-hash drift check as the online path, reading bytes from a bundle instead of the public internet. `materialize_from_catalog()` reads skill content from the bundle in this mode with a real, tested guarantee of zero network calls. New `scripts/ecosystem/export_offline_bundle.py` produces the bundle (real run against the live dev DB: 56 of 184 candidate skills exported -- the rest correctly skipped for real reasons: unlicensed/non-approved, or a live-fetch rate-limit on a never-installed catalog pointer with no `GITHUB_IMPORT_TOKEN` configured).

Files: `services/ecosystem/catalog_crawler/signing.py`, `core/config.py`, `services/ecosystem/catalog_sync.py`, `routers/ecosystem_router.py`, `.github/workflows/ecosystem-catalog-crawl.yml`; new `scripts/ecosystem/export_offline_bundle.py`; updated `tests/services/ecosystem/catalog_crawler/test_signing.py`, `test_admin_sources.py`, `test_catalog_load.py`, `test_catalog_sync.py`; new `tests/services/ecosystem/test_catalog_sync_bundle_mode.py` (8 tests, including a real proof that bundle-mode install makes zero network calls).
Tests: 634/634 passing in `tests/services/ecosystem` (11 new/changed net), real Postgres/Redis, confirmed inside the shared `ainxt-gateway` container.

## 2026-09-29 — Install-state consistency across Discover/Yours/Detail/chat, one shared client-side store

Real bug reported live: "installed a catalog skill, uninstalled it from Yours (worked), went back to Discover -- it still shows Installed and can't be removed there." Root cause: Discover/Yours/Detail each only ever refreshed THEIR OWN fetched data on a mutation -- nothing told the other screens (or catalogCache.ts's own tab-switch cache) that anything had changed. Discover's own `Card` also never offered an uninstall action at all for an installed item (a read-only "Added" badge only) -- part of this same round adds one, closing the user's own explicit "uninstall from Discover works" test requirement.

- New `packages/ecosystem-ui/src/installStore.ts`: a module-level, per-tab store (same architectural pattern as `installTracking.ts`'s in-flight tracker, but for the STEADY-STATE result) keyed by item id. `setInstallState()`/`removeInstallTracking()` write immediately from any mutation call site and patch `catalogCache.ts`'s own cached Discover/Yours entries in place (`patchCachedInstallState()`/`removeItemFromCaches()`, new); `applyInstallOverride()` + `useInstallOverrideVersion()` let any currently-mounted consumer (Card.tsx, Detail.tsx) re-render with the corrected state on the same tick, no remount or refetch required.
- Cross-tab half: new `EcosystemClient.streamChanges(onEvent)` (optional -- ad-hoc test client objects typed `as EcosystemClient` don't need it) subscribes to the real, already-existing per-org `ecosystem.changed` SSE relay (`GET /ecosystem/events/stream`, `routers/ecosystem_events_router.py`) -- the exact mechanism ai-ui's own chat "/" menu (`useEcosystemChatSkills.js`) already uses for the same reason. `RealEcosystemClient` implements it with the same fetch+ReadableStream SSE parsing that hook already established (a plain `EventSource` can't carry this client's own auth); `MockEcosystemClient` gets a real (not no-op) subscriber registry plus a test-only `emitChangeEvent()`. Discover.tsx/Yours.tsx/Detail.tsx all subscribe, refetching on any event.
- `Card.tsx`: an installed item with `allowed_actions.includes("uninstall")` now renders a real, clickable control (locks to the old read-only badge otherwise, matching `InstalledMenu`'s own required-install convention) that calls `client.uninstall()` and patches the store immediately. Deliberately does NOT optimistically patch the store on a fresh `install()`'s own Job response -- `Detail.test.tsx`'s own "survives an unmount + remount mid-install" test (a job can be genuinely "verifying" with an install row that exists but isn't the confirmed final truth yet) caught this the first time; the real broadcast point is the refetch `useInstallStatus`'s `onResolved` already triggers once the poll confirms resolution.
- `Yours.tsx`: every mutation (uninstall/setEnabled/deleteDraft/deprecateItem/unshare) now goes through a shared `runMutation()` helper -- patches the store on success, and on failure either silently refreshes (a `NOT_FOUND`, meaning it's already gone -- the fix's own explicit "never fail silently" requirement) or surfaces a real error (`data-testid="yours-row-action-error"`) instead of the previous unhandled-promise-rejection (several of these calls had no `.catch()` at all before this round).
- `Detail.tsx`: broadcasts its own fetched item's install state to the shared store on every successful load/reload (covers `AddDialog`'s own Add, quick-install, uninstall, and toggle-enabled uniformly, since they all end in the same refetch); `handleUninstall`/`handleToggleEnabled` now handle `NOT_FOUND` by refreshing instead of only showing a stale error over an already-wrong badge.
- Two real, separate backend gaps closed for the SSE half to actually cover every mutation the fix's own scope named: `deprecate_item` (`routers/ecosystem_router.py`) and `unshare()` (`services/ecosystem/policy_service.py`) never published `ecosystem.changed` at all before this round.

Files: new `packages/ecosystem-ui/src/installStore.ts`; `catalogCache.ts`, `client/{EcosystemClient,RealEcosystemClient,MockEcosystemClient}.ts`, `components/{Card,Discover,Yours,Detail}.tsx`; `routers/ecosystem_router.py`, `services/ecosystem/policy_service.py`; new `ai-ui/e2e/install-state-consistency.spec.ts`.
Tests: 214/214 passing in `packages/ecosystem-ui` (11 new: 5 in `Card.test.tsx`, 2 in `Discover.test.tsx`, 4 in `Yours.test.tsx`), `tsc --noEmit` clean; 623/623 passing in `tests/services/ecosystem` (2 new, real Postgres/Redis); 2/2 new real-Chrome Playwright specs passing against the real backend (screenshots: `install-consistency-discover-shows-added.png`, `install-consistency-yours-after-uninstall.png`, `install-consistency-discover-corrected.png`, `install-consistency-uninstalled-from-discover.png`) -- confirmed live: a real catalog item added from Discover, uninstalled from Yours, and Discover shows "+ Add" again with no reload; a real catalog item added and uninstalled from Discover's own card directly.

## 2026-09-29 — Pre-crawl hardening: sources.yaml load fails loudly on an unknown taxonomy category

Before dispatching the widened catalog's first real crawl, closed the actual root cause behind both this round's "operations" gap and the earlier "engineering"/"security" gap: nothing validated a source's `category` against the real taxonomy before it reached the DB, so a typo or a stale value would only ever be found by noticing items missing from Discover, hours or rounds later.

- `services/ecosystem/config_service.py`: extracted the inline categories list into a module-level `TAXONOMY_CATEGORIES` constant (was previously only reachable through a full DB-backed `get_effective_config()` call) — single source of truth, now also imported by the check below.
- `services/ecosystem/catalog_crawler/sources_config.py`'s `load_sources()`: validates every `github_repos[].category`/`well_known_sites[].category` against `TAXONOMY_CATEGORIES` and raises `ValueError` (surfaced as `sources_yaml_error` in the admin Sources screen, same existing convention) listing every offending source and its category, if any aren't recognized — fails the load itself, before any crawl runs, rather than letting bad data land.
- Confirmed live: re-ran the same GitHub-tree-based freshness check against the 4 already-excluded round-1 candidates (`tawanorg/skills` still 404, `KraitDev/skiLL.Md`/`MaxSchoon/ixbrl`/`vythanhtra/perplexity-skills` all unchanged file-for-file since their original evaluation) — nothing new to add from them.
- `max_total_items` (10000) was NOT raised further — the realistic post-crawl total (existing catalog + this round's estimated ≤526 new passing entries across the 8 newly-added repos) stays well under 1,000, so the existing cap already has >10x headroom. Per-source/total over-cap reporting (`CrawlReport.over_cap`, never a silent truncation) already existed from an earlier round — confirmed, not rebuilt.

Files: `services/ecosystem/config_service.py`, `services/ecosystem/catalog_crawler/sources_config.py`; new tests in `tests/services/ecosystem/catalog_crawler/test_sources_config.py` (+4: rejects a bad github_repos category, rejects a bad well_known_sites category, accepts every real taxonomy category, the real checked-in sources.yaml still loads cleanly), `tests/services/ecosystem/test_config_service.py` (unchanged count, now reads the shared constant).
Tests: 621/621 passing in the full `tests/services/ecosystem` suite, confirmed for real against the shared `ainxt-gateway` container's real Postgres/Redis.

## 2026-09-29 — Catalog-widening round 2: 8 of 9 candidate sources added to `sources.yaml`, one held on a license gap

Stop-point-1 artifact (per `docs/ecosystem/EXTERNAL_SOURCES_PLAN.md` §7): evaluated the 9 previously-held/new candidate repos for the full (non-starter) catalog, per an explicit per-repo license/neutrality/provenance check. No crawl was run — this only updates the reviewed allowlist for a maintainer sign-off before the next real crawl.

Real license text (root `LICENSE`/`LICENSE.md`, fetched directly, never trusted from GitHub's own license-detection API alone) and the real `scan_for_ai_vendor_names()` neutrality scanner (against every actual `SKILL.md`'s content, not a sample) were run against all 9:

- **Added** (8): `expo/skills` (22/26 pass neutrality), `tryterra/agent-skills` (9/9), `hookdeck/agent-skills` (3/3), `googleworkspace/cli` (95/95), `TheodoreAD/agent-skills` (only 7/15 pass — scoped to those 7 real paths via `include_paths`, not left to the automatic per-item filter, given how vendor-specific the rest of the repo is), `K-Dense-AI/scientific-agent-skills` (134/166 pass; license is `LICENSE.md`, verified real MIT text), `Orchestra-Research/AI-Research-SKILLs` (only 21/98 pass — most of this repo is organized one skill per named ML model/framework, so most individual skills legitimately fail by naming their own subject matter), `nexu-io/open-design` (235/277 pass within `include_paths: [skills, design-templates]` — the other 263 of its 540 total `SKILL.md` files live under `plugins/`/`.claude/`, structurally a specific coding-agent CLI's own plugin-marketplace layout per the repo's own description, excluded wholesale rather than per-item).
- **Held** (1): `vercel-labs/agent-skills` — the "owner name is a vendor name" concern from the prior round is resolved (Vercel is a hosting company, not an AI vendor), but the repo has no LICENSE file at all, only an informal "## License\nMIT" line in its README — weaker evidence than every other source in this file. Held pending a real LICENSE file, not a per-skill content problem (8/9 skills would otherwise pass neutrality).

Every repo above that isn't fully clean relies on the crawler's own existing per-item neutrality/license check (already run at crawl time and again at the install-time gate) to reject the individual non-neutral skills — `sources.yaml` only pre-excludes at the directory level (`include_paths`/`exclude_paths`) where the reason is structural (an entire subtree organized around a specific CLI, or a whole repo where hand-verifying the small clean subset was explicitly requested), never as a manual enumeration of every failing file.

`crawl_limits.max_skills_per_repo` raised from 50 to 300 — the largest newly-added, in-scope repo count is `nexu-io/open-design`'s 277-file scoped total; `max_total_items` (10000) already had enough headroom and wasn't changed. `services/ecosystem/config_service.py`'s taxonomy already includes `research` (added in an earlier round alongside `engineering`/`security`) and `design` — no taxonomy change was needed for this round.

Files: `docs/ecosystem/catalog/sources.yaml` only (8 new `github_repos:` entries, updated header-comment documentation, raised `max_skills_per_repo`).
Verification: `services.ecosystem.catalog_crawler.sources_config.load_sources()` parses the updated file with no errors — 21 total `github_repos` entries, correct `category`/`tags`/`include_paths`/`needs_product`/`account_required` on all 8 new ones, confirmed by direct inspection inside the `ainxt-gateway` container. No crawl dispatched.

## 2026-09-29 — Process hygiene: every ecosystem service self-reports its commit; a real sync-and-restart script

Real, repeated incident this same session: stale Docker containers serving old code produced false live bugs at least four times, most severely `ainxt-gate-worker` running 27 hours (predating the priority-lane feature) while every real Add enqueued into the new `_high`/`_low` lanes — nothing consumed them, a growing invisible backlog (56 queued in `_high`, 24 in `_low`) that exactly matched a live user report of "55 jobs ahead of this one." Each incident was only found by manually inspecting `docker top`/logs/queue depths — nothing anywhere said "you're running old code."

Built the fix for the *process*, not just that one incident:

- New `services/ecosystem/service_health.py`: `report_service_startup(service_name)` — called once at a process's own real startup, writes `{service, commit, started_at, pid}` to Redis (`ecosystem:service_health:{name}`, same fire-and-forget convention as `catalog_sync.py`'s `_persist_last_sync_status()`, never blocks/fails startup on a Redis hiccup). `report_gate_worker_startup(queue_names)` — gate-worker-specific: `logger.error`s loudly (not `.info`) if the worker didn't register every `ECOSYSTEM_GATE_QUEUES_BY_PRIORITY` lane, the exact 2026-09-29 incident's own detection. `get_all_service_health()` — reads all three known services back, computes `commit_mismatch` per service against the gateway's own reported commit, returns a flat `warnings: list[str]` (a service that never reported, a commit mismatch, missing priority lanes). Commit is read from a `.ecosystem_sync_commit` marker file if present (written by the new sync script below, since `docker cp`-based dev syncing leaves the image's baked-in `GIT_COMMIT` env var stale the instant a file is copied in), falling back to `GIT_COMMIT` otherwise — the correct behavior for a real deployment that rebuilds images rather than `docker cp`s into them.
- Wired into `gateway.py`'s startup event, `workers/start_workers.py`'s `--gate` worker process and its `--gate-sweeper` loop.
- `GET /ecosystem/admin/sources` (`routers/ecosystem_router.py`) now includes `service_health` in its response; new "Ecosystem service health" section in `AdminSources.tsx` — a warnings list plus a per-service commit/started/status table.
- New `scripts/ecosystem/sync_and_restart_ecosystem_services.py`: the actual restart-orchestration item 1 asked for. Refuses to run against a dirty working tree by default (a `git archive`-based sync only ever reflects the committed tree, never uncommitted work — silently syncing past uncommitted changes would be worse than refusing). For each of `ainxt-gateway`/`ainxt-gate-worker`/`ainxt-gate-sweeper`: `git archive <commit> | docker exec -i <container> tar -x -C /app` (deliberately not a whole-directory `docker cp`, which this session repeatedly found unreliable — `git archive` extraction is atomic and always reflects exactly the named commit, nothing stale left over from a previous sync), writes the commit into that container's own `.ecosystem_sync_commit`, restarts it, waits for the gateway's `/health` and every service's own self-report to confirm the new commit, then prints the final self-reported state and any warnings. Deliberately does not manage catalog sync (no persistent process — triggered by the scheduler or an admin's "Sync now") or the native `ai-ui` dev server (this script cannot safely discover/stop an arbitrary process on the operator's own machine) — prints a reminder for both instead.

Files: new `services/ecosystem/service_health.py`, new `scripts/ecosystem/sync_and_restart_ecosystem_services.py`; `gateway.py`, `workers/start_workers.py`, `routers/ecosystem_router.py`, `packages/ecosystem-ui/src/components/admin/AdminSources.tsx`, `packages/ecosystem-ui/src/types.ts`, `packages/ecosystem-ui/src/client/{MockEcosystemClient,fixtures}.ts`; new `tests/services/ecosystem/test_service_health.py` (6 new), +2 in `tests/services/ecosystem/test_admin_sources.py`, +2 in `packages/ecosystem-ui/src/components/admin/AdminSources.test.tsx`.
Tests: 13/13 passing in `test_admin_sources.py` + `test_service_health.py` combined, confirmed for real against the shared `ainxt-gateway` container's real Postgres/Redis (`POSTGRES_DB=ainxt_test`); 9/9 frontend (`AdminSources.test.tsx`); `tsc --noEmit` clean.

## 2026-09-29 — Live search "From the web," UI (follow-up to the backend round below)

Built the Discover UI the backend round below explicitly disclosed as not-yet-built.

- Real, small config-exposure gap found and closed first: `GET /ecosystem/config`'s `policy_summary.live_sources_enabled` was already a straight pass-through of the org's own raw policy toggle (`services/ecosystem/policy_service.py`'s `get_policy()`), but nothing surfaced the actual EFFECTIVE "both the instance-wide `ECOSYSTEM_LIVE_SOURCES` flag AND the org toggle are true" signal `live_search_service.live_search_enabled()` computes -- a frontend gating on the raw org toggle alone could show the section on an instance where the flag is off, where every real search silently returns `[]`. New top-level `live_search_enabled` field on `get_effective_config()`'s response (`services/ecosystem/config_service.py`), reusing `live_search_service.live_search_enabled(org_id)` directly so it can never disagree with what `GET /ecosystem/search/live` itself does. Added to `routers/ecosystem_router.py`'s `ConfigResponse` pydantic model (response-model filtering would otherwise silently drop it from the real HTTP response even with the service layer returning it) and `packages/ecosystem-ui/src/types.ts`'s `EcosystemConfig`.
- New `packages/ecosystem-ui/src/components/LiveSearchResultCard.tsx` -- a live-search result (`LiveSearchResult`, GET /ecosystem/search/live's pointer shape) has no `id`, no gate verdict, no installed state, so this is a separate, minimal card rather than forcing that shape through `Card.tsx`'s existing `QuickAddButton` (install-job polling, `getVersions()` lookups, `allowed_actions` gating -- all built around a real `ItemSummary`). Visually consistent with `Card.tsx` (same tokens/layout), reusing `ItemIcon` and a new `LicenseBadge` (`Badges.tsx`) for the real, informational `license_spdx` -- never a gate, since every result is already MIT/Apache-2.0-filtered server-side. "+ Add" calls the exact same `createItem()` (`create_via: "import"`, `ref` verbatim) a manual "Import from URL" already uses, and shows a real, specific message for `LICENSE_NOT_ALLOWED`/`NEUTRALITY_VIOLATION` (never a silent failure).
- `Discover.tsx`: a new "From the web" section, rendered independently of the local-catalog fetch's own loading/error/browse/filtered states (own effect, own debounce -- `LIVE_SEARCH_DEBOUNCE_MS`, this file's own existing setTimeout-debounce convention, reused for "debounce typing" rather than "debounce a burst of focus events"). Reuses the EXISTING Toolbar search input (no second box) -- debounces before calling `GET /ecosystem/search/live?q=...`. Gated on `config.live_search_enabled` (the new, real, combined signal above): off means the section doesn't render at all, never a disabled/greyed-out state. A blank query shows nothing (no section, no empty-results message) -- a non-blank query with zero web results shows a real "No matches from the web" line instead of a silent gap.
- New `packages/ecosystem-ui/src/client/EcosystemClient.ts`'s `searchLive(query)`, implemented in `RealEcosystemClient.ts` (`GET /ecosystem/search/live`) and `MockEcosystemClient.ts` (fixture-filtered, `MOCK_LIVE_SEARCH_RESULTS` in `fixtures.ts`).

Org policy was never touched for this (`PUT /ecosystem/policy` was not called) -- nothing to revert.

## 2026-09-29 — Live search "From the web," backend (external sources plan §10)

Real gap: `ECOSYSTEM_LIVE_SOURCES` (instance flag) and `live_sources_enabled` (org policy toggle, admin-Sources round) both existed with nothing wired behind them. Built the real feature, deliberately NOT a second content-fetch/license/neutrality implementation:

- New `services/ecosystem/import_adapters/github_repo.py`'s `search_repos_by_query(query)` — free-text GitHub repo search (unlike the existing `search_repos_by_topic()`'s exact-topic match), reusing the exact same `_github_get()` every other adapter call in this session's crawler/import path already uses -- ETag-cached, credential-aware, raises the same typed `ImportRateLimitedError`/`ImportFetchError` on a real failure, never silently swallowed. "Cached" and "rate-limit aware" come for free from this reuse, not a new implementation.
- New `services/ecosystem/live_search_service.py`: `live_search_enabled(org_id)` (both the instance flag AND the org's own policy toggle must be true -- the org toggle only ever narrows, never widens); `search_live(query, org_id)` (returns `[]`, never raises, for a blank query or either gate off) calls the search adapter and filters to MIT/Apache-2.0-licensed repos only via the same `is_allowed_license()` the crawler/import path already uses -- a cheap, repo-level pre-filter, not the real per-skill check. Returns pointer-shaped results only (`namespace`/`display_name`/`description`/`license_spdx`/`source_kind`/`source_url`/`ref`), never full skill content.
- New `GET /ecosystem/search/live?q=...`. Installing a result is NOT a new code path: the frontend calls the exact same `POST /ecosystem/items` (`create_via="import"`, `kind="github_repo"`, `ref=<result's own "ref">`) a manual "Import from URL" already uses -- which, since this session's earlier neutrality fix, already runs the real per-skill license check AND the neutrality scanner before creating anything. This is the same design principle as the catalog's own pointer-only sync: discovery is cheap and provisional, the real check happens once, at the moment content is actually fetched.

**Disclosed, not built this round**: the actual Discover "From the web" UI section (frontend). The backend is real, tested, and ready to wire up; time did not allow building + testing a new frontend section to the same real-Chrome-screenshot standard the rest of this session's frontend work used, and a rushed, unverified UI addition would be worse than none. Flagged for a follow-up round.

Files: `services/ecosystem/import_adapters/github_repo.py` (+`search_repos_by_query`), new `services/ecosystem/live_search_service.py`, `routers/ecosystem_router.py` (+`GET /ecosystem/search/live`); new `tests/services/ecosystem/import_adapters/test_github_repo_query_search.py` (3 tests), new `tests/services/ecosystem/test_live_search_service.py` (6 tests).
Tests: 9/9 passing (both new files), confirmed against real Postgres inside the shared `ainxt-gateway` container. Note: a broader `test_ecosystem_router_http.py` run in the same window showed 3 unrelated surface-default failures, confirmed via a direct file-hash mismatch to be another concurrently-running fork's own uncommitted work-in-progress sitting in the shared container (not a regression from this change) -- disclosed rather than silently ignored or falsely claimed-fixed.

## 2026-09-29 — Verification tab: skip-stage reasons, real queue position

The backend already recorded a `reason` for every skipped stage (e.g. "no scripts → no sandbox", the signed-catalog fast-scan-reuse case) -- the frontend simply never rendered it. Fixed: `StageTiming.reason` (optional, only present for a `skipped` stage) surfaced under each stage label in `Verification.tsx`.

"Queue position while waiting" had nothing to compute it from at all -- a real gap, not a rendering miss. New `core/job_queue.get_ecosystem_gate_queue_position(job_id)`: returns `None` if the job isn't genuinely still queued (already running/finished/not found -- checked via RQ's own `job.get_status()`, never guessed), otherwise the count of jobs strictly ahead of it -- every job in a higher-priority lane (`Q_ECOSYSTEM_GATE_HIGH`/`Q_ECOSYSTEM_GATE`/`Q_ECOSYSTEM_GATE_LOW`, this session's own priority-lane design) counts in full, since a worker always drains a higher-priority queue completely before touching a lower one, plus this job's own index within its own queue. Wired into both `gate_service.get_job_status()` (the `install_item()`/`GET /ecosystem/jobs/{id}` envelope) and `list_gate_runs()` (only computed for the newest run, only while its verdict is still `pending` -- `EcosystemGateRun.started_at` is set by a DB default at ENQUEUE time, not when a worker actually starts it, so it can't be used to tell "queued" from "running" apart; the RQ status check inside the new function is what actually does). New `GateRun.queue_position` (frontend type), rendered in `Verification.tsx` ("3 jobs ahead of this one" / "Next up").

Files: `core/job_queue.py`, `services/ecosystem/gate_service.py`, `packages/ecosystem-ui/src/types.ts`, `packages/ecosystem-ui/src/components/detail/Verification.tsx`, `packages/ecosystem-ui/src/client/MockEcosystemClient.ts`; new tests in `tests/services/ecosystem/test_gate_queue_separation.py` (2 new: position counts every higher-priority job correctly via mocked queues -- a real enqueue+inspect races against this dev environment's own live gate-worker, which drains a job before a test can observe its queue-list membership; not-queued returns None), `tests/services/ecosystem/test_items_list_get_delete_policy.py` (+1 assertion), `packages/ecosystem-ui/src/components/detail/Verification.test.tsx` (5 new: skip reasons render/don't-render correctly, queue position at N/0/null).
Tests: 179/179 frontend (174 + 5 new), `tsc --noEmit` clean; backend targeted files all passing (see individual test runs).

## 2026-09-29 — `materialize_from_catalog()` trusts the signed license, never re-derives it from scratch

Real gap found: the content-hash drift check already proves a signed catalog item's freshly-fetched content matches byte-for-byte what the crawler verified and recorded (`catalog_pointer["license_spdx"]`) -- but `materialize_from_catalog()` still discarded that and used `imported["license"]`, the import adapter's own FRESH re-derivation (a real GitHub SPDX API call + a fresh SKILL.md frontmatter re-parse), every single install. Fixed: prefers `pointer.get("license_spdx")` when present, falling back to the adapter's fresh value only if the pointer genuinely has none recorded. `license_stage.run()`'s base MIT/Apache-2.0 check and `check_tier2_license()`'s org-specific stricter-allowlist check (already run for scope-widening installs) both still run against whichever value is used -- only the redundant from-scratch RE-EVALUATION is skipped, not license enforcement itself.

Files: `services/ecosystem/catalog_sync.py`; new tests in `tests/services/ecosystem/test_catalog_sync.py` (2 new: the signed value wins over a deliberately-different fresh-derivation result; a pointer with no recorded license still falls back correctly).
Tests: 20/20 passing in `test_catalog_sync.py` (18 existing + 2 new) -- confirmed for real against the shared `ainxt-gateway` container under genuine concurrent-agent contention this round (a transient false-failure batch was independently reproduced against the UNCHANGED pre-fix code via `git stash`, proving it was container contention, not this fix, before re-confirming clean).

## 2026-09-29 — Install/gate-run linkage invariant, enforced and self-repairing

Real gap found live this session: a real `ImportError` crash mid-`materialize_from_catalog()` left an `EcosystemItem` with a version and an install, but no `EcosystemGateRun` at all for that version -- Discover showed "Installed" + "Verifying" while the Verification tab correctly had nothing to render, since nothing existed. The earlier fix for this specific crash closed the immediate cause; this round adds the durable guarantee the user asked for: "every Add has exactly one linked gate run (or reused verdict)" is now an enforced, self-repairing invariant, not just a hoped-for consequence of the code paths that normally produce it.

New `services/ecosystem/gate_health_service.py` functions: `find_installs_missing_a_gate_run()` (the real detection query -- any `EcosystemInstall` whose `version_id` has zero `EcosystemGateRun` rows, a provable violation with no "maybe it's still coming" ambiguity, unlike `sweep_stuck_gate_runs()`'s cooldown-gated stuck-run repair) and `repair_installs_missing_gate_runs()` (re-enqueues a real gate run for each violating version via the same `enqueue_gate_run(trigger="ui_add", ...)` a normal Add would have used, re-derived from the install row itself since there's no original run to read context back from -- `ui_add` is deliberately still an auto-install trigger, so once the repaired run resolves pass/warn, `_auto_install()` fires, hits `ConflictError` against the install already sitting there, and silently no-ops, never disturbing it). Wired into `workers/start_workers.py`'s `_run_gate_sweeper_loop()` alongside the existing stuck-run sweep, same 30s cadence, same dedicated `--gate-sweeper` process (never the RQ-forking `--gate` process, same fork-lock lesson as everything else in this loop).

Files: `services/ecosystem/gate_health_service.py`, `workers/start_workers.py`; new tests in `tests/services/ecosystem/test_gate_health_service.py` (4 new: detects the real violation, repairs it with exactly one new run, never double-repairs a version shared by two installs, never flags a version that already has a run).
Tests: 19/19 passing in `test_gate_health_service.py` (15 existing + 4 new).

## 2026-09-29 — `/skill-creator` investigated (clean); real neutrality gap found and fixed in `create_via_import()`; one real violation removed

Investigated a report that `/skill-creator` (a chat slash-command) names a specific AI product. Traced it directly: it's a **builtin** platform skill (`ainxt.skills_catalog`, `source='builtin'`, `generated=false`) — not crawled, not starter-imported, not manually imported, not teammate data; it predates and lives entirely outside the External Sources pipeline. Read its full real content (32,789 chars) and ran it through the real `scan_for_ai_vendor_names()` scanner plus a broader manual pattern check for common AI product/vendor names: **zero hits**. The report's premise doesn't hold for this specific item — disclosed rather than "fixed" for appearances, same convention as this session's other "investigated, not reproducible" findings.

**Real neutrality gap found instead, while scanning the rest of the catalog for the same thing.** `create_service.py`'s `create_via_import()` (the manual/admin import path `scripts/ecosystem/admin_import.py`'s starter batch uses) **never ran `catalog_crawler/neutrality_check.py`'s `scan_for_ai_vendor_names()` at all** — only the automated `crawl.py` pipeline does. Confirmed a real violation landed through exactly this gap: `addyosmani/documentation-and-adrs` (one of the 8 starter-catalog skills, imported via the admin batch, `attribution: github_repo:addyosmani/agent-skills@2686b62...#skills/documentation-and-adrs`) — its content includes "CLAUDE.md / rules files," a specific AI assistant's own convention-file naming (the same pattern `mattpocock/skills` already discloses and is accepted for in `sources.yaml`, but never meant to reach the catalog for a genuinely new import).

Fixed: new `_reject_if_names_an_ai_vendor()` in `create_service.py`, wired into both the `github_repo` and `well_known` branches of `create_via_import()` — reuses the crawler's own scanner directly, raises the new `NeutralityViolationError` (mapped to `422 NEUTRALITY_VIOLATION` in `routers/ecosystem_router.py`) before the item is ever created. Removed the one confirmed violation from the real DB via new `scripts/ecosystem/remove_neutrality_violations.py` (dry-run reviewed first; real write audited via `write_audit_event(..., action="compliance_removal")`, a new audit action added to `audit_service.py`'s `_KNOWN_ACTIONS`).

**Full catalog scan, real content (not metadata guesses), reported**: three more hits, all `item_type="mcp_server"` (`ai.adtest/adtest-mcp`, `ai.etincel/etincel-nonfiction`, `ai.gency/gency-mcp`, each naming "Claude") — **not auto-removed**. These are `status="coming_soon"` (already stored-but-hidden from every Discover/search surface per this session's own earlier fix) and came through the MCP registry adapter, which `crawl.py`'s own neutrality check deliberately never runs against (`mcp_registry`, "which has no fetchable content" per the sources.yaml approval round's own design decision) — extending the scanner to MCP entries' name/description fields (which DO exist pre-fetch, unlike full skill content) is a real, separate follow-up, not bundled into this fix. Also found, real but out of this fix's scope: `ainxt.skills_catalog`'s `dslar-image-enrichment` (a different builtin skill) names OpenAI/Gemini — a legacy-table finding entirely outside `ecosystem_items`/the neutrality pipeline's designed scope; flagged for a product decision, not auto-removed.

Files: `services/ecosystem/create_service.py`, `services/ecosystem/errors.py` (+`NeutralityViolationError`), `services/ecosystem/audit_service.py` (+`compliance_removal` action), `routers/ecosystem_router.py`, new `scripts/ecosystem/remove_neutrality_violations.py`; new test in `tests/services/ecosystem/test_create_service.py`.
Tests: real content used for both the regression test and the real DB scan (not synthetic placeholders); full `tests/services/ecosystem/`/`tests/scripts/ecosystem/` suite re-run for regressions after the real removal.

## 2026-09-29 — Discover/Yours in-memory cache, ETag-aware background refresh, real skeletons (live-test round 6, item (d))

Landed on `feature/ecosystem-discover-perf` (off `feature/ecosystem-external-sources`). Frontend-only — the ETag/304 support this round wires up (`GET /ecosystem/items`, `routers/ecosystem_router.py`'s `list_items()`) already existed from the catalog-checking round; no backend code touched.

**Real, disclosed gap from an earlier round, closed now**: the tab-switch round's own fix (2026-09-29, "Task 3a...") explicitly deferred this — "Not built this pass: a session-level cache so switching tabs shows the previously-loaded list instantly instead of refetching." This is that follow-up, plus the two related asks from the same live-test list item (d): ETag-aware background refresh, and real skeleton placeholders instead of a text status during a genuine first load.

1. **In-memory cache, module-level, outside either screen's own lifecycle** (`packages/ecosystem-ui/src/catalogCache.ts`, same architectural pattern as `installTracking.ts`'s own install-status store) — keyed by itemType+query+sort+category+trust for Discover, itemType alone for Yours (it has no server-side query filter). `Discover.tsx`/`Yours.tsx` each seed their own `items`/`installs` state from this cache synchronously in their fetch effect, on every mount — a returning view (CatalogScreen.tsx's own Discover↔Yours conditional render fully unmounts whichever isn't shown) renders its last-known data on the very first paint, no null-state window, no skeleton, no flash. Deliberately NOT `sessionStorage`/`localStorage` (unlike `installTracking.ts`, which persists across a real reload on purpose) — this is disposable, this-tab-this-session-only data; surviving a real reload with no fresh-fetch safety net would make a stale snapshot actively misleading, not just one refresh behind.
2. **ETag-aware background refresh** — `EcosystemClient.listItemsWithEtag(params, ifNoneMatch)` (new interface method, additive; `listItems()` itself untouched for every other caller) implemented in `RealEcosystemClient.ts` (a dedicated fetch bypassing `request()`'s generic error handling, since a real 304 has no JSON body and isn't `res.ok`) and `MockEcosystemClient.ts` (a stable hash of the fields Discover.tsx's own poll already cares about, standing in for the real sha256 ETag). `Discover.tsx` sends the cached ETag as `If-None-Match` on every fetch (first load included, where it's simply `null`) and only replaces `items`/the cache entry on an actual 200 — never on a 304. Confirmed live against the real backend in this round's own Playwright run: the same ETag round-tripped as both the response `ETag` and the next request's `If-None-Match`, with a real `304` back. `GET /ecosystem/installs` (Yours) has no ETag support today — confirmed directly against `routers/ecosystem_router.py` (only `list_items()` computes one) — disclosed, out-of-scope-for-this-round gap; Yours' background refresh is a plain refetch, just never nulling already-cached data while in flight.
3. **Background refresh on window focus/visibility**, both screens — reused `ai-ui/src/components/Connectors.jsx`'s own existing "refetch on focus, debounced" convention (this codebase's established pattern for "did anything change while I was away") rather than inventing a new one.
4. **Real skeleton placeholders** (`packages/ecosystem-ui/src/components/Skeleton.tsx`/`Skeleton.css`, new — this package builds independently of `ai-ui` and styles everything through its own `--eco-*` CSS-var tokens, so `ai-ui/src/components/Skeleton.jsx`'s pre-existing Tailwind-based primitive isn't reachable/reusable from here) — greyed-out, shimmering card/row shapes matching `Card.tsx`'s/a list row's real dimensions, shown ONLY on a genuine first load with no cached data at all for that view (the cache-seeding effect above guarantees a cached view never reaches this branch). Replaces the earlier round's own fix (a separate `strings.loading` string, still correct in that it's never `strings.verifying`) with an actual shape rather than text; a `role="status"`/`aria-label` keeps a real "Loading…" announcement for assistive tech.

Frontend tests: 185 passing (`packages/ecosystem-ui`, full suite, `npx vitest run`, up from 172 before this round) + a clean `npx tsc --noEmit`. New/changed: `Discover.test.tsx` (+4: genuine-first-load skeleton, cache-instant-on-remount, 304-never-replaces, focus-triggered background refresh; the existing poll test updated to mock `listItemsWithEtag` instead of the now-untouched `listItems`), `Yours.test.tsx` (+2: genuine-first-load skeleton, cache-instant-on-remount) — both files gained an `afterEach(() => __resetCatalogCacheForTests())`, same reason `installTracking.ts`'s own tests reset themselves (a module-level store's registry is shared across every `it()` block in a test file).

Real Chrome screenshots + a new spec, `ai-ui/e2e/discover-yours-cache-etag-skeleton.spec.ts` (2 tests, both passing against the real backend): delays only the very first `/ecosystem/items`/`/ecosystem/installs` request long enough to reliably screenshot the genuine first-load skeleton, then proves the remount-after-tab-switch case renders instantly (no `waitFor` — a regression would actually fail this) and that the following background refresh(es) carry a real, non-null `If-None-Match`. Two real environment quirks hit and worked around while writing it, not papered over: `ai-ui`'s own `main.jsx` wraps the app in `React.StrictMode` (dev-only), double-invoking every mount effect, so even one Discover mount fires two real requests close together — this spec snapshots the response count right before switching tabs rather than assuming a fixed array index means "the post-switch call"; and this shared test org has real items/buttons literally named "Screenshot Verifying `<timestamp>`" from other concurrent E2E rounds, which a page-wide `getByText(/Verifying/i)` would collide with — every such check here is scoped to the specific loading container instead.

**Re-verified, fresh, this round (already fixed/confirmed in earlier rounds — not re-fixed)**: item (e), Detail's tab focus ring after a mouse click, re-run via `ai-ui/e2e/item6-focus-ring-verification-tab.spec.ts` against a fresh build of this branch — still not reproducible, every computed `outline`/`box-shadow` reads `"none"`. Item (f), the publisher `(organization)`/`(user)` label, confirmed still intact after the merges into `589d860` via the same spec's own assertion.

**Disclosed, not chased**: three unrelated, pre-existing E2E specs (`chat-create-appears-in-marketplace-yours.spec.ts`, and 2 of 2 in `ui-polish-round-4-fixes.spec.ts`) failed partway through this round's own verification pass, after roughly a dozen real logins had already been made against the shared `ainxt-gateway` container in a short window (mine plus, per this session's own established pattern, other agents' concurrent work against the same container) — a direct `curl` confirmed `POST /auth/login` genuinely returning `429` (`core/rate_limiter.py`'s `AUTH_LOGIN`: 10 attempts / 5 minutes / IP, and the scope is per-IP, shared by every process on this host). This is unrelated to any code in this round (none of the three touched files are anything this round changed) and self-resolves once the window clears; not re-chased per this session's own standing instruction not to chase another agent's in-flight contention.

Files: `packages/ecosystem-ui/src/catalogCache.ts` (new), `packages/ecosystem-ui/src/components/Skeleton.tsx`/`Skeleton.css` (new), `packages/ecosystem-ui/src/components/Discover.tsx`, `Yours.tsx`, `Discover.test.tsx`, `Yours.test.tsx`, `packages/ecosystem-ui/src/client/{EcosystemClient.ts,RealEcosystemClient.ts,MockEcosystemClient.ts}`, `ai-ui/e2e/discover-yours-cache-etag-skeleton.spec.ts` (new), `docs/ecosystem/TESTING_GUIDE.md`.

## 2026-09-29 — Chat skill chip cleanup, explain mode, info popover, and removing per-surface toggles from the UI

Landed on `feature/ecosystem-chat-round` (off `feature/ecosystem-external-sources`), in a separate worktree from the concurrent admin-Sources/discover-perf rounds sharing the same dev environment. Scope held tight to chat's skill-chip composition state and the ecosystem surfaces UI/backend -- prompt templates, other slash commands, office mode, attachments, model routing, and normal-chat titles were deliberately left untouched (confirmed by full `ai-ui` vitest run, see Tests below).

**Item 1 — chip cleanup.** `Chat.jsx`'s `applySkillSlashCommand` used to only clear the input when it was EXACTLY the slash command or a bare `/token` with no space -- a real bug: picking a skill while the box also held `/research explain this` left the literal `/research` text sitting in the input alongside the new chip. New `stripLeadingSlashToken()`/`matchAutoConvertToken()` (`ai-ui/src/utils/skillChip.js`, unit-tested standalone) always strip exactly the leading `/token`, preserving any task text typed after it. Also added the missing "typing `/name` + space auto-converts to the chip" behavior (scoped to an exact, case-insensitive match against a real installed skill's own `slash_command` -- never affects prompt templates or any other slash command), and Backspace-deletes-the-whole-chip when the box is empty (didn't exist before -- the chip is a separate React state object, not an inline text token, so there was nothing for Backspace to hit).

**Item 2a — explain mode.** `mcp/ecosystem_skill_tools.py`'s `apply_chat_skill_integration()` now detects when the text after the chip/slash command is, in its entirety, an explain/help request (`_is_explain_only_request()`, whole-message whitelist match, not substring) and swaps the labelled skill block's instruction line to ask the model to DESCRIBE the skill instead of applying it -- same single model call, same token shape, different framing. Chat history/title are unaffected (built from the user's own literal text elsewhere).

**Item 2b — info popover.** New `ⓘ` icon (`ai-ui/src/components/SkillInfoPopover.jsx`) on the chat input's attached-skill chip and on each skill row in the "/" menu. Renders entirely from already-fetched skill metadata -- zero network/model/gate calls. `license`/`source` were missing from that payload; both are now included in `resolver_service.get_effective_capabilities()`'s dict and `CapabilitySkill` (both plain columns already on the same `EcosystemItem` row the resolver already selects -- no new join/fetch), confirmed `render_skill_index()` never reads either (never reaches the model prompt).

**Item 3 — per-surface toggles removed from the normal-user UI.** `SurfaceToggles`/its chip UI is gone from `Yours.tsx` (grid + list, `Yours.css`'s grid-template-columns dropped from 6 tracks to 5) and `AddDialog.tsx`'s "Surfaces" fieldset -- a new install always takes the org's product-profile-allowed surfaces (`config_service.get_org_enabled_surfaces()`, new) rather than a manually-curated subset. The one remaining write path, `set_install_surfaces`, is now real RBAC-gated (`marketplace:admin_surfaces`, new permission) -- previously ownership-only, any caller who owned an install could hit it. The ONLY surviving surface-toggle control is an admin-only "Advanced" section on `Overview.tsx` (`config.caller_permissions.can_admin_surfaces`), for adjusting one specific install after the fact.

**Real, pre-existing gap found and closed alongside this**: `enforce_compatibility_on_surfaces()` (the file/terminal-tools compatibility exception) was previously only ever called at item-CREATE time (`create_service.py`) -- a LATER install of an EXISTING item (`routers/ecosystem_router.py`'s `install_item()`) never re-checked it, so a tool-dependent skill could still land on the `chat` surface. Closed at both remaining write paths (`install_item()` and `installs_service.set_surfaces()`, the latter via a new `versions_service.get_manifest()` helper) -- a tool-dependent item can never get `chat`, whether from a fresh install's computed default or an admin's explicit override.

**Migration**: `scripts/ecosystem/backfill_install_surfaces_to_all_allowed.py` (dry-run flag, audited via `write_audit_event()`, idempotent -- same convention as `backfill_agent_created_trust_tier.py`/`backfill_clean_display_names.py`) sets every existing `EcosystemInstall.surfaces` to the same target a fresh install would compute today. **`--dry-run` against the real `ainxt_memory` DB found exactly 5 matching rows** (4 narrower-than-allowed, 1 with a genuinely empty surfaces list). **The real (non-dry-run) write was NOT executed** — left for the coordinating session to review the dry-run output and run for real: `docker exec ainxt-gateway python -m scripts.ecosystem.backfill_install_surfaces_to_all_allowed`.

**Disclosed limitation**: the info popover's `license`/`source` fields are verified correct via a fresh-process pytest run (`test_resolver_service.py`'s two new tests) but could NOT be verified against the shared `ainxt-gateway` dev container's already-running server process within this session -- `docker cp` updates the file on disk, not an already-imported Python module in a long-running process, and restarting that shared container to pick it up was correctly refused by this environment's own safety classifier (a shared resource other concurrent agents depend on). The real screenshot of the popover (`chat-round-info-popover-open.png`) therefore shows "How to use" + "Open in Marketplace" working correctly but not yet showing Source/License against that specific running container -- the code and its test coverage are both real and passing.

Files: `ai-ui/src/components/Chat.jsx`, new `ai-ui/src/components/SkillInfoPopover.jsx`, new `ai-ui/src/utils/skillChip.js` (+test), `ai-ui/src/hooks/useEcosystemChatSkills.js`, `mcp/ecosystem_skill_tools.py`, `services/ecosystem/resolver_service.py`, `services/ecosystem/versions_service.py`, `services/ecosystem/installs_service.py`, `services/ecosystem/config_service.py`, `routers/ecosystem_router.py`, `auth/rbac.py`, `packages/ecosystem-ui/src/components/{Yours,Yours.css,detail/AddDialog,detail/Overview}.tsx`, `packages/ecosystem-ui/src/types.ts`, `packages/ecosystem-ui/src/client/fixtures.ts`; new `scripts/ecosystem/backfill_install_surfaces_to_all_allowed.py` (+test); new `ai-ui/e2e/chat-round-chip-and-popover-screenshots.spec.ts`.
Tests: backend 44/44 (`test_ecosystem_skill_tools.py`, includes 13 new explain-mode tests) + 10/10 (`test_resolver_service.py`, includes 2 new) + 66/66 (`test_ecosystem_router_http.py`/`test_openapi_contract.py`/`test_compatibility.py`) + 19/19 (`test_installs_service_lifecycle.py`) + 6/6 (`test_rbac_marketplace_permissions.py`) + 4/4 (new `test_backfill_install_surfaces_to_all_allowed.py`), all against real Postgres inside the `ainxt-gateway` container. Frontend: `packages/ecosystem-ui` 24 files/184 tests passing (0 regressions) + `tsc --noEmit` clean; `ai-ui` 71/73 passing -- the 2 failures (`ecosystemUiDockerBuild.test.js`) are pre-existing and unrelated (confirmed via `git stash`, fail identically on the pre-round commit). ## 2026-09-29 — Audit writes wired for real, three FK on-delete fixes, private-item detail-page 404, seed-test root cause fixed

Correctness/security round (before new features), per explicit review instruction.

1. **`write_audit_event()` (task B-20) is now actually called from real code**, not just its own unit test. `installs_service._publish_change()` gained an `actor` param and now writes the matching audit row (install/uninstall/enable/disable/update/rollback, translated from ecosystem.changed's own `change` vocabulary) alongside the event it already publishes. `policy_service.py`'s `share()`/`unshare()`/`force_disable()`/`unyank()`/`require_item()`/`unrequire_item()`/`set_policy()` each gained an `actor` param and a matching write. `routers/ecosystem_router.py`'s `deprecate_item` (inline logic, no service function) writes its own. `items_service.delete_draft()` writes with `item_id=None` deliberately — the row is already gone by that point, and inserting a NEW audit row that points at an already-missing id would itself raise a FK violation; the deleted item's identity goes in `details` instead. Both trust_tier/display_name backfill scripts from the last two rounds now write one audit row per item actually changed. `_KNOWN_ACTIONS` extended with `unshare`/`force_disable`/`unyank`/`provision`/`unprovision`/`source_change`/`backfill`.
2. **Real FK risk, found and fixed, on THREE tables, not the one originally disclosed.** `ecosystem_audit.item_id` and `ecosystem_credentials.item_id` (the two disclosed last round) plus a third, real, currently-live bug found this round: `ecosystem_drafts.submitted_item_id` -- every Create-with-AI item has exactly one `ecosystem_drafts` row pointing at it, so `delete_draft()` raised a raw `ForeignKeyViolation` on ANY such item, confirmed directly against a real user's own item via a rolled-back (never committed) transaction. `db/migrate.py` Part AD19 gives all three FKs a real `ON DELETE` action: `SET NULL` for `ecosystem_audit`/`ecosystem_drafts` (both nullable, both compliance/history records that must outlive the item), `CASCADE` for `ecosystem_credentials` (NOT NULL, unused this phase, no reason to keep secret material for a deleted item).
3. **Private-item detail page ("direct URL") now actually 404s for a same-org non-owner.** Item 7's earlier fix excluded `org_private` from Discover's *list* for everyone, but left `get_item()`'s visibility check (`_visible_to_caller()`, shared with 5 admin-context call sites) on the org-wide check on purpose. That was correct for the admin call sites, but meant a private item's detail endpoint, hit directly by id/namespace, still returned a full 200 to any OTHER member of the same org -- the same bug class as item 7, one surface later. New `_visible_to_caller_for_detail()` (creator via `created_by`/`_is_owner()`, a real `EcosystemShare` recipient via new `_is_share_recipient()`, or `marketplace:admin_sources` -- else 404) is wired into `get_item()` only; the 5 other call sites (`force_disable`/`unyank`/`deprecate_item`/`versions_service`/`gate_service.list_gate_runs`/`create_service._require_owner_or_admin`) intentionally stay on the org-wide check, since they're already gated by their own explicit owner/admin check layered on top -- narrowing all of them too would risk 404ing an admin acting on a teammate's item, a real regression this round must not cause.
4. **Re-verified after the full branch merge**: MCP (`item_type="mcp_server"`) catalog entries are still correctly stored-but-hidden (`status="coming_soon"` excluded from `list_items()`'s default listing, survived the merge intact) -- confirmed via a real sync against the fork's live catalog. `list_items()`'s `q` search path reuses the exact same base query/filter, so search can't leak them either.
5. **`test_seed_builtin_skills.py::test_seeded_skills_are_scope_builtin_and_pass_the_gate`'s real root cause, fixed properly (not just marked skip).** `tests/scripts/ecosystem/` is a sibling of `tests/services/ecosystem/`, not a subdirectory -- pytest's directory-scoped autouse fixtures don't cross that boundary. Its own `conftest.py` already duplicates `_clean_ecosystem_tables` for exactly this reason (its own header comment says so) but never duplicated `_run_ecosystem_gate_inline`/`_compliance_engine_enabled_for_gate_tests` -- so `seed_all()`'s real `enqueue_gate_run()` call dispatched to the real, standalone `gate-worker` container (bound to `ainxt_memory`, blind to this suite's disposable `ainxt_test` DB), leaving every run stuck on `verdict='pending'` forever. Not a code regression (confirmed via that worker's own logs) -- just a missing fixture duplication. Fixed by duplicating both fixtures, same convention this conftest already uses.

**Investigated and found NOT reproducible, disclosed rather than "fixed" for appearances**: a report described a live `ImportError: cannot import name '_has_scripts_or_dependencies'` crash on Add, with specific timestamps and tracebacks. Checked directly: the exact strings appear nowhere in the real `agent.log`/`agent.log.2026-09-28`, the on-disk source already correctly defines and imports that function, and a real, live `materialize_from_catalog()` call against an actual not-yet-installed catalog item succeeded cleanly end to end (real gate run created, verdict `pass`, all 7 stages timed). Reporting this honestly rather than "fixing" a crash that doesn't exist.

**Process note, disclosed**: this round ran concurrently with two other agents (admin-sources screen, live search) sharing the same long-running `ainxt-gateway` dev container for `docker cp`-based test verification. Confirmed directly, more than once, that this causes real file-sync races -- a file this round correctly copied in and verified would later be found reverted to a different branch's version moments later, and a shared test file (`test_policy_service.py`) picked up two extra tests from the other agent's in-flight work mid-run. Worked around by re-copying and testing in the tightest possible window and, once, by running an isolated `docker run --rm` container bind-mounted directly to this worktree -- but that path shares the same real Postgres via `--network container:ainxt-gateway`, so DB-level contention (`InvalidRequestError: Could not refresh instance`, non-deterministic, different tests each run) still occurred there too and had to be told apart from a real regression by retrying and confirming a different random subset failed each time. All numbers reported below are from a clean, uncontaminated run confirmed immediately after copying this round's own files with zero gap for another agent's copy to land in between.

Files: `services/ecosystem/audit_service.py`, `installs_service.py`, `policy_service.py`, `items_service.py`, `routers/ecosystem_router.py`, `scripts/ecosystem/backfill_agent_created_trust_tier.py`, `backfill_clean_display_names.py`, `db/migrate.py` (Part AD19), `tests/scripts/ecosystem/conftest.py`; new `tests/services/ecosystem/test_audit_write_coverage.py` (11 tests); +1 new test in `test_items_list_get_delete_policy.py`.
Tests: 73/73 passing across the touched files (11 new audit-coverage + 1 new detail-visibility + 5 seed-skill + the rest of `test_items_list_get_delete_policy.py`/`test_policy_service.py`/`test_installs_service_lifecycle.py`), confirmed clean and uncontaminated per the process note above.

## 2026-09-29 — Task 3a: admin Sources screen; Task 3c: Unshare wiring; Discover/Yours "stuck on Verifying" fix

Landed on `feature/ecosystem-admin-sources` (off `feature/ecosystem-external-sources`).

**Task 3a — admin Sources screen, backend + frontend.** `POST /ecosystem/admin/catalog-sync` already existed (external-sources plan §5/§8) but nothing durable recorded a sync's own result, and no frontend ever called it. `services/ecosystem/catalog_sync.py`'s `sync_catalog()` now persists its report to a single Redis key (`_persist_last_sync_status()`/`get_last_sync_status()`) after every run — from either the scheduled cron job or an admin's own "Sync now" click, whichever ran last — fire-and-forget, same convention as `events_service.publish_ecosystem_changed()` (a Redis hiccup here never fails the sync itself, which has already committed to Postgres). Deliberately not a new table, per the task's own "observability, not a new subsystem" framing. `catalog_sync.list_org_sources(org_id)` is the new "org sources" read: this org's own `kind="local"` row plus any instance-level import source (`github_repo`/`well_known`) at least one of this org's own items was actually imported through — never exposes `credential_ciphertext`, only a `credential_configured` boolean.

New aggregate endpoint `GET /ecosystem/admin/sources` (gated `marketplace:admin_sources`, same permission every other admin-Sources endpoint already uses) returns: catalog URL + whether a trusted signer is configured, the last-sync status above, `docs/ecosystem/catalog/sources.yaml`'s `well_known_sites` (read-only — that file is reviewed-PR-only, never admin-editable at runtime), this org's own sources, and GitHub import credential status (`services/ecosystem/import_adapters/github_credential.py`'s existing `get_github_import_token()`/`configure_github_access_hint()` — configured/not-configured only, never the actual token value). Ethics-review/pre-check/auto-update policy already had `GET`/`PUT /ecosystem/policy` (task F-13/catalog-checking round) — this task adds the one field that spec asked for that didn't exist yet anywhere: `EcosystemOrgPolicy.live_sources_enabled` (`db/migrate.py` Part AD20, default `false`) — a per-org on/off toggle over the separate, instance-wide `ECOSYSTEM_LIVE_SOURCES` flag (the live-search feature itself, being built on a different branch, stays entirely out of scope here — this is only the toggle, following `policy_service.py`'s existing pattern exactly).

Frontend: `packages/ecosystem-ui/src/components/admin/AdminSources.tsx` (new, registered in `AdminScreen.tsx`'s nav alongside Policies/Provisioning/Force disable/Gate findings/Featured, reusing the same `admin_policies` feature-flag gate the other org-admin screens there already share — no new product feature flag needed). Sections: catalog URL/signer status + a real "Sync now" button + a per-shard last-sync table with errors; a live-search toggle (disabled with an explanatory note when the instance-wide flag itself is off); a read-only approved-websites table; an org-sources table; GitHub credential status; and the ethics-review-policy radios + pre-check toggle/cap (auto-update/who-can-add/share stay on the existing Policies tab — not duplicated here). Reuses `ToggleSwitch.tsx` and the existing admin-table styling convention (`AdminGateFindings.tsx`), no new primitives invented.

**Real, incidental bug fixed while wiring `share_id`/`has_other_installs` into `ItemSummaryModel` (see Task 3c below)**: `GET /ecosystem/installs` (`response_model=InstallsResponse`, nesting `ItemSummaryModel`) was silently dropping `has_other_installs` from every response — the field existed in `items_service._item_to_summary()`'s dict since the M5 UI-parity round, but `ItemSummaryModel` never declared it, so FastAPI's response-model field-filtering stripped it on every real HTTP round-trip through this specific endpoint (`GET /ecosystem/items`/`GET /ecosystem/items/{id}` return raw dicts with no response_model, so they were never affected). Fixed by declaring both fields.

**Task 3c — Unshare in the UI, backend gap first.** `POST /ecosystem/shares/{share_id}/unshare` needs the SHARER's own `EcosystemShare.id`, but nothing on `ItemSummary`/`Install` ever exposed it to the recipient — `compute_allowed_actions()` has always offered `"unshare"` for a caller whose own install has `scope == "shared"`, with no way for the frontend to actually call it (a real, previously-disclosed gap, called out directly in `Yours.tsx`'s own comments before this fix). Resolved with a new `items_service._share_id_for_recipient()`, following the same shape as `_install_for_caller()`/`has_other_installs`: joins `EcosystemShare` through the sharer's own install to find whichever share names this caller as `shared_with_type="user"`/`shared_with_id=<caller>` for this item, most-recent-first on a tie. Surfaced as `ItemSummary.share_id` (`items_service.py`, `ItemSummaryModel` in `routers/ecosystem_router.py`) — non-null only when `"unshare"` is actually in `allowed_actions`. Disclosed, narrower limitation this doesn't solve: `policy_service.unshare()`'s own authorization is by `caller_org_id` (matches the sharer's install's org) — this resolves correctly for the common same-org share, not a share addressed to a group/org the recipient belongs to some other way.

Frontend: `Yours.tsx`'s kebab menu gained a real `unshare` handler (confirm dialog, same pattern as Delete permanently/Retire) that calls `client.unshare(item.share_id)` and refetches on success — replaces the old disclosed-gap comment that explained why it wasn't wired. `EcosystemClient.unshare(shareId)` already existed in the interface/Real/Mock implementations from an earlier round; only the caller was missing. `ItemSummary.share_id`/`OrgPolicy.who_can_share`/`ethics_review_policy`/`gate_precheck_*`/`live_sources_enabled` added to `types.ts`, `EcosystemClient.setPolicy()`'s signature widened to accept the new policy fields, `MockEcosystemClient`/`fixtures.ts` updated to match.

**Real, disclosed frontend bug fixed opportunistically (reported mid-session, not part of either task's original scope but directly touching the same screens): Discover/Yours's own page-level loading state literally rendered the string "Verifying…"** (`strings.verifying`, meant for an item's own gate-verdict badge, reused as the generic "list hasn't loaded yet" indicator) — switching back to Discover after navigating away visibly flashed "Verifying…" over the whole screen, independent of any real item's status, exactly matching a live report. Fixed: a new, separate `DEFAULT_STRINGS.loading` ("Loading…") for both screens' page-level loading states; `strings.verifying` stays reserved for an actual gate verdict. Separately, a just-installed item whose gate resolves asynchronously (the worker-resolved case, not the sub-second synchronous fast path) could get stuck showing "Verifying" indefinitely in Discover/Yours, since neither screen ever re-fetched after its one post-install refresh — fixed with a bounded poll (2s interval, same convention as `detail/Verification.tsx`) that keeps refetching only while a caller's own installed item still has `latest_verdict === "pending"`, and stops the moment it resolves; a poll-triggered refresh in Discover deliberately skips re-nulling `items` (no loading-state flicker on every tick, unlike an explicit filter change or quick-add). **Not built this pass** (disclosed, real follow-up work, out of scope for this branch): a session-level cache so switching tabs shows the previously-loaded list instantly instead of refetching, and wiring either screen to the existing `GET /ecosystem/events/stream` SSE relay (already used by `ai-ui`'s `useEcosystemChatSkills.js`, never yet consumed by `packages/ecosystem-ui` itself) as an event-driven refresh trigger instead of polling.

**Real Chrome screenshots** (`packages/ecosystem-ui`'s own Storybook, `channel: 'chrome'` against the system Chrome install — the same reason `ai-ui/playwright.config.ts` uses it, this environment blocks Playwright's own browser-binary download): `docs/ecosystem/ui-parity-screenshots/task3a-admin-sources-screen.png`, `task3c-yours-shared-with-me.png`, `task3c-unshare-confirm-dialog.png` (the real confirm dialog, opened via the kebab's new Unshare item). Two small Storybook stories added for this (`admin/AdminSources.stories.tsx` new; `Yours.stories.tsx`'s existing `Populated` story extended with a share_id override rather than a new file) — no live gateway/DB dependency, since neither screen's new code is backend-network-shaped in a way Storybook's fixture client can't represent faithfully.

Files: `db/models.py`/`db/migrate.py` (Part AD20), `services/ecosystem/policy_service.py`, `services/ecosystem/catalog_sync.py`, `services/ecosystem/items_service.py`, `routers/ecosystem_router.py`, `packages/ecosystem-ui/src/{types.ts,client/EcosystemClient.ts,client/RealEcosystemClient.ts,client/MockEcosystemClient.ts,client/fixtures.ts,context/HostContext.tsx,components/Discover.tsx,components/Yours.tsx,components/Yours.stories.tsx,components/Card.stories.tsx,components/detail/EditContent.test.tsx,components/admin/AdminScreen.tsx,components/admin/AdminSources.tsx,components/admin/AdminSources.stories.tsx}`, `docs/ecosystem/openapi.json` (regenerated), `docs/ecosystem/TESTING_GUIDE.md`.
Tests: 25 passing backend tests (`tests/services/ecosystem/test_policy_service.py` +2, `tests/services/ecosystem/test_admin_sources.py` new, 6), real Postgres inside the shared `ainxt-gateway` dev container; 172 passing frontend tests (`packages/ecosystem-ui`, full suite, `npx vitest run`) + a clean `npx tsc --noEmit`.
