## 2026-09-28 — Catalog checking, part 2: instructions-only synchronous fast path, pre-check dispatcher, real load test

Follow-up round closing three gaps disclosed at the end of the first catalog-checking round.

**Real bug found and fixed first**: the first round's "skip static_safety when the signed catalog hash matches" check compared `EcosystemItemVersion.content_hash` (`versions_service.create_version_for_content()`'s `content_hash()`, a sha256 of the ENCODED ENVELOPE bytes) against `EcosystemItem.catalog_pointer["content_hash"]` (`catalog_sync.compute_content_hash()`, a sha256 of manifest_text+files separately) — two different hash functions over two different representations of the same content, which can never be equal even for a genuinely verified item. Caught by the new synchronous-fast-path test actually invoking the (real, network-dependent) ethics stage when it should have been skipped. Fixed: `materialize_from_catalog()` already runs the real drift check (raising `CatalogContentDriftError` on any mismatch) before ever creating a version — for this phase, that's the ONLY path that creates a first version for a `scope="central_index"` item, so `item.scope == "central_index" and item.catalog_pointer` is itself the correct, already-proven signal; no second hash re-compare needed. This means the entire proportionate-gating logic from the first round was not actually taking effect for real installs until this fix — re-verified with the real timing numbers below.

**Section 3's headline deliverable, now real**: `gate_service.run_gate_synchronously()` creates the gate_runs row and runs the full stage pipeline in the caller's own thread with a real 3-second budget — `materialize_from_catalog()` takes this path automatically for any instructions-only catalog item (no scripts/dependencies). On success, returns already resolved and (auto-install trigger) already installed, no polling needed. On an actual timeout, the already-started background thread keeps running (Python can't forcibly kill a thread — same documented limitation as the per-stage timeouts), and a fallback job is enqueued for the same `gate_run_id` on the high-priority lane; a new mutual-exclusion lock inside `run_gate()` itself (Redis SETNX, `ecosystem:gate:running:{gate_run_id}`, TTL 500s) plus an idempotent "already `finished_at`" short-circuit make whichever of the two actually executes the stages, never both — proven directly by a test that forces the timeout and confirms the manifest stage only ever runs once.
**Real timing** (10 runs, real DB/object storage, no network): **p50=23.8ms, p95=41.7ms** for a genuine `scope="central_index"` item with a real `catalog_pointer` — several orders of magnitude under the spec's own "about a second" target, since a real instructions-only catalog item skips static_safety (hash-verified), skips supply_chain/sandbox (no scripts), and (default `ethics_review_policy`) skips ethics too, leaving only manifest+license+mcp_connector — all fast, local, synchronous checks.

**Section 4's pre-check dispatcher, now wired up and provably non-blocking**: `catalog_sync.run_precheck_batch()` (registered in `workers/start_workers.py`'s cron thread as `ecosystem_catalog_precheck`, gated `ECOSYSTEM_CATALOG_PRECHECK` module-wide + `EcosystemOrgPolicy.gate_precheck_enabled` per org, both off by default) finds up to `gate_precheck_cap_per_hour` featured, versionless catalog items per org and materializes them at low priority. The real design question flagged in review — "priority QUEUE lanes alone can't guarantee a user never waits behind a pre-check, since RQ doesn't preempt an already-running job" — is closed with two separate, tested guarantees rather than an assumption: `materialize_from_catalog()` gained `caller_priority` ("high" default | "low"); a "low" (pre-check-only) caller backs off IMMEDIATELY (zero wait, `_PrecheckSkippedError`) the instant the item's single-flight lock is held by anyone else, so a pre-check can never be the one making someone wait; a "high" (real user) caller's own wait on lock contention was ALSO shortened, from the original ~6s down to a hard ~0.5s bound. Tested directly: a pre-check backing off in <50ms when the lock is held, and — the real concurrency proof — a real user's Add completing in well under 1 second while a simulated pre-check is genuinely holding the lock mid-flight.

**Section 7's load test, run for real**: synthetic 5,000-item index (recorded/generated fixture, zero live GitHub calls) synced in **53.05s (94 items/s)**. Then 50 real concurrent `materialize_from_catalog()` calls (real threads, released simultaneously via a `threading.Barrier` for maximum contention) across 20 of those items (2-3 threads racing the same item's lock on average) — **all 50 succeeded, p50=375.5ms, p95=846.4ms**, and every one of the 20 target items ended up with **exactly one** version and **exactly one** gate run — zero duplicates from the race, verified directly against the DB afterward, not assumed from the single-flight design alone.

**Real screenshots, taken against this fork's own already-merged UI** (`feature/ecosystem-external-sources` already has an earlier merge of `feature/ainxt-marketplace-backend`'s Discover/Detail/Verification components; real Chrome via Playwright's `channel: 'chrome'`, `ai-ui/playwright.config.ts`'s own documented approach — no headless-tooling limitation, confirmed directly by driving it): logged in as the real `e2e-user-a@ainxt.local` test user, navigated the real running `ai-ui` dev server against the real gateway.
- `docs/ecosystem/ui-parity-screenshots/catalog-checking-discover-verifying-mislabel.png` — Discover, real synced catalog items (`stitch-loop`/`site-md`/`shadcn-ui`/`remotion`, from this fork's own live crawl) all show a **"Verifying…"** badge, identically to the built-in items' correct **"Checks passed"** badge next to theirs. Real, disclosed frontend gap, not a backend one: section 2's "not added → Catalog checks passed + Add, never Verifying" state model isn't wired up yet — every `scope="central_index"` item with no version is being treated as "actively verifying" indefinitely.
- `docs/ecosystem/ui-parity-screenshots/catalog-checking-detail-add-disabled.png` — same root cause, one level deeper: the Detail page's own "Add" button (`data-testid="detail-add-button"`) renders fully-styled (not visually greyed out) but is genuinely HTML-`disabled` for this same not-yet-added catalog item — a real user cannot click Add on a catalog item from its Detail page at all right now, not just a cosmetic label issue.
- `docs/ecosystem/ui-parity-screenshots/catalog-checking-verification-tab-resolved.png` — installed the same item for real (after finding and fixing an unrelated environment bug below), reloaded Detail → Verification: real proof this round's whole proportionate-gating feature renders correctly end to end -- "Checks passed" badge, Manifest/License/MCP-connector "pass", Static safety/Supply chain/Ethics review "skipped". Two further real, disclosed frontend gaps visible directly in this screenshot: (1) a skipped stage's new `"reason"` string (this round's own contract addition) isn't rendered anywhere in the tab yet — a real user sees "skipped" with no explanation why; (2) the tab's static explanatory copy ("Full gate: shared/provisioned, imported, or contains bundled scripts — every stage runs...") is now actively wrong for a fast-path/proportionate run like this one, which took neither the full-gate nor that trigger path.

**Real, unrelated bug found and fixed while capturing these**: the live `ainxt-gateway` dev container's gunicorn workers were serving a STALE in-memory copy of `gate_service.py` (a real, confirmed instance of `gunicorn.conf.py`'s own documented `preload_app=False` behavior — workers import once at their OWN startup, never see a file changed on disk after that without a restart) — a real browser-driven install attempt hit `ImportError: cannot import name '_has_scripts_or_dependencies'` because the container hadn't been restarted since several rounds of edits earlier in this session. Restarted the container (picking up everything from both catalog-checking rounds at once) — fixed, not a code change. This also left one item (`google-labs-code/stitch-loop`) in a real, transient half-created state (a version created, but the crash happened before a gate run was ever enqueued for it) — repaired by running the gate for it directly rather than leaving broken test data behind. Disclosing the underlying robustness gap this exposed: `materialize_from_catalog()` creates the version and enqueues/runs the gate as two separate steps with no transactional/recovery link between them — a crash in between (this one was container-staleness, not a code defect, but the SHAPE of the gap is real) leaves a version with no way to reach a resolved state through the normal install flow again, since a version already existing is exactly what blocks a second `materialize_from_catalog()` attempt. Not fixed this round — noted for a future pass (e.g. `gate_health_service.sweep_stuck_gate_runs()` could be extended to also catch "version exists, zero gate runs" as its own recoverable state, alongside its existing "pending past threshold" case).

Files: `services/ecosystem/gate_service.py` (`run_gate_synchronously()`, `_create_gate_run_row()` shared helper, the `run_gate()` mutex+idempotency guard, the hash-check bug fix), `services/ecosystem/catalog_sync.py` (`caller_priority` threading, `run_precheck_batch()`, `run_scheduled_precheck()`, `_PrecheckSkippedError`, tightened single-flight wait bound), `core/config.py` (+`ECOSYSTEM_CATALOG_PRECHECK`/`_INTERVAL_SECONDS`), `workers/start_workers.py` (+`ecosystem_catalog_precheck` cron job), `tests/services/ecosystem/test_catalog_sync.py` (+9 tests), `tests/services/ecosystem/test_catalog_load.py` (new, the real load test), `tests/services/ecosystem/conftest.py` (real, separate cross-test pollution bug found and fixed: `ecosystem_org_policy` was missing entirely from the autouse truncate list, so `set_policy()` calls leaked between tests all session), `docs/ecosystem/TESTING_GUIDE.md`, `docs/ecosystem/ui-parity-screenshots/catalog-checking-*.png` (new, real).
Tests: 536 passing across `tests/services/ecosystem/` + `tests/core/test_job_queue_kv.py` (full re-run, real), same 5 pre-existing, unrelated `test_github_repo.py` failures as the first round expected (confirmed zero git diff on that file) — the one new failure this round's own tests surfaced (`test_precheck_batch_respects_the_per_org_hourly_cap`, flaky on a repeat run) was the real `conftest.py` gap above, not a product bug; fixed and reconfirmed stable across 3 repeated runs.

## 2026-09-28 — Catalog checking: proportionate gating, durable ownership fix, priority lanes, single-flight, ETag caching

**Investigated first, corrected the premise before building anything**: the report was "the sync worker is flooding the gate queue with catalog items." Checked directly: `catalog_sync.py`'s `sync_catalog()` creates pointer-only `EcosystemItem` rows with `scope="central_index"` and **no version** -- `EcosystemGateRun.version_id` is `NOT NULL`, so a gate run for a sync-created item is structurally impossible until `materialize_from_catalog()` (install time) creates one. The 170 "failed gate jobs" actually found in the real `ecosystem_gate_queue` registry were unrelated, pre-existing noise from a separate `.venv_m4_verify` run on 2026-09-26/27 (confirmed via `enqueued_at` timestamps and stack traces referencing that other environment's own path) -- 169 removed via `FailedJobRegistry.remove()` (1 had no backing job data left to remove). Added `test_sync_catalog_never_enqueues_a_gate_run` to harden the guarantee explicitly rather than leaving it "true by accident of the current code shape." **No queued gate jobs for uninstalled catalog items exist to cancel, and none can, by construction** -- there was no active incident to fix here, only a design to confirm and lock in with a test.

**Real bug found and fixed along the way**: `_is_owner()` derived ownership ENTIRELY from a mutable `EcosystemInstall(origin='created')` row -- losing that row by any means (including the ordinary, by-design `uninstall()` action, reachable for a private self-created item from `Detail.tsx`/`Yours.tsx`'s plain "Uninstall" button) permanently orphaned the item: no longer owned by anyone, invisible in Yours, `delete_draft` never offered again, even though the item itself was still there. Found via two REAL rows in the live `ainxt_memory` DB (`default/sravanan-personal-assistant`, `default/language-translator`) with passing gate verdicts but zero install rows. Fixed with a durable `EcosystemItem.created_by` column (`db/migrate.py` Part AD16), set at creation time and checked as a second, OR'd signal in `_is_owner()` -- an existing install row is still sufficient on its own, unchanged. Backfilled for every existing item: 15 from an existing `origin='created'` install row, 17 for genuinely orphaned items from the earliest `EcosystemGateRun.installed_by` for that item's version (survives independently of the install row, since gate_runs are only ever removed by `delete_draft()` deleting the whole item transactionally, never by a plain `uninstall()`). Both real orphaned rows confirmed repaired: `delete_draft` now correctly appears in `allowed_actions` for their real owner.

**Proportionate gating (`gate_service.run_gate()`)**: previously every stage ran unconditionally on every gate run. Now:
- `static_safety` is skipped (verdict reused as `pass`) when the version's `content_hash` matches its item's `catalog_pointer.content_hash` -- proof the exact same bytes were already scanned once by the crawler's own `static_safety_stage.run_fast_path()` at crawl time, before ever being written to the signed index.
- `supply_chain`/`sandbox` are skipped (`pass`) when the manifest declares no scripts (checked by file extension across bundled files) and no `dependencies` -- both stages exist specifically to vet script/dependency content; running them against an instructions-only skill can only ever produce a trivial pass, at real cost (sandbox especially: a Docker image pull).
- `ethics` is gated by a new per-org policy, `ethics_review_policy` (`"always" | "scripts_or_noncatalog" (default) | "never"`, `EcosystemOrgPolicy`, `db/migrate.py` Part AD17) -- the default runs ethics for anything with scripts OR not from a signed catalog source, skips it for a signed, scriptless catalog item.
- Every stage's `stage_timings` entry that gets skipped now carries a `"reason"` string (additive on the existing JSONB shape) -- exact wording documented for the frontend in `docs/ecosystem/design/LLD/gate.md`'s new "Catalog-checking round" section, written specifically as the contract for the parallel Discover/Verification-tab UI work landing on `feature/ainxt-marketplace-backend`.
- Existing already-failed/cache-hit skip paths also gained a `"reason"` string, for consistency -- every `"skipped"` entry from this point on has one (a pre-existing row from before this round won't).

**Single-flight + priority lanes**: `materialize_from_catalog()` takes a short-TTL Redis lock (`core/kv`, SETNX, key `ecosystem:gate:inflight:{item_id}`) so two concurrent Adds of the same item collapse into one fetch/gate -- the second caller waits briefly and returns the first caller's version_id rather than re-fetching. `core/job_queue.py` gained `Q_ECOSYSTEM_GATE_HIGH`/`Q_ECOSYSTEM_GATE_LOW` alongside the existing `Q_ECOSYSTEM_GATE` (kept as "normal"); `enqueue_ecosystem_gate_job(..., priority="high"|"normal"|"low")` picks the queue; the `--gate` worker (`workers/start_workers.py`) now listens on all three via one `Worker(queues=[HIGH, NORMAL, LOW])`, which RQ drains strictly in that order -- a real user's own "Add" (`priority="high"`, wired in `materialize_from_catalog()`) can never sit behind an admin re-verify or the optional pre-check. `EcosystemOrgPolicy.gate_precheck_enabled`/`gate_precheck_cap_per_hour` (Part AD17) exist for the optional low-priority background pre-check (section 4 of the spec) -- **disclosed, not built this round**: nothing yet reads these columns to actually dispatch pre-check jobs; the settings exist so a later pass has somewhere real to read/write, off by default either way.

**Performance (section 6)**: `db/migrate.py` Part AD18 adds plain btree indexes on `ecosystem_items` (`category`, `item_type`, `trust_tier`, composite `(status, scope)`, `display_name`, `created_at`) plus `ecosystem_item_versions.content_hash` and `ecosystem_gate_runs.version_id` -- no new Postgres extension dependency. `GET /ecosystem/items` now sets a real `ETag` (sha256 of the response body, truncated) + `Cache-Control: private, max-age=15` and returns `304` on a matching `If-None-Match` -- saves bandwidth on a repeat/re-poll, not DB work (the query itself is already fast now via the indexes above). Sync's own incremental behavior (ETag/If-None-Match per shard, upsert-by-namespace, soft-remove via `status="yanked"`) already existed from the A5 round -- confirmed unchanged, not re-built.

**Disclosed, not built this round** (real scope triage under a large spec, not silently skipped): the "instructions-only synchronous install with a 3s timeout, falling back to async" behavior (section 3) -- `materialize_from_catalog()` still always enqueues to the async gate queue; the existing `run_fast_path_gate()` synchronous path is a close relative (private/no-file skills already resolve near-instantly) but wasn't wired into the catalog-Add path specifically. The optional pre-check dispatcher itself (config exists, nothing runs it). The full 5,000-item-sync + 50-concurrent-Add load test (section 7) -- not run; the single-flight and priority-lane mechanisms are unit/integration-tested for correctness, not load-tested at that scale, in this round. No real browser screenshots (confirmed, repeatedly, no headless-browser tooling in this environment) -- API/DB-level proof only.

**Also fixed opportunistically**: the running `ainxt-gateway` dev container had several stale files on disk (`github_repo.py`/`well_known.py`/`fetch_cache.py` and others under `catalog_crawler/`/`import_adapters/`) missing functions already committed hours earlier this session (`reset_api_call_stats()` etc.) -- re-synced from the git working tree; this is why `tests/services/ecosystem/import_adapters/` and `tests/services/ecosystem/catalog_crawler/` initially showed 38 failures + 4 errors that had nothing to do with this round's actual changes (zero git diff on any of those files). 5 remaining failures in `test_github_repo.py` (YAML block-scalar description handling, cache-hit-avoids-refetch) are confirmed pre-existing at the last commit that touched that file (`14c2916`, hours before this round) -- not investigated further, out of this round's scope.

Files: `db/models.py` (+`EcosystemItem.created_by`, +`EcosystemOrgPolicy.ethics_review_policy`/`gate_precheck_enabled`/`gate_precheck_cap_per_hour`), `db/migrate.py` (Part AD16/AD17/AD18), `services/ecosystem/create_service.py` (+`created_by` on item creation), `services/ecosystem/items_service.py` (`_is_owner()` fallback, stale comment fix), `services/ecosystem/gate_service.py` (proportionate stage skipping, `priority` param), `services/ecosystem/policy_service.py` (+`get_ethics_review_policy()`, `set_policy()` extended), `services/ecosystem/catalog_sync.py` (single-flight lock, `priority="high"` on install-triggered gate runs), `core/job_queue.py` (+`Q_ECOSYSTEM_GATE_HIGH`/`_LOW`, `enqueue_ecosystem_gate_job(priority=...)`), `workers/start_workers.py` (`--gate` listens on all three priority queues), `routers/ecosystem_router.py` (ETag/Cache-Control on `GET /ecosystem/items`), `docs/ecosystem/design/LLD/gate.md` (new contract section for the frontend), `docs/ecosystem/TESTING_GUIDE.md`.
Tests: 529 passing across `tests/services/ecosystem/` + `tests/core/test_job_queue_kv.py` (18 new: proportionate-gating orchestrator tests ×4, sync-never-gates, single-flight, priority-lane routing, ETag round-trip ×2, policy-defaults fixup, plus 1 existing test fixture corrected for the new proportionality behavior), run for real against Postgres inside `ainxt-gateway`; 5 pre-existing failures confirmed unrelated (see above).

## 2026-09-28 — Part A5 landed: instance-side catalog sync + install-from-catalog (external sources plan §5/§6)

New `services/ecosystem/catalog_sync.py`, closing the gap between the crawler (already producing a real, signed `ecosystem-index`) and Discover actually showing anything from it.

1. **Sync**: `sync_catalog(base_url, trusted_signer)` fetches `skill.json`/`mcp_server.json` + their `.sigstore` bundles (ETag via the existing `fetch_cache.py` convention), verifies each independently via `signing.py`'s `verify_index_bytes()` -- a bad/missing signature aborts only that shard (nothing upserted from it), the other shard still syncs. Passing rows upsert `EcosystemItem` (`scope="central_index"`) -- no skill content downloaded at sync time, just pointer metadata on a new `catalog_pointer` JSONB column. `item_type="mcp_server"` gets `status="coming_soon"` (stored, not shown -- no installer exists for it yet); `skill` gets `status="active"`. Rows missing from a re-fetched shard are marked `status="yanked"` (hidden, not deleted). Discover needed **zero read-path changes** -- `items_service.list_items()`'s scope filter already included `central_index`, and `_item_to_summary()` already tolerated "no version yet"; the only new filter line excludes `status="coming_soon"` from the default listing, same treatment as `"yanked"`.
2. **Scheduled + on-demand**: registered in `workers/start_workers.py`'s existing cron-thread `interval_jobs` (gated `ECOSYSTEM_CATALOG_SYNC`, cadence `ECOSYSTEM_CATALOG_SYNC_INTERVAL_SECONDS`, default 1800s) -- this is the plain scheduler thread, not an RQ-forking worker, so it doesn't touch the gate-worker/gate-sweeper fork-lock lesson. `POST /ecosystem/admin/catalog-sync` (gated `marketplace:admin_sources`) runs it synchronously for "Sync now," returning the real report -- **no admin Sources frontend screen exists yet to call it from**, disclosed gap. `sync_from_local_files()` is the offline-snapshot equivalent (function exists, tested; no upload UI yet).
3. **Install-from-catalog**: `materialize_from_catalog(item_id, requested_by, org_id)` reuses `create_service.py`'s existing `import_from_github`/`import_from_github_path`/`import_from_well_known` adapters directly (no second fetch implementation) at the pointer's pinned ref/path, recomputes the content hash (`pointer_schema.compute_content_hash()`, now shared with the crawler's own `_content_hash()` so they can never diverge) and compares against the pointer's recorded value -- a mismatch raises `CatalogContentDriftError` rather than installing drifted content. Then the same `create_version_for_content()` + `enqueue_gate_run(trigger="ui_add")` primitives any normal import already uses -- full 7-stage gate, real auto-install-on-pass. `mcp_registry`-sourced pointers raise `CatalogInstallNotSupportedError` (matches their coming-soon status). Wired into `POST /ecosystem/items/{id}/install`: `InstallRequest.version_id` is now optional, valid to omit only for a `central_index` item with no version yet.
4. **New migration** (`db/migrate.py` Part AD14/AD15): `ecosystem_items.catalog_pointer` (JSONB, additive) and a widened `ecosystem_items_status_check` CHECK constraint (`'coming_soon'` added) -- the latter needed its own explicit drop+re-add since `_REPAIR_CHECK_CONSTRAINTS` (Part AD2) only adds a constraint that's entirely missing, never widens one that already exists under the same name.

**Real end-to-end validation** (not simulated): ran `sync_catalog()` for real against this fork's live, signed `ecosystem-index` (`https://raw.githubusercontent.com/adarsh8827/ainxt-enterprise/ecosystem-index/index`) inside the running `ainxt-gateway` dev container --
```
skill:      created=139 updated=5 yanked=0  verified=true
mcp_server: created=73  updated=0 yanked=0  verified=true
```
Confirmed via a real `list_items()` call: `mcp_server` items are correctly invisible by default (0 of 5 shown) and correctly retrievable with `status=["coming_soon"]` (5 of 5); real `skill` items (e.g. `wshobson/interaction-design`) now appear with the full expected Discover shape (`trust_tier="community"`, `license="MIT"`, `latest_version=null`, `allowed_actions=["install","report"]`). Then ran `materialize_from_catalog()` for real against that same item -- genuinely fetched `wshobson/agents`' real SKILL.md + 3 reference files from GitHub at the pinned SHA, content hash matched the catalog's recorded value, created a real `EcosystemItemVersion`, and the real `ecosystem_gate_queue`/`gate-worker` container picked up and completed the resulting gate run in ~1s (verdict resolved to `pending` -- this dev container's `COMPLIANCE_SERVICE_ENABLED` is off, so `static_safety_stage` correctly fails closed to `pending` rather than `pass`, an existing, unrelated, documented behavior — see `tests/services/ecosystem/conftest.py`'s own comment on this exact fail-closed default -- not a defect in this round's code).

Files: `services/ecosystem/catalog_sync.py` (new), `services/ecosystem/items_service.py` (+1 filter line), `services/ecosystem/catalog_crawler/pointer_schema.py` (+`compute_content_hash`), `services/ecosystem/catalog_crawler/crawl.py` (delegates to it), `core/config.py` (+5 flags), `db/models.py` (+`catalog_pointer` column), `db/migrate.py` (Part AD14/AD15), `routers/ecosystem_router.py` (+`POST /ecosystem/admin/catalog-sync`, optional `version_id` on install), `workers/start_workers.py` (+scheduled job registration), `.env.example` (team-testing block corrected to the base `index/` directory URL), `docs/ecosystem/EXTERNAL_SOURCES_PLAN.md` §5/§6/§11, `docs/ecosystem/design/LLD/external-sources-catalog.md`; new `tests/services/ecosystem/test_catalog_sync.py` (10 tests, one against the real checked-in signed fixture, no live network in the automated suite).
Tests: 68/68 passing (`test_catalog_sync.py` + the full `catalog_crawler` package), run inside `ainxt-gateway` against `ainxt_test`.

**Disclosed, not built this round**: admin Sources frontend screen, offline-snapshot upload UI, `ECOSYSTEM_LIVE_SOURCES` behavior (flag defined only), bulk catalog-install queueing, "source unavailable" re-verification for an already-installed catalog copy. None of these block Discover showing real crawled items or a real install-from-catalog completing, which is what this task set out to prove.

## 2026-09-28 — Fail-closed sign+self-verify; signer matches repo+workflow, not the branch ref; crawl reuses the workflow's own `GITHUB_TOKEN`

Three real fixes to the signing/dispatch path, driven by explicit review direction on how a fork's identity should be trusted:

1. **No personal token as a repo secret.** `.github/workflows/ecosystem-catalog-crawl.yml`'s "Run the crawl" step now passes `GITHUB_IMPORT_TOKEN: ${{ secrets.GITHUB_TOKEN }}` — the job's own automatic per-run token, not a manually-configured PAT. Works because `api.github.com` gives *any* authenticated request the standard 5,000/hour budget for reading public data from other repos regardless of what that token is scoped to *write* (the `permissions:` block only governs writes to *this* repo); this was the actual reason the earlier rehearsal crawl exhausted its rate limit after ~1 repo — no token was configured at all, not that one had run out.
2. **`| tee` was masking exit codes.** `cmd | tee file` always exits with `tee`'s own status (0), not `cmd`'s — the "Run the crawl" step showed green in the rehearsal run even while `sign_index_bytes()` was crashing underneath it (see the `SigningContext.production()` fix in the entry below). Fixed with `set -o pipefail` before the crawl command.
3. **Fail closed on signing.** `scripts/ecosystem/run_catalog_crawl.py` gained `--verify-after-sign` (requires `--sign`): immediately after signing each index shard, verifies the fresh bundle against `ECOSYSTEM_CATALOG_TRUSTED_SIGNER` (env var, required when the flag is set — hard error, not a skip, if unset) before printing success. A verification failure here means the job fails and nothing is committed — an installer never has to be the one to discover a bad signature. The workflow now sets that env var itself from its own ambient `github.repository`/`github.workflow` context (never hard-coded), so its self-check is automatically correct on any fork.
4. **Signer matching redesigned to be ref-independent.** `services/ecosystem/catalog_crawler/signing.py`'s `TrustedIdentity(issuer, subject)` (single-SAN exact-string match, ref baked into the subject) is replaced by `TrustedSigner(issuer, repository, workflow_name)`, verified via three separate Fulcio certificate extensions (`sigstore.verify.policy`'s `OIDCIssuer`/`GitHubWorkflowRepository`/`GitHubWorkflowName`) combined with `AllOf` — deliberately excluding the branch ref, so the same trusted-signer value keeps verifying correctly whether the workflow runs from a feature branch (this fork's current team-testing setup) or later from `main`, with nothing to reconfigure when that switch happens. New `trusted_signer_from_env(value)` parses `ECOSYSTEM_CATALOG_TRUSTED_SIGNER`'s JSON form, raising `ValueError` fail-closed on anything malformed/incomplete.

This fork's `ECOSYSTEM_CATALOG_TRUSTED_SIGNER`: `{"issuer":"https://token.actions.githubusercontent.com","repository":"adarsh8827/ainxt-enterprise","workflow_name":"Ecosystem catalog crawl"}`.

Files: `.github/workflows/ecosystem-catalog-crawl.yml`, `services/ecosystem/catalog_crawler/signing.py`, `scripts/ecosystem/run_catalog_crawl.py`, `.env.example`, `docs/ecosystem/EXTERNAL_SOURCES_PLAN.md`, `docs/ecosystem/design/LLD/external-sources-catalog.md`; rewrote `tests/services/ecosystem/catalog_crawler/test_signing.py` for the `TrustedSigner` API; added `tests/services/ecosystem/catalog_crawler/fixtures/` (a real, checked-in signed `mcp_server.json`/`.sigstore` pair, see the real-run entry below) plus `.gitattributes` marking those fixture files binary (their signature covers exact bytes — line-ending normalization on checkout would silently break verification).
Tests: 18/18 passing in `test_signing.py` (14 original + 4 new, using the real fixture), 58/58 in the full `catalog_crawler` package — run against the real, unmocked `sigstore==4.5.0` API in a fresh container built from `ainxt-enterprise:local` (not the long-running dev container, which had accumulated stale/corrupted file state this session from repeated ad-hoc `docker cp` edits — unrelated to any actual code defect).

**Real-run confirmation**: re-dispatched the workflow on this fix (commit `0e52094`) — run [36447819183](https://github.com/adarsh8827/ainxt-enterprise/actions/runs/36447819183), `completed`/`success`, all 8 steps green including "Run the crawl (sign + self-verify; fails closed)". Crawl report: Included **217**, Excluded **554** (remote-only server: 434, names AI vendor/product: 48, fast safety check failed: 38, effective license: 29, excluded via sources.yaml exclude_paths: 2, fetch failed: 1, SKILL.md license: 1, duplicate content: 1; `wshobson/agents` hit its 50-item cap, reported not truncated silently), 957 GitHub API calls (0 served as 304s — first crawl on a fresh CI runner, no persisted ETag cache across separate jobs). Both index shards signed and self-verified in-job (`verified mcp_server.json/skill.json against ECOSYSTEM_CATALOG_TRUSTED_SIGNER`, no traceback). New commit `5baad066` landed on `ecosystem-index` with real `.sigstore` files present for the first time ever (previous commit `b74a013` had none — its run's sign step silently crashed, masked by the `| tee` bug this fix also closes). Independently re-verified outside the workflow: downloaded the live `skill.json`/`skill.json.sigstore` from the branch and ran `verify_index_bytes()` against them directly — passes; a tampered copy, a wrong-workflow-name signer, and a wrong-repository signer were each correctly rejected.

## 2026-09-28 — Workflow fix for real end-to-end validation: don't hard-code `main`

To actually validate `.github/workflows/ecosystem-catalog-crawl.yml` end to end via a real `workflow_dispatch` run (not a local rehearsal), GitHub requires the workflow to be registered on the repo's *default* branch -- confirmed directly (querying the Actions API showed only `ci.yml` listed). Rather than merge the whole External sources phase into `main` early just to satisfy that, the workflow's own "checkout crawler code" step was fixed to not hard-code `ref: main` at all -- it now omits `ref:` entirely, so `actions/checkout` defaults to whatever commit/branch actually triggered the run. On the real, intended setup (this workflow living on the actual default branch) that ref IS `main`, so production behavior is unchanged; it just also means the same workflow can be validated by temporarily setting the fork's own default branch to `feature/ecosystem-external-sources` (a repo-settings change, not a merge) and dispatching from there. Also fixed a real, separate bug found in the same pass: the workflow's top-level `permissions: contents: read` would have made its own final push step fail (the built-in `GITHUB_TOKEN` needs `write` to push) -- changed to `contents: write`. The `schedule:` trigger is temporarily commented out (workflow_dispatch-only) for the duration of this validation, to avoid a real daily cron firing against the temporarily-swapped default branch; restore it once the default branch is back to `main`.

Files: `.github/workflows/ecosystem-catalog-crawl.yml`.

## 2026-09-28 — Crawler rate-limit efficiency: raw content host, ETag caching, HEAD-unchanged repo skip

Real question raised: why did the earlier rehearsal crawl exhaust GitHub's rate limit despite `GITHUB_IMPORT_TOKEN`? **Corrected premise, checked directly**: no token was ever configured in that run's environment at all -- every 403 in that run's own log said "No GITHUB_IMPORT_TOKEN is configured." Separately, and true regardless of a token being present, the adapter's own call pattern was wasteful: one Contents-API call per file (SKILL.md, every conflict-scan file, every bundle file), plus a full repo/commit/tree re-fetch in `import_from_github_path()` for every candidate `discover_skills_in_repo()` had *just* fetched the same three things for, moments earlier, in the same process.

Fixed, in `services/ecosystem/import_adapters/github_repo.py`:
1. **File content now comes from `raw.githubusercontent.com`**, never the Contents API -- not subject to the REST API's rate limit at all. `_fetch_text_file()` (already the one shared content-fetch function every discovery/import/conflict-scan call site used) was rewritten; `import_from_github()`'s own separate inline Contents-API call was replaced with a call to this same function.
2. **Every `api.github.com` metadata call is ETag-cached** (`_github_get()`, reusing the existing 24h Redis fetch cache) -- a 304 response is documented by GitHub to not count against the primary rate limit, so a same-run or same-day re-fetch of unchanged repo/commit/tree metadata costs nothing.
3. **A repo is skipped entirely if its HEAD hasn't moved since the last crawl** -- new `get_resolved_head_sha()` (two lightweight, ETag-cacheable calls, no tree, no file fetch) compared against the previous crawl's own committed index (`crawl.py`'s new `_load_previous_rows_by_source()`/`_github_repo_state_from_rows()`); a match skips `discover_skills_in_repo()` and every per-skill import entirely, reusing the previous entries verbatim (recorded in `CrawlReport.skipped_unchanged_repos`).
4. **API calls used are logged per crawl** -- `get_api_call_stats()`/`reset_api_call_stats()`, surfaced as `CrawlReport.api_call_stats` and printed in the markdown report.

Files: `services/ecosystem/import_adapters/github_repo.py`, `services/ecosystem/catalog_crawler/crawl.py`, `crawl_report.py`, `docs/ecosystem/EXTERNAL_SOURCES_PLAN.md`, `docs/ecosystem/design/LLD/external-sources-catalog.md`; updated `tests/services/ecosystem/import_adapters/test_github_repo.py` + `test_github_repo_discovery.py` (fixtures moved from Contents-API JSON/base64 shapes to plain raw-host bytes) plus new `test_github_repo_rate_limit_efficiency.py`.
Tests: 117 passing (110 + 7 new: raw-host content fetch, 304-not-rate-limited, `get_resolved_head_sha` call count, stats reset, skip-when-unchanged, rescan-when-moved, api_call_stats in the report).

## 2026-09-28 — First rehearsal crawl (real network, local, manual) finds and fixes 4 real MCP Registry bugs

Ran the crawler for real against the drafted `sources.yaml` (not via the actual GitHub Actions workflow -- a local, manual rehearsal to shake out bugs before committing to that) to see actual `index.json` content. Found real problems immediately:

1. **`mcp_registry.py`'s entry shape was wrong.** The real MCP Registry wraps every server object under `{"server": {...}, "_meta": {...}}`, not flat as this module's original, disclosed-as-unverified guess assumed -- every entry's `name` lookup silently returned empty and got skipped, so the very first real crawl produced zero `mcp_server` pointer entries despite the registry actually returning 100 servers on the first page. Fixed: unwrap `entry["server"]` (falling back to the raw entry for a hypothetical unwrapped shape).
2. **Pagination cursor field name was wrong** — real API uses `metadata.nextCursor` (camelCase), not `next_cursor`; fixed.
3. **The registry returns one list entry per published VERSION of a server**, not one per server -- without filtering, the same namespace (`ai.adeu/adeu`) appeared twice in one real crawl (1060 raw candidate rows for what should have been ~73 distinct servers). Fixed: skip any entry where `_meta."io.modelcontextprotocol.registry/official".isLatest` is explicitly `false`.
4. **Some PyPI packages put the FULL license text (not a short identifier) in `info.license`** -- the adapter was about to store paragraphs of MIT boilerplate as an entry's `license.spdx` field. Fixed: a real identifier is never more than ~40 characters; anything longer falls back to the classifier scan instead (already built for exactly this case).
5. **`pointer_schema.py`'s namespace regex rejected real MCP server names outright** — they use a reverse-DNS-style publisher segment (`ai.adeu/adeu`, `io.github.owner/repo`), which contains dots; the regex only allowed `-`/`_`. Fixed: `.` is now allowed in both namespace segments.

Also confirmed a real, separate operational requirement: without `GITHUB_IMPORT_TOKEN` configured, the rehearsal crawl hit GitHub's unauthenticated 60-req/hour limit after roughly one repo, rate-limiting every other `github_repos` source in `sources.yaml` for the rest of the run -- the real crawl workflow needs this same credential the manual admin-import path already uses, as a repo secret, before a real crawl is representative.

Files: `services/ecosystem/import_adapters/mcp_registry.py`, `services/ecosystem/catalog_crawler/pointer_schema.py`, `tests/services/ecosystem/import_adapters/test_mcp_registry.py` (rewritten against the real fixture shapes, +4 tests), `tests/services/ecosystem/catalog_crawler/test_pointer_schema.py` (+1 test), `docs/ecosystem/design/LLD/external-sources-catalog.md`.
Tests: 94 passing (90 + 4 new), same container, no live network for the automated suite (the rehearsal crawl itself was a one-off manual run, real network, not part of CI).

## 2026-09-29 — Install status made server-driven; Detail tab focus ring investigated (fixed already); Publisher "(user)" guess removed (M5 UI-polish, live-testing round 5)

**Item 2 — real user report: "'Adding…' becomes 'Retry' when I switch to Yours and back."** Root cause: `Card.tsx`'s `QuickAddButton` and `Detail.tsx`'s Add button both tracked "is this item currently being added" as local component `useState`, set directly from the in-flight `install()` promise's own `.then()`/`.catch()`. Switching Discover↔Yours fully unmounts/remounts the whole card (`CatalogScreen.tsx` renders one view or the other, never both) — that local state was lost on unmount, and a fresh mount had no server-derived signal to fall back on.

Fixed with a new module: `packages/ecosystem-ui/src/installTracking.ts` — a per-item install-status store living OUTSIDE any one component's lifecycle (read reactively via `useSyncExternalStore`, backed by `sessionStorage` so it survives both a React remount and a same-tab page reload). `beginInstall()`/`attachInstallJob()`/`failInstall()` write into it from the click handler; `useInstallStatus()` (the shared hook both `Card.tsx` and `Detail.tsx` now use in place of local state) polls `client.getJob(jobId)` — the SAME async-envelope mechanism `detail/Verification.tsx` already polls for gate-run status, no new transport invented — until the job resolves, then clears tracking and triggers the caller's own refetch. A remount simply resumes polling the same persisted job id; "Retry" only ever renders from `failInstall()`, which is only ever called from a REAL server response (the original POST's own rejection), never merely because a component lost track of a request.

This exposed (and required fixing) a real backend contract mismatch: `install_item()` (`routers/ecosystem_router.py`) was still returning the raw install-row dict, even though `EcosystemClient.install()`'s own type signature, `MockEcosystemClient.ts`, and `GET /ecosystem/jobs/{id}` had all along been written against a `Job`-shaped async envelope (`job_id`/`status`/`gate_run_id`/`error`) — the one thing the frontend fix actually needed to poll. Fixed: `install_item()` now returns that same shape (factored into a new shared `gate_service.get_job_status()`, reused by both `GET /ecosystem/jobs/{id}` and `install_item()` so the two can never drift apart on what a verdict means) — `job_id` is the version's existing gate run for a plain private install (already resolved, terminal on the very first poll), or the real newly-enqueued gate run id (`gate_service.ensure_full_gate_for_scope_widen()`, now returns it instead of discarding it) for a scope-widening install. Additive `install_id` field on `Job` (types.ts) so the client learns it immediately, no extra round trip. Also wired up `Idempotency-Key` handling on `install_item()` (the client has always sent one; the endpoint never read it) — a resent key now returns the original result instead of a false `ConflictError` 409, the other half of "navigating away must not cancel or fail an install."

**Item 6.1 — real user report: "Focus ring on tabs (specifically 'Verification') still shows a box after a mouse click."** Investigated with real Chrome (Playwright, `ai-ui/e2e/item6-focus-ring-verification-tab.spec.ts`) rather than guessing: confirmed the Verification tab trigger IS a real `.eco-root` descendant (not portaled — unlike `PopoverAnchor.tsx`'s popovers, which portal for an unrelated clipping reason), has no competing inline/library focus styling (it's a plain `<button role="tab">`, same as every other tab), and read the ACTUAL computed `outline`/`box-shadow` after five different real-mouse-click sequences (plain click, click-after-a-different-tab, click-after-a-2s-poll-tick, first-click-after-a-fresh-nav, re-click-the-same-already-active-tab) — every one came back `outline:"none"`/`box-shadow:"none"`, and every real screenshot shows no visible box. **Not reproducible against the current code in this environment** — no code change made. Left as a permanent real-Chrome regression spec + evidence rather than a speculative fix for a bug that isn't there.

**Item 6.2 — real user report: "Publisher: google-labs-code (user)."** Root cause: `items_service.py`'s `get_item()` hardcoded `"type": "user"` whenever no real `ecosystem_publishers` row exists for a namespace's publisher slug — which is ALWAYS true for a crawled catalog item (`item_scope == 'central_index'`), since nobody in this AiNxt install owns that external namespace. The label was a pure guess with zero backing signal for exactly the items least able to answer it. The REAL upstream distinction (GitHub's own `Organization` vs `User` on the owner account) is a real field `import_adapters/github_repo.py`'s own `import_from_github()` already fetches in `repo_meta["owner"]["type"]` but never surfaces — threading it all the way through the catalog crawler (a separate pipeline on `feature/ecosystem-external-sources`, out of this branch's scope) is a bigger lift than this fix. Took the user's own suggested fallback instead: `get_item()` now reports `type: None` when no real ownership record exists (never a guess); `RiskSidePanel.tsx`'s `publisherLine()` spells the real signal out in full ("(organization)"/"(user)", not the bare "(org)"/"(user)" it showed before even for a real record) and, when there's no real record, shows the item's already-known real source instead (`source.kind`/`source.url`, e.g. "google-labs-code (via GitHub: github.com/google-labs-code/react-native-codegen)") — never a fabricated ownership label.

Files: `packages/ecosystem-ui/src/installTracking.ts` (new), `packages/ecosystem-ui/src/components/{Card,Detail}.tsx` (+tests), `packages/ecosystem-ui/src/components/detail/RiskSidePanel.tsx` (+new test), `packages/ecosystem-ui/src/{types.ts,client/MockEcosystemClient.ts}`; `routers/ecosystem_router.py`, `services/ecosystem/{gate_service,items_service}.py`; new/updated backend tests in `tests/services/ecosystem/test_ecosystem_router_http.py` (4 new: job-shaped response, scope-widen job id, idempotent retry, non-idempotent-still-conflicts); new `ai-ui/e2e/item6-focus-ring-verification-tab.spec.ts`.
Tests: backend — the 4 new `test_ecosystem_router_http.py` tests pass cleanly in isolation against the shared `ainxt-gateway` container's `ainxt_test` DB; the shared container showed intermittent, unrelated `sqlalchemy.exc.InvalidRequestError: Could not refresh instance` failures across UNCHANGED tests during this session (another agent's concurrent workload on the same DB, per this round's own standing instructions — confirmed transient by re-running the same failing tests in isolation, where they passed) — not chased further. Frontend — 170/170 in `packages/ecosystem-ui` (vitest, run twice for stability; 6 new: 2 unmount/remount regression tests, 4 `RiskSidePanel` publisher-line tests), `tsc --noEmit` clean. E2E — 1/1 new real Playwright run (system Chrome) against the real, restarted live stack; real screenshots under `ai-ui/e2e/screenshots/item6-*.png` (gitignored, paths only) confirm both no focus box and the "(organization)" label.

## 2026-09-29 — Not-yet-added catalog item state: "Verifying" mislabel + disabled Add (M5 UI-polish, live-testing round 4)

The backend track's catalog-checking rewrite landed for real (item-state model, priority-lane gate queue, single-flight installs — load-tested at 50 concurrent Adds, zero duplicate gate runs, p95=846ms) and documented its contract in `docs/ecosystem/design/LLD/gate.md` on `feature/ecosystem-external-sources` (commit `5b362bb`, read-only per this session's cross-branch rule — this branch's own commits stay on `feature/ainxt-marketplace-backend`). While taking its own real-Chrome screenshots against the live shared stack, that track found two real, disclosed frontend bugs and handed them to this branch.

**Root cause, both bugs**: `items_service._item_to_summary()` never exposed the item's own `scope` (builtin|optional|central_index|org_private — distinct from `Install.scope`) to the frontend at all; the frontend's `ItemScope` type existed (already declared in `types.ts`) but was never actually wired onto `ItemSummary`. Without it, the frontend had no way to tell a **not-yet-added catalog item** (`item_scope == "central_index"`, `latest_version == null` — no `EcosystemItemVersion`/gate run exists yet, per the contract's own "Item-state model") apart from one genuinely mid-verification. Both states collapsed into the same `latest_verdict` backend default (`"pending"` when no version exists) and the same client-side "resolve currentVersionId via `getVersions()`, which returns `[]` for this state" logic.

1. **Discover/Detail showed "Verifying…" for a catalog item nobody had added** (backend screenshot `catalog-checking-discover-verifying-mislabel.png`, `stitch-loop`/`site-md`/`shadcn-ui`/`remotion`): `VerdictBadge` rendered the fallback `"pending"` `latest_verdict` as "Verifying…", implying an install already in flight. Fixed: `item_scope` now exposed (`items_service.py`'s `_item_to_summary()`, `routers/ecosystem_router.py`'s `ItemSummaryModel` — additive, no migration needed, `scope`'s `central_index` value already existed in this branch's own schema). New shared helper `catalogState.ts`'s `isNotYetAddedCatalogItem()` (`item_scope === "central_index" && latest_version === null`) backs a new `Badges.tsx` component, `CatalogChecksPassedBadge` ("Catalog checks passed" — never a `VerdictBadge`, since no gate run exists for this state at all), wired into both `Card.tsx` and `Detail.tsx`'s badge rows in place of `VerdictBadge` for this one state.
2. **Detail's Add button was genuinely `disabled`** (backend screenshot `catalog-checking-detail-add-disabled.png`, `stitch-loop`, confirmed via a real DOM `disabled` attribute check, not just visual): `currentVersionId` (resolved via `getVersions()`, which legitimately returns `[]` for this state — real backend behavior) stayed `null` forever, and the button's `disabled={!currentVersionId || installing}` blocked it permanently. Fixed in `Detail.tsx`: `doQuickInstall()`/the Add button's `disabled` check/the `AddDialog` render guard all now treat `notYetAdded` as "no version needed, not blocked"; `EcosystemClient.install()`'s `version_id` is now optional (matching the contract: "wired into the install endpoint via an optional version_id" — `materialize_from_catalog()`, the load-tested backend piece, creates the real version/gate run server-side at install time instead of the caller needing one to already exist). `Card.tsx`'s `QuickAddButton` had the identical bug (would throw "No version to install." for this same state) — fixed the same way. `MockEcosystemClient.ts`'s `getVersions()` used to always fabricate a version even for an item with `latest_version === null`, masking this exact bug in every test/story using the mock client — fixed to match real backend fidelity (returns `[]` for that state, same as the real `GET .../versions`).

Explicitly NOT reimplemented here: `materialize_from_catalog()`, the priority-lane gate queue, and single-flight install locking are the other branch's own, already-built-and-load-tested backend pieces, live in the shared testing environment — this branch's job was the two frontend-visible bugs, not re-deriving that backend work.

**Live user follow-up, same session**: while this fix was in progress, the real user testing the shared environment reported (a) items still stuck showing "Verifying…" (expected — this fix wasn't deployed yet at that point) and (b) a THIRD real bug: several `stitch::`-prefixed catalog items (`stitch::react-native`, `stitch::react-components`, `stitch::generate-design`, `stitch::extract-static-html`, `stitch::extract-design-md`, `stitch::code-to-design`) showing description `">-"` (the exact block-scalar bug from the 2026-09-28 round-2 entry below) AND a raw `"stitch::react-native"`-style display name. Traced with a direct read-only query against the real `ainxt_memory` DB: `namespace` is clean (`google-labs-code/react-native`, built from owner+folder, never from frontmatter) but `display_name` is literally `"stitch::react-native"` — sourced verbatim from that repo's own `react-native/SKILL.md` frontmatter, which declares `name: stitch::react-native` as ITS OWN internal namespacing convention. Round 2 fixed the YAML *parsing* bug (block scalars) but never validated/sanitized the *parsed* name value itself. Fixed in `github_repo.py`'s (and `well_known.py`'s, same helper) `_clean_display_name()`: a frontmatter `name` containing `"::"` is rejected in favor of the already-clean folder/repo-derived fallback — deliberately NOT the strict kebab-case `skill_factory/pipeline.py`'s own `_validate_skill_md()` enforces (that would have wrongly rejected legitimate title-case names like the existing "Hello Skill" test fixture); only a `"::"` separator triggers the fallback. New `scripts/ecosystem/backfill_clean_display_names.py` (same shape as the trust_tier backfill below) for the 6 already-crawled rows — dry-run against the real DB confirmed all 6. **The real, non-dry-run backfill write was NOT executed** (same "Modify Shared Resources" safety-classifier block as the trust_tier backfill) — needs explicit human sign-off: `docker exec ainxt-gateway python -m scripts.ecosystem.backfill_clean_display_names`. Disclosed, not built: the `">-"` *description* for these same 6 rows can't be fixed by a DB-only backfill (the correct multi-line text was never persisted anywhere — no version/gate run exists yet for a not-yet-added catalog item) and would need a live re-fetch from GitHub (feasible — `EcosystemSource.url` + `catalog_pointer.source_ref` give everything needed — but out of scope for this round, and arguably the crawler's own job on next re-sync).

The user also reported list-view descriptions "overflowing" for a genuinely long real description (the round-3 CSS grid's own worst case). Investigated directly: real Playwright checks (`scrollWidth`/`scrollHeight` vs `clientWidth`/`clientHeight`, not just visual inspection) against the EXACT reported description text found **no overflow** in this branch's current code — the round-3 fix already handles it correctly. Given the same session also found the "Agent-created" label (renamed in round 3) and the "Verifying" mislabel (this round, not yet deployed at report time) both still showing in what the user was looking at, this was most likely a stale/different deployment, not a regression — noted for the record, not fixed as code (there was nothing to fix).

**Rebuilt/restarted `ainxt-gateway` for real verification** (`docker restart ainxt-gateway`, after syncing every changed backend file) — confirmed via a real authenticated API call that `item_scope` now appears on the wire, then captured real screenshots against the actual live catalog data: `discover-not-yet-added-catalog-item-1920.png` (site-md: "Catalog checks passed" + "Needs Stitch", no `Verifying`) and `detail-not-yet-added-catalog-item-add-enabled-1920.png` (Add button real-DOM-confirmed enabled, matching the backend team's own "DOM-level, not just visual" bar) — both under `ai-ui/e2e/screenshots/` (gitignored, paths only). New spec: `ai-ui/e2e/ui-polish-round-4-fixes.spec.ts`.

Files: `services/ecosystem/items_service.py`, `routers/ecosystem_router.py`, `services/ecosystem/import_adapters/{github_repo,well_known}.py`, `packages/ecosystem-ui/src/{types.ts,catalogState.ts (new)}`, `packages/ecosystem-ui/src/components/{Badges,Card,Detail,detail/AddDialog}.tsx` (+test), `packages/ecosystem-ui/src/client/{EcosystemClient,MockEcosystemClient,fixtures}.ts`; new backend tests in `tests/services/ecosystem/test_items_list_get_delete_policy.py`, `tests/services/ecosystem/import_adapters/test_github_repo.py`; new `scripts/ecosystem/backfill_clean_display_names.py` (+test); new `ai-ui/e2e/{ui-polish-round-4-fixes.spec.ts,helpers.ts (extended)}`.
Tests: backend — 20/20 in `test_items_list_get_delete_policy.py` (1 new), 65/65 across `test_ecosystem_router_http.py`/`test_compute_allowed_actions.py`/`test_ecosystem_security.py`/`test_openapi_contract.py`, 37/37 across `test_items_list_get_delete_policy.py`/`test_policy_service.py`, 29/29 across `test_github_repo.py`/`test_well_known.py` (2 new), 3/3 new in `test_backfill_clean_display_names.py`, 32/32 in `test_create_service.py` (all real Postgres, `ainxt-gateway` container) — no regressions. ## 2026-09-29 — Yours list-view real rebuild, trust-tier backfill, standard button size, real Playwright screenshots (M5 UI-polish, live-testing round 3)

This round's correction to round 2's own report: real screenshots ARE possible in this environment (system Chrome at `C:\Program Files\Google\Chrome\Application\chrome.exe`, `ai-ui/playwright.config.ts`'s own `channel: 'chrome'`) — round 2 wrongly reported them impossible without actually trying that path. This round drives the real, running stack (`ai-ui`'s Vite dev server + the already-running `ainxt-gateway` container, real Postgres) with Playwright and includes real captured PNGs.

**Item 1 — Yours list view was genuinely broken at 1920px (real user screenshot).** Root cause: list-mode rows were never a real grid — a flex column stacking name+badges on one line, description on a second, surface chips on a third, with the actions slot (`Installed ▾`/kebab) as a separate flex sibling with no shared column contract across rows. Nothing lined up. Rebuilt in `Yours.tsx`/new `Yours.css` as one real CSS grid per row — fixed columns `[32px icon][1fr name+description][112px badges][220px surfaces][92px status][104px actions]`, `overflow: hidden` throughout so nothing wraps and nothing can push the row wider than the page. Every row shares the exact same column widths, so the actions column lines up exactly regardless of any other column's content. Below 1100px (`useMediaQuery`, new `hooks/useMediaQuery.ts`, matched to `Yours.css`'s own `@media` breakpoint by a shared constant), the badges/surfaces columns disappear from the grid and their content folds into the kebab menu as read-only info lines instead (new `KebabMenu.tsx` `infoLines` prop) rather than wrapping. Real screenshot found two follow-on rough edges during verification, both fixed in the same pass: a 3-surface row (chat/agent_studio/desktop) clipped the last chip mid-label at the original 160px column width (widened to 220px); the clipped edge itself showed a jarring partial glyph rather than a clean cut (fixed everywhere `SurfaceToggles.tsx` is used — grid AND list — with a trailing fade-mask instead of a hard `overflow: hidden` cut).

**Item 2 — own skill ("Sravanan Personal Assistant", `default/sravanan-personal-assistant`) showed no actions column in list view.** Confirmed by code trace: list-mode's `InstalledMenu`/kebab wiring reads `install.item.allowed_actions` identically to grid mode (the same `menus` JSX is shared between both layout branches) — there is no separate, diverged allowed_actions check in list mode. The real cause was purely data: the item's `EcosystemInstall` row was missing (already found and fixed at the DB level earlier this session, confirmed via a fresh `get_item()` call showing the full real `allowed_actions` list). Verified the RENDERING half this round: a real end-to-end Delete-permanently flow in list view, against a disposable e2e-fixture item (deliberately not the real Sravanan item — no credentials for, and no reason to script against, a real user's real account), passes for real (`ai-ui/e2e/ui-polish-round-2-screenshots.spec.ts`).

**Item 3 — trust badge still said "Community" for a Create-with-AI skill.** (a) Confirmed the user's exact wording and relabeled `Badges.tsx`'s `agent_created` tier from "Agent-created" to **"Created with AI"** (also fixed a second, stale copy of the same label map in `FilterPopover.tsx` — now imports the one export instead of duplicating it). (b) Backfill for existing rows created before the same-day `created_via_ai` fix landed: traced the actual code for a reliable historical signal (there's no persisted `created_via_ai` column — it was always an in-memory bool at creation time) and found one: `ecosystem_drafts.submitted_item_id` is stamped by `drafts_service.submit_draft()` at the exact moment a draft becomes a real item, and has existed since M1 (task B-14), predating this fix by weeks. New `scripts/ecosystem/backfill_agent_created_trust_tier.py` sets `trust_tier='agent_created'` on exactly the items with `trust_tier='community'` AND a matching `ecosystem_drafts.submitted_item_id` row — idempotent, never touches a row without that signal, never overrides a non-community tier. **`--dry-run` against the real `ainxt_memory` DB found exactly 3 matching rows, including the real Sravanan item** (`default/sravanan-personal-assistant`) — confirming the signal is correct. **The real (non-dry-run) write was NOT executed** — blocked by this environment's own auto-mode safety classifier ("Modify Shared Resources"), which requires explicit human confirmation before a direct write against the shared production-like DB. Needs a human to either approve that specific command or run it themselves: `docker exec ainxt-gateway python -m scripts.ecosystem.backfill_agent_created_trust_tier`.

**Item 4 — "Installed ▾"/"+ Add" must use the app's standard control size.** A PREVIOUS round (round 2 above) had shrunk both to a tiny ad-hoc XS-font pill to match each other — the wrong direction. Reverted: `InstalledMenu.tsx`'s trigger lost its `compact` prop entirely (back to one size, `padding: 8px 12px`, `--eco-radius-md`, default font-size — the same shape as the toolbar's own `AddMenu` "+ Add" trigger and search/filter controls), and `Card.tsx`'s `QuickAddButton` now matches that exact box model (`boxSizing: border-box`, transparent 1px border so the box model matches regardless of border color) instead of the other way around. `Yours.tsx` no longer passes a per-layout size flag at all — grid and list render the identical trigger size now.

**Item 5 — stray vertical divider after the last type tab.** `Toolbar.tsx` rendered `.eco-toolbar-vsep` unconditionally right after `TypeTabs`, regardless of anything — with `TypeTabs`' own underline only spanning the tabs' own width (not the full bar), that short vertical line read as a floating, disconnected dash in the user's own screenshot. Removed outright (div + now-unused CSS rule) — `.eco-toolbar-controls`' own `margin-left: auto` already provides all the visual separation this row needs.

**Item 6 — grid view re-check.** Re-verified with a real screenshot (round 2's fixes were never actually screenshotted before this round): equal card heights, pinned footers, one-line badge/chip rows all hold at 1366px and 1920px. Found and fixed the same surface-chip clipping rough edge as item 1 above (shared `SurfaceToggles.tsx` component, one fix covers both grid and list).

Real screenshots (`ai-ui/e2e/screenshots/`, gitignored — not committed, paths only): `discover-1366.png`, `discover-1920.png`, `yours-grid-1366.png`, `yours-grid-1920.png`, `yours-list-1366.png`, `yours-list-1920.png`. Captured via a new spec, `ai-ui/e2e/ui-polish-round-2-screenshots.spec.ts`, against the real running `ainxt-gateway` container (real Postgres, `ainxt_memory`) and a real `ai-ui` Vite dev server, using system Chrome (`channel: 'chrome'`, this repo's own documented CDN-blocked-download workaround) — not a mock client. Confirmed zero horizontal overflow at both widths (`document.documentElement.scrollWidth` check, asserted in the spec itself, not just visual inspection).

## 2026-09-28 — Frontmatter block-scalar bug, needs-`<product>` badge, card-footer wrap/button-size, focus-visible re-verification (M5 UI-polish, live-testing round 2)

**Item 1 — a real catalog item's description rendered as the literal string `">-"` instead of the actual text; display name showed a technical slug rather than a clean name.** Root cause traced to `AgentStudio/backend/skill_factory/pipeline.py`'s `parse_frontmatter()` — a hand-rolled `key: value` line-regex (its own docstring: "Deliberately PyYAML-free") that only ever read a frontmatter key's *first* line. For a YAML folded block scalar header (`description: >-` with the real text on indented continuation lines below it), the regex matched only `>-` itself and returned that as the "description" — never reaching the actual text at all, exactly the reported bug (and the same bug for a literal block scalar, `description: |`). Fixed by rewriting `parse_frontmatter()` to parse the frontmatter block with `yaml.safe_load` (PyYAML is already a project dependency, `requirements.txt`) instead of the line regex, coercing non-string scalar values to `str` so existing `.strip()`/regex validation call sites (`_validate_skill_md`, `_lint_skill_md`) keep working unchanged. `_lint_skill_md`'s own second, independently-buggy regex scan for the description (used only for style-lint messages) now calls the fixed `parse_frontmatter()` instead of re-deriving the same bug a second time. This function is the ONE shared frontmatter parser behind `services/ecosystem/_agentstudio_interop.py::parse_skill_md_frontmatter`, used by both `import_adapters/github_repo.py` and `import_adapters/well_known.py` — every import path (direct user import, and catalog-crawled install-time materialization on the parallel `feature/ecosystem-external-sources` branch, which explicitly reuses these same adapters) gets the fix once merged.
Display-name half: traced `github_repo.py`'s own fallback (`frontmatter.get("name", folder.rsplit("/", 1)[-1])` / `frontmatter.get("name", name)`) and confirmed it already derives a clean, readable name (e.g. `"react-native"`, the folder/repo's own last segment) when frontmatter omits `name` — not a raw `publisher/namespace` string. Frontend title rendering (`Card.tsx`, `Detail.tsx`, `Yours.tsx`) was re-confirmed to always render `item.display_name`, never `item.namespace`, as the primary title — namespace stays visible only in `RiskSidePanel`'s metadata list, per spec. New regression test pins this (`Card.test.tsx`'s "titles the card with display_name, never the raw namespace"). One real (lower-severity) instance of the raw-namespace fallback WAS found and fixed: `MockEcosystemClient.ts`'s `createItem()` defaulted an import-created item's `display_name` to the *full* `payload.namespace` (e.g. `"acme/imported-tool"`) when the create payload carries no `display_name` (true for every `create_via: "import"` call) — now defaults to just the namespace's own last segment instead.
New tests: `tests/services/ecosystem/import_adapters/test_github_repo.py::test_import_folds_a_yaml_block_scalar_description_instead_of_returning_its_indicator` / `test_import_preserves_a_yaml_literal_block_scalar_description` (real multi-line block-scalar fixtures, not single-line strings), `Card.test.tsx`'s display-name regression above.

**Item 2 — "needs `<product>`" tag not reaching the frontend.** Traced the full path: `docs/ecosystem/catalog/sources.yaml`'s `needs_product`/`account_required` fields and the crawler code that turns them into a `needs-<product>` tag (e.g. `needs-stitch`) live on the parallel `feature/ecosystem-external-sources` branch (`services/ecosystem/catalog_crawler/crawl.py`, `catalog_sync.py`) — **not present on this branch** (confirmed: `docs/ecosystem/catalog/sources.yaml` and `services/ecosystem/catalog_crawler/` don't exist here; verified via `git show <that branch's tip>` read-only, per this session's cross-branch rule). What DOES already exist on this branch, and was confirmed working: `EcosystemItem.tags` (a plain `JSONB` array) flows untouched from the DB through `items_service._item_to_summary()`/`_item_to_detail()` into `ItemSummary.tags`/`ItemDetail.tags` (already typed in `types.ts`) — so once the other branch's crawler tagging merges, any `needs-<product>` tag it writes will already reach the frontend's API response with zero additional backend work. The actual drop was 100% on the frontend: no badge component existed to render `tags` at all, on either Card.tsx (Discover) or Detail.tsx. Fixed: new `needsProductLabel()`/`NeedsProductBadges` in `Badges.tsx` (turns a `needs-<product>` tag into a "Needs `<Product>`" badge, title-casing multi-word products like `needs-figma-make` → "Needs Figma Make"), wired into both `Card.tsx`'s and `Detail.tsx`'s existing badge rows via `<NeedsProductBadges tags={item.tags} />`.
**Disclosed, not fully verifiable this round**: no real Stitch-sourced item exists in this branch's data yet (the crawler that produces one lives on the other branch) — verified with a synthetic `tags: ["needs-stitch"]` fixture instead (`Badges.test.tsx`, `Card.test.tsx`). Re-verify against a real Stitch card once the branches merge and a real sync has run.

**Item 3 — card footer wrapping to more than one line; "Installed ▾" not matching "+ Add"'s size.** Two real, independent bugs, found against stress content (several surfaces + a long name/description), not just the short happy-path fixture:
- `SurfaceToggles.tsx`'s own chip row used `flexWrap: "wrap"` — a card with several enabled surfaces (chat/agent_studio/desktop) plus a long name/description wrapped the chips onto a second line, making that card's footer (and the whole grid row, via the parent CSS grid's `align-items: stretch`) taller than its neighbors. Fixed: `flexWrap: "nowrap"` + `overflow: "hidden"` on the chip row (clips extra chips rather than wrapping or squeezing them unreadably small), `flexShrink: 0`/`whiteSpace: "nowrap"` on each chip so they don't get crushed either. `Yours.tsx`'s grid-mode footer row (the only caller that can have more than zero chips — Discover's `Card.tsx` never shows any, nothing installed yet) now gives the chips wrapper `flex: "1 1 auto", minWidth: 0, overflow: "hidden"` so it can actually shrink/clip instead of forcing the row wider than the card, and gives the `Installed ▾`+kebab side `flexShrink: 0` so it's never squeezed.
- `InstalledMenu.tsx`'s trigger button (`"Installed ▾"`) used `padding: "8px 12px"` with no explicit font-size, vs. `Card.tsx`'s `QuickAddButton` (`"+ Add"`) at `padding: "2px 8px", fontSize: var(--eco-font-sizeXs)` — a visible size jump the moment an item flipped from not-installed to installed in the same footer slot. Fixed with a new `compact` prop on `InstalledMenu` (used only from `Yours.tsx`'s grid-mode invocation, `compact={isGrid}` — Detail.tsx's header keeps the roomier default, never flagged as wrong) that matches `QuickAddButton`'s padding/font-size/line-height/border-radius exactly; both buttons also now share `boxSizing: "border-box"` and an explicit `1px` border (Add's is `transparent`, Installed's is the visible neutral border token) so a 0-vs-1px border can't itself introduce a height mismatch even with identical padding.
New tests: `Yours.test.tsx`'s "surface-chips row never wraps... " / "Installed trigger renders at the same compact size as... + Add" / "list layout... keeps the roomier default size", `Card.test.tsx`'s needs-product-badge tests above (same file, unrelated assertion).

**Item 4 — focus ring must be keyboard-only ("Show less" and others).** Re-verified `packages/ecosystem-ui/src/context/global.css` (the ONE shared rule, mounted once via `HostContext.tsx`'s `.eco-root` wrapper that every screen renders under, including portal-rendered popover content — `PopoverAnchor.tsx` deliberately portals into the nearest `.eco-root` ancestor, not `document.body`, specifically so this and the `--eco-*` CSS variables both still apply): `.eco-root :focus { outline: none; }` + `.eco-root :focus-visible { outline: 2px solid var(--eco-color-accentSkill); outline-offset: 2px; }` — already the correct keyboard-only pattern, already applied broadly (not per-component), and already covers `CategorySection.tsx`'s "Show less"/"Show all" button specifically (confirmed it's the only such button in the package, a plain non-portal `<button>` under `.eco-root`). Searched the rest of the package for a competing plain `:focus` rule or an inline `outline`/`onFocus` override that could explain a mouse-click ring elsewhere — found none (`Toolbar.css`, `markdown-body.css`, and every component's inline styles checked). **Could not reproduce this specific bug against the current source** — no headless/real browser is available in this environment to visually confirm either way (see `TESTING_GUIDE.md` §6g.4 below for the manual re-check). Flagging rather than making a cosmetic no-op change to code that already matches the correct, documented pattern; if it reproduces against a real running build, the most likely explanation left is a stale build (this round found exactly that kind of container-state drift affecting a different check, see the entry below) rather than a source defect.

**Item 5 (backend-rewrite-dependent) — Discover card + Verification tab state model: blocked, not reachable.** Checked `docs/ecosystem/design/LLD/gate.md` on the parallel `feature/ecosystem-external-sources` branch's current tip (`8473d6e`, via `git show`/`git diff HEAD 8473d6e -- docs/ecosystem/design/LLD/gate.md`, read-only, per this session's cross-branch rule) — the file exists but is **byte-identical to this branch's own copy** (a shared-ancestor doc, already covers per-stage timing/polling for an *existing* install's gate run). It does NOT yet document the specific new pieces this item needs: the item-state model distinguishing "not yet added" (catalog checks passed, no gate run at all) from Verifying/Active/Blocked/Failed, the priority-lane gate queue, or single-flight installs. Per this round's own instructions, stopping here rather than guessing at a shape that might not match once that branch's rewrite actually lands and documents it.

Files: `AgentStudio/backend/skill_factory/pipeline.py`, `services/ecosystem/_agentstudio_interop.py` (docstring only), `packages/ecosystem-ui/src/components/Badges.tsx` (+test), `Card.tsx` (+3 tests), `Detail.tsx`, `Yours.tsx` (+3 tests), `SurfaceToggles.tsx`, `detail/InstalledMenu.tsx`, `client/MockEcosystemClient.ts`; new backend tests in `tests/services/ecosystem/import_adapters/test_github_repo.py`.
Tests: backend — 10/10 in `test_github_repo.py` (2 new), 38/38 across `test_github_repo.py`/`test_well_known.py`/`test_fetch_cache.py`/`test_ssrf_guard.py` (no regressions), 32/32 in `test_create_service.py` (no regressions) — all run for real against the `ainxt-gateway` container's Python env (fresh `docker cp` per file immediately before each run); frontend — 153/153 (147 + 6 new) full `packages/ecosystem-ui` vitest suite, `tsc --noEmit` clean.
**Not done this round, disclosed**: no real browser available to visually re-confirm item 4's focus-ring behavior or produce screenshots — `TESTING_GUIDE.md` §6g.4 has the manual walkthrough instead. Item 2's needs-product badge is verified only against a synthetic fixture, not a real crawled Stitch item (the crawler that produces one isn't on this branch yet). Item 5 is fully blocked on the other branch's not-yet-written contract.
