## 2026-09-28 — Discover-visibility/trust-tier bugs, deprecate/delete-draft event gaps, starter-catalog scope, toolbar/card alignment (M5 UI-parity follow-up round)

**Item 7 — a caller's own private (`org_private`) skill showed in Discover as "Community" with "+ Add", and never counted toward "Created by me".** Two real, independent bugs traced and fixed:
- `services/ecosystem/items_service.py`'s `list_items()` (the Discover query) included `scope='org_private'` items for any caller in the item's own org — meaning a personal Create-with-AI skill was visible to the *whole org's* Discover feed, not just its owner (and, per the caller's report, even the owner saw it mislabeled). Discover is the browse-the-catalog surface; `org_private` is now excluded from it **entirely, for every caller** — never shown to the owner, a same-org teammate, or another org. It still shows up via Yours (the owner's own install, `origin='created'`) and via direct detail/share links (`get_item()`/`_visible_to_caller()`, deliberately unchanged). New regression test proves this for three callers at once (owner, same-org teammate, different org): `test_list_items_excludes_org_private_from_everyones_discover_feed` (`tests/services/ecosystem/test_items_list_get_delete_policy.py`).
- `services/ecosystem/create_service.py`'s `_create_item_and_version()` hardcoded `trust_tier="community"` for every creation path, so a Create-with-AI skill got the same badge as a hand-written one — the `agent_created` tier (`TrustBadge`'s existing "Agent-created" label, `packages/ecosystem-ui/src/components/Badges.tsx`) has existed in the frontend since M4 but nothing on the backend ever set it. New `created_via_ai` parameter threads from `drafts_service.submit_draft()` (the only real Create-with-AI entry point) through `create_via_write()` into `_create_item_and_version()`. `test_submit_draft_tags_the_item_agent_created_not_community` / `test_create_via_write_creates_item_and_version_and_gate_run`'s new assertion cover both sides.
- The "0 in Created by me" half of the report was traced independently and found NOT to be a code bug on the fast-path (private, no-files) creation path — `_auto_install()`/`_is_owner()` already correctly stamp `origin='created'`, confirmed by the existing `test_create_via_write_auto_installs_creator_on_pass` plus this round's own `test_list_items_excludes_org_private_from_everyones_discover_feed` (which asserts the `origin='created'` install row exists). If the real skill in question went through the **full async gate** instead (bundled reference files disable the fast path — see `create_via_write()`'s `attempt_fast_path` check) and the standalone `gate-worker` process wasn't running at the time, the verdict would stay `pending` forever with no install ever created — an operational/infra condition (`CLAUDE.md`'s own "healthy response doesn't guarantee the async write landed" caveat), not a code defect; disclosed rather than papered over.

**Item 9 — "Delete permanently"/Retire re-verified end to end; two real gaps found and fixed.** `InstalledMenu.tsx`/`KebabMenu.tsx`/`Yours.tsx`/`Detail.tsx` wiring itself re-checked against the spec and found correct (owner + no-other-installs → "Delete permanently" from kebab, "Installed ▾", and Detail; shared/installed-elsewhere → "Retire" + a short explanation, no hard delete; server-side `allowed_actions`, never just UI-hidden). Two real bugs found live:
- `routers/ecosystem_router.py`'s `deprecate_item` endpoint 403'd **every** non-admin caller, including the item's own owner — contradicting `compute_allowed_actions()`, which has always offered `deprecate` to `is_owner OR marketplace:admin_sources`. A real owner clicking "Retire" on their own shared item got a 403 despite the UI legitimately showing the button. Fixed via `items_service._is_owner()` (no schema change needed — the endpoint's own comment claiming one was required was stale). New tests: `test_deprecate_endpoint_allows_the_items_own_owner_not_just_admins`, `test_deprecate_endpoint_still_rejects_a_non_owner_non_admin_caller` (`tests/services/ecosystem/test_ecosystem_router_http.py`).
- `items_service.delete_draft()` bulk-deleted the `EcosystemInstall` row directly with a raw query, never going through `installs_service._publish_change()` the way every other install mutation does — so a hard delete never invalidated `resolver_service`'s per-caller capabilities cache or published an `ecosystem.changed` event. A deleted skill kept showing up in chat's skill menu until that cache happened to expire on its own, and no SSE-connected client ever heard about the deletion. Fixed: captures `item_type`/`scope`/`installed_for` before the delete (the row's gone afterward), then calls `invalidate_capabilities_cache()` + `publish_ecosystem_changed(..., change="uninstalled")` (the closest existing `ChangeKind` — CONTRACTS.md §13 has no dedicated "deleted" value) after commit. New test: `test_delete_draft_publishes_an_uninstalled_event_and_invalidates_the_capabilities_cache`.
- Confirmed, not re-fixed: the previously-disclosed "Unshare has no working frontend path" gap (recipient's own `Install`/`ItemSummary` never exposes the sharer's `EcosystemShare.id`) is unchanged — still real, still needs a backend resolve-share-id-from-install-id fix before a frontend "Unshare" button can work. `write_audit_event()` (the audit-log write helper) is confirmed **never called from any real code path yet** (only its own unit test exercises it) — so "keeping audit log entries" on delete is trivially true today (nothing writes them), but the `ecosystem_audit`/`ecosystem_credentials` FK constraints on `ecosystem_items.id` have no `ON DELETE` action at all; the day audit writes actually land, a `delete_draft()` on an audited item will raise a raw `ForeignKeyViolation` instead of failing gracefully. Disclosed, not fixed — out of this round's scope (audit writes aren't wired into any endpoint yet).

**Item 6 — 8 imported starter-catalog skills invisible in Discover.** Root cause: `scripts/ecosystem/admin_import.py`'s `starter` batch called `create_via_import()`, which (like every creation path) hardcoded `scope='org_private', org_id=<importing org>` — directly contradicting the batch's own docstring and `docs/ecosystem/catalog/starter-approved.md`'s description of these 8 skills as "Discover catalog items (community tier, not org-wide auto-provisioned)." An `org_private` item is only ever visible to its own org at best, and (per item 7's fix above) isn't even visible there anymore — so these 8 skills were invisible to **every** org's Discover, including the importing org's own. Fixed: new `catalog_scope` parameter on `create_via_import()` (`"org_private"` default, unchanged for every other caller; `"central_index"` sets `EcosystemItem.org_id=None`, gated behind `marketplace:admin_sources` — the same tier `force_disable()`/`unyank()` already use for "acts on the shared catalog itself"). `admin_import.py`'s `main()` now defaults the built-in `starter` batch to `catalog_scope="central_index"` (a custom `--specs-json` batch keeps the safe `org_private` default unless `--catalog-scope` is passed explicitly — a custom batch might legitimately be a private import). New tests: `test_create_via_import_catalog_scope_central_index_requires_admin_sources_permission`, `test_create_via_import_catalog_scope_central_index_creates_an_org_independent_item`. **Operational note, not a code fix**: any *already*-imported copies of these 8 skills from a prior run of the unfixed script are still `org_private`-scoped rows sitting in whatever org ran the import — re-running `admin_import.py starter` with the fix creates fresh, correctly-scoped rows alongside them; cleaning up the old org_private rows (if any exist in a real deployment) is a one-time manual/DB step, not automated here.

**Item 8 / UI alignment — Toolbar and Card/Yours-grid brought into line with the spec.** Two real Toolbar bugs found reading the code (not from a stale build — see below): (1) the Yours/Discover `ViewSwitch` rendered on the tabs side of a standalone grow-spacer, visually grouping it with the *left* (type tabs) instead of leading the spec's right-aligned cluster ("left: type tabs; right: Yours/Discover switch, search, filter, sort, Grid/List, + Add"); (2) the right-hand controls were loose flex children with plain `flex-wrap`, which has no concept of "wrap as one clean group" — at in-between widths a single control (e.g. Sort) could wrap onto its own line while the rest stayed put, violating the spec's "no partial wrap" rule. Fixed: the whole right cluster is now one `.eco-toolbar-controls` wrapper (`margin-left: auto` to hug the right edge on the shared row above 1280px; `flex-basis: 100%` below 1280px to drop the *entire* cluster to its own full-width second row as one clean unit, `Toolbar.tsx`/`Toolbar.css`). `Card.tsx` (Discover) and `Yours.tsx`'s grid-mode `InstallRow` both had the same three gaps versus the card-anatomy spec, now fixed identically in both: name truncates on one line with a tooltip instead of wrapping; trust/verdict/New/compatibility badges moved to their own single, non-wrapping row directly under the name (previously mixed into the footer row alongside the Add button); footer (surfaces left, Installed ▾ + kebab / "+ Add" right) now pinned to the card's bottom edge via `marginTop: "auto"` on a flex-column card, so a short description no longer leaves the footer floating above a long-description card's footer in the same row (equal-height cards already came for free from the existing CSS grid's default `align-items: stretch`). List-mode `InstallRow` is unchanged — its own single-line-row/column-alignment shape is a separate concern this round didn't touch.
**Item 8's "stale build" half — could not be verified**: the shared `ainxt-gateway` dev container was observed being actively rebuilt/restarted mid-session by unrelated, concurrent work on `feature/ecosystem-external-sources` in the main clone (confirmed: `docker cp`'d files reverted to that other branch's code between test runs, and the container's `State.StartedAt` changed) — there was no stable "currently-running build" to hash-compare against this branch's HEAD. Backend verification for this round instead relied on fresh `docker cp` immediately before each short, targeted `pytest` run (per the standing per-fix-testing convention), never a long-running full-suite invocation against that same shared container.

Files: `services/ecosystem/items_service.py`, `services/ecosystem/create_service.py`, `services/ecosystem/drafts_service.py`, `routers/ecosystem_router.py`, `scripts/ecosystem/admin_import.py`, `packages/ecosystem-ui/src/components/Toolbar.tsx`, `Toolbar.css`, `Card.tsx`, `Yours.tsx`, plus new/extended tests in `tests/services/ecosystem/test_items_list_get_delete_policy.py`, `test_ecosystem_router_http.py`, `test_create_service.py`, `test_drafts_service.py`.
Tests: backend — 95 passing across the four touched ecosystem test files (`test_items_list_get_delete_policy.py`, `test_ecosystem_router_http.py`, `test_create_service.py`, `test_drafts_service.py`), run for real against Postgres inside the `ainxt-gateway` container (fresh `docker cp` immediately before each run, per this round's own contention finding above); frontend — 139 passing (full `packages/ecosystem-ui` vitest suite, no regressions), `tsc --noEmit` clean.
**Not done this round, disclosed**: no real browser screenshots (no headless-browser tool available in this environment) — see `docs/ecosystem/TESTING_GUIDE.md`'s new §6g.3 for the manual walkthrough instead. List-view column alignment (a separate, larger spec item) was not audited this round.

## 2026-09-28 — "Delete permanently"/Retire + Grid/List for Yours (M5 UI-polish round)

**Item 1 — "Delete permanently" for a caller's own private item, everywhere it should appear.** Real bug found: `delete_draft` was already computed correctly server-side (unchanged; confirmed by the pre-existing `test_get_item_returns_full_detail_by_id_and_by_namespace` test) but `InstalledMenu.tsx` (Detail.tsx's and Yours.tsx's shared "Installed ▾" popover) never rendered it at all -- only Yours' kebab menu did, so the single most natural place to see it (the Detail page right after Create-with-AI) never showed it. Fixed:
- `InstalledMenu.tsx` gained `canDeleteDraft`/`hasOtherInstalls`/`canDeprecate` + `onDeletePermanently`/`onRetire`, rendering "Delete permanently" when eligible, or a short note + "Retire" when the item is shared/installed elsewhere instead.
- New `services/ecosystem/items_service.py` field, `has_other_installs`, exposed on `ItemSummary`/`ItemDetail` (`_item_to_summary()`'s own already-computed boolean, previously internal-only) -- the one signal the frontend needs to distinguish "no delete because shared" from "no delete because not owner/built-in/required."
- New `ConfirmDialog.tsx` -- neither `delete_draft` nor `deprecate` had a confirmation step before this; both do now, from either the kebab or "Installed ▾".
- Relabeled `delete_draft`/`deprecate` from "Delete draft"/"Deprecate" to **"Delete permanently"**/**"Retire"** (`KebabMenu.tsx`'s `ACTION_LABELS`).
- **Real, disclosed gap found, not fixed**: "Unshare" (`install.scope == "shared"`, offered to a share's *recipient*) has no working frontend path -- `POST /ecosystem/shares/{share_id}/unshare` needs the *sharer's* own `EcosystemShare.id`, which nothing currently exposes to the recipient. Left unwired (would 404) pending a backend fix.

**Item 2 — Grid/List toggle for Yours.** New toggle in `Toolbar.tsx` (icon-button pair, `Squares2X2Icon`/`ListBulletIcon`), shown only when `view === "yours"`. `Yours.tsx`'s `InstallRow`/`InstallGroup` now take a `layout` prop, restyling the same underlying controls (status chip, surface toggles, "Installed ▾", kebab) into either a card grid (`Card.tsx`'s own visual shell) or the pre-existing list rows -- group section headings unchanged in both. `CatalogScreen.tsx` owns the preference (`localStorage`, key `ecosystem-ui:yours-layout`, default grid) -- a UI choice only, never a server call.

A real bug found and fixed *while building item 1*: the new `useState` for the confirm dialog was initially declared in `Detail.tsx` after that component's early `return`s -- "Rendered more hooks than during the previous render" the moment `item` went from `null` to loaded. Moved above the early returns, alongside every other hook.

Files: `packages/ecosystem-ui/src/components/ConfirmDialog.tsx` (new, +test), `detail/InstalledMenu.tsx`, `KebabMenu.tsx` (+test), `Yours.tsx` (+3 tests), `Detail.tsx`, `Toolbar.tsx` (+2 tests), `CatalogScreen.tsx`, `types.ts`, `client/fixtures.ts`/`MockEcosystemClient.ts`/`Card.stories.tsx`/`detail/EditContent.test.tsx` (new required field), `services/ecosystem/items_service.py` (+2 assertions in existing tests, no logic change).
Tests: frontend 139 passing (130 + 9 new), `tsc --noEmit` clean; backend 46 passing (`test_items_list_get_delete_policy.py`, `test_compute_allowed_actions.py`, `test_ecosystem_security.py`), all run for real (Postgres, inside the `ainxt-gateway` container).

## 2026-09-28 — CI investigation: three real, pre-existing (unrelated to this session) blockers found and fixed on both branches

Requested check: confirm CI on `feature/ainxt-marketplace-backend` (the Skills branch) shows 0 new regressions vs. `scripts/ci/known_failures.txt` after the chat-skills round. It didn't -- Tier 1 was failing outright, meaning **Tier 2 (the actual pytest run) had never once completed since before M4/M5 landed**. Traced through three separate, real, all pre-existing (none caused by this session's chat-skills or external-sources work) blockers, one at a time, fixing each and re-running CI to reveal the next:

1. **`scripts/ci/ecosystem_license_check.py` failing Tier 1** — `@playwright/test@1.63.0` (Apache-2.0, added back at the M5 Playwright E2E commit `328d805`) was never recorded in `compliance/node-components.tsv`/`THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md`. Fixed on both branches.
2. **`docs/ecosystem/openapi.json` stale**, blocking Tier 2's own "Ecosystem OpenAPI/TS-enum contract conformance" step — `routers/ecosystem_router.py` changed at commit `ccf229e` (subdirectory GitHub import, well before this session) without a follow-up `generate_openapi.py` run. Regenerated (purely additive, 678 insertions/3 deletions -- fields/models from several real commits since the spec was last generated). Fixed on both branches.
3. **`tests/core/test_job_queue_kv.py::test_ecosystem_gate_job_id_passes_rqs_own_validation`** imports `rq.job.validate_job_id`, which does not exist in `rq==2.7.0` (the pinned version) -- confirmed directly against the installed package. This test (added at `c868ee6`, also well before this session) always raised `ImportError`, never validating anything, since the moment it was written. Fixed to exercise RQ's real, current validation path (`Job.id`'s own setter) instead. Fixed on both branches.

**Verification method**: reproduced CI's exact Tier 2 command (`pytest tests/auth tests/config tests/core tests/agents tests/store tests/cil tests/db tests/services/ecosystem`) against the real commit, inside the real `ainxt-gateway` container (copied in, dependencies + `ainxt_test` schema applied fresh), then ran `scripts/ci/compare_test_failures.py` for real. Of the resulting "new vs. baseline" failures, five (`tests/config/test_compose_ecosystem_flags.py` x4, `tests/config/test_compose_webchat_documents.py` x1) were confirmed to be `docker compose config` failing with exit 125 specifically because it was invoked from *inside* a container (nested Docker-in-Docker) -- not reproducible on a real GitHub Actions runner, which already proved `docker compose` works natively there (Tier 1's own "docker compose file is valid" step). One (`test_google_auth.py::test_redirect_uri_carries_the_api_prefix`) was confirmed caused by this reproduction container's own ambient local-dev env var (`CONNECTOR_OAUTH_REDIRECT_BASE=http://localhost:5173`) shadowing the test's fixture override -- re-ran with it unset, passed cleanly; a real CI runner never has this var set at all. Neither category is a real regression or a real CI-environment failure -- both are artifacts of this specific reproduction method, disclosed rather than silently reclassified as "known."

Files (both `feature/ainxt-marketplace-backend` and `feature/ecosystem-external-sources`): `compliance/node-components.tsv`, `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md`, `docs/ecosystem/openapi.json`, `tests/core/test_job_queue_kv.py`.

## 2026-09-28 — External sources phase, part 1b: offline signature verification + full sigstore dependency-license audit

Review feedback on part 1, addressed before any real crawl runs:

1. **Offline verification, made real.** `Verifier.production()` (the part-1 default) fetches Sigstore's trust root via a live TUF refresh unless the caller passes `offline=True` -- and even then relies on a *previously cached* refresh, not a guarantee of zero network calls from a cold, air-gapped start. Fixed properly: fetched Sigstore's real production trust root once (`Verifier.production(offline=False)`, this session, from a real network-connected container) and vendored it at `services/ecosystem/catalog_crawler/vendor/sigstore_trusted_root.json`. `verify_index_bytes()` now defaults to `TrustedRoot.from_file()` against that pinned file -- confirmed directly against `sigstore-python` 4.5.0's own `Verifier.verify_artifact()` source that this makes genuinely zero network calls (its `RekorClient` is constructed but never called when the bundle carries its own embedded inclusion proof, which every bundle `sign_index_bytes()` produces does -- grepped the installed package for `self._rekor.` call sites: none). A new `allow_online_trust_root_refresh=True` param is the explicit opt-in for the online path, documented as never the default. `docs/ecosystem/EXTERNAL_SOURCES_PLAN.md` §2 rewritten accordingly; §9's egress-host placeholder filled in for real, by mode, including confirmation that default-mode verification contacts nothing.
2. **Full transitive dependency-license audit for `sigstore==4.5.0`.** Walked the entire installed dependency tree (`pipdeptree`) and checked every package's real license (via `importlib.metadata`'s `License-Expression`/classifiers, or its bundled `LICENSE` file where metadata was blank, e.g. `sigstore-models`): `cryptography` (Apache-2.0 OR BSD-3-Clause), `cffi` (MIT-0), `pycparser`/`idna`/`Pygments` (BSD), `id`/`rfc3161-client`/`rfc8785`/`sigstore-rekor-types`/`sigstore` itself (Apache-2.0), `urllib3`/`platformdirs`/`pydantic`/`pydantic_core`/`annotated-types`/`typing-inspection`/`PyJWT`/`rich`/`markdown-it-py`/`mdurl`/`sigstore-models`/`securesystemslib` (MIT), `pyasn1` (BSD-2-Clause), `pyOpenSSL`/`tuf` (Apache-2.0, `tuf` dual Apache-2.0/MIT), `typing_extensions` (PSF-2.0, already an accepted category in this repo's own `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md` §1.4), `email-validator` (Unlicense), `dnspython` (ISC). `certifi` (MPL-2.0) also appears in the tree but is a **pre-existing** transitive dependency of `requests` (already in `requirements.txt` long before this phase) and already documented in `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md` §1.6 -- not something this change introduces. Every dependency actually new to this phase is MIT/Apache-2.0/BSD/ISC/PSF/Unlicense -- no GPL/AGPL/LGPL anywhere in the tree.
3. **`compliance/node-components.tsv`/`THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md`: unrelated pre-existing gap found and fixed while investigating the Skills branch's CI failure** (see the separate report for that investigation) -- `@playwright/test@1.63.0` (Apache-2.0, added back at M5's `328d805` Playwright E2E commit, months before this session) had never been recorded in the compliance inventory, which is what `scripts/ci/ecosystem_license_check.py` was actually failing on. Fixed on both this branch and `feature/ainxt-marketplace-backend`.

Files: `services/ecosystem/catalog_crawler/signing.py`, `services/ecosystem/catalog_crawler/vendor/sigstore_trusted_root.json` (new, vendored), `tests/services/ecosystem/catalog_crawler/test_signing.py` (+3 tests), `docs/ecosystem/EXTERNAL_SOURCES_PLAN.md`, `docs/ecosystem/design/LLD/external-sources-catalog.md`, `docs/ecosystem/TESTING_GUIDE.md`, `compliance/node-components.tsv`, `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md`.
Tests: 90 passing (87 from part 1 + 3 new), same container, no live network.

## 2026-09-28 — External sources phase, part 1: catalog crawler + adapters + signing (pre stop-point-1)

New phase, branch `feature/ecosystem-external-sources`. `docs/ecosystem/EXTERNAL_SOURCES_PLAN.md` rewritten first, per the phase's own instruction to update the plan before implementing — the design changed from the original proposal's "separate catalog repo" to a pointer-only orphan branch (`ecosystem-index`) of this same repo, no shared history with `main`, signed with Sigstore keyless (CI OIDC) rather than a self-managed minisign key.

Landed this entry (crawler-side only — nothing has actually crawled anything live yet; this is infrastructure, gated by the plan's own **stop point 1**, which requires showing `sources.yaml` for sign-off before any real crawl runs):

1. **`services/ecosystem/catalog_crawler/`** (new package) — `pointer_schema.py` (the `catalog/<item_type>/<publisher>/<name>.yaml` shape + `index/<item_type>.json` sharding), `sources_config.py` (parses the crawl allowlist + `yanked.yaml`), `crawl.py` (orchestrator: reuses the existing adapters' own license verdicts, runs `static_safety_stage.run_fast_path()` as a crawl-time pre-filter, writes pointer files + the index, honors `yanked.yaml`), `crawl_report.py` (the stop-point-2 artifact), `signing.py` (Sigstore keyless sign/verify, lazy-imported).
2. **New adapter, `services/ecosystem/import_adapters/mcp_registry.py`** — official MCP Registry (API v0.1) client; license resolved from the entry's own npm/PyPI package metadata (fetched fresh, never trusted from the registry's free-text fields), or an OCI `licenses` annotation; remote-only servers (no installable package) are excluded pending a human ToS review, never auto-included.
3. **Additive adapter extensions** (existing functions untouched): `github_repo.py` gained `search_repos_by_topic()` (discovery-only, for maintainers proposing new `sources.yaml` entries — not called by the crawler itself); `well_known.py` gained `discover_skills_at_well_known()` (lists every entry at a domain's index with a license verdict, mirroring `discover_skills_in_repo()`'s shape).
4. **New dependency**: `sigstore==4.5.0` (Apache-2.0) — added to `requirements.txt`, `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md` §1.2, `compliance/python-components.tsv`. The pinned version in this doc's own earlier draft (3.6.1) turned out to depend on a since-yanked `rfc3161-client` release; verified the actual installable resolution (4.5.0) inside the real `ainxt-gateway` container before pinning it, and confirmed its `sigstore.oidc`/`sigstore.sign`/`sigstore.models`/`sigstore.verify` API surface matches what `signing.py` calls.

## 2026-09-28 — chat-skills round: index parity, labeled injection, history preservation, input/reply chips (deferred: native tool-calling)

Six-item chat-skills improvement pass. **Deferred, deliberately, not built this round**: making `skill_view`/`read_skill_file` (`mcp/ecosystem_skill_tools.py`'s `ECOSYSTEM_SKILL_TOOLS` schema, already dead code today) real model-callable tools. Checked first — `models/model_router.py`'s `generate()` (the single function both the orchestrator's `generate_answer_tool` and the PIPELINE_V2 fast path call) has no `tools`/`tool_choice` parameter at all; wiring native tool-use through it touches the most shared LLM-calling function in the platform. Explicit decision: ship the other five items now without it; when the MCP/Connectors phase adds real model tool-calling (that phase needs it anyway, for its own external tools), design `model_router`'s tool-use support once, for every tool type together, with its own dedicated blast-radius review — not bolted on here for just these two skill tools.

What shipped instead, so the index is still useful without tool-calling: `render_skill_index()`'s header now explicitly tells the model to *suggest* the exact slash command when a skill clearly fits ("You can use `/name` for this"), never to claim it already applied one it was never given the body of. `ai-ui`'s `Chat.jsx` detects a suggested `/name` in a finished assistant reply that matches an installed skill and renders a clickable "Use <skill>" chip that re-sends the same preceding user message with that skill attached — one click, no retyping, no tool-calling required.

Other four items:
1. **PIPELINE_V2 index parity** (`gateway.py`'s fast-path tail) — `apply_chat_skill_integration()` used to only run when the message was already a recognized slash invocation, so a plain PIPELINE_V2 message never even computed the "## Skills" index (that function sets it as its own first line, unconditionally) — the model never saw which skills existed unless one was already explicitly invoked. Re-gated the same way `agents/orchestrator.py` gates its own unconditional call: directly on `_ECOSYSTEM_CHAT_SKILLS`/mode/surface, not on whether this specific message happens to be a slash command.
2. **Labeled injection block** — a bare `"---"` separator gave the model (and anyone reading raw logs) no signal that what followed was a platform-injected skill body versus more of the user's own text, and no record of which skill/version. Now `[SKILL: <display_name> (<namespace>), version <id>]\nFollow these instructions for this request:\n\n<body>\n\n[USER REQUEST]\n<rest>`.
3. **Stored-history preservation** — `stored_question` (already a dedicated, PII-redacted, pre-skill-mutation variable feeding the main `ainxt.chat_history` produce call and its Kafka-down fallback) already kept the raw typed text out of storage; fixed the one remaining gap in that same pair of call sites — the initial chat *title* was built from the raw un-stripped text, so a skill-invoked first message could title a chat `"/email-tone-polish fix this email..."` verbatim (`routers/chat_router.py`'s `auto_title_chat()` already stripped this for its own, later LLM title regeneration; the inline `title_hint` set at first-message time never did). Also added `chat_messages.skill_used` (JSONB, `db/migrate.py`'s Part AD11) so the "Using skill: X" chip survives a page reload instead of only showing during the live SSE stream — wired through both the real Kafka-consumer path (`workers/kafka_consumer.py`, previously never wrote it) and the Kafka-down direct-Postgres fallback (`gateway.py`'s `_save_chat_messages()`), plus `pipeline/stream_events.py`'s `SkillUsedMarker`/`skill_used_event()` gaining a `version_id` field. **Disclosed, not fixed**: several OTHER, unrelated persistence call sites (voice-mode/KB-grounded branch, doc-job, runtime-canary, edit-message paths — `gateway.py` lines 4704/7817/8769/9045-ish) store `safe_question`/`q.question` directly and were never touched — fixing them safely means auditing each branch's own PII-redaction semantics individually, since `apply_chat_skill_integration`'s `rest` is taken from `original` (pre-redaction), not the PII-masked `safe_question` — a separate, pre-existing PII-redaction subtlety, not caused by this round and not addressed by it.
4. **`skills: [namespace]` wire field** — the chat request now accepts an explicit skill attachment, checked *before* leading `/name` detection (`mcp/ecosystem_skill_tools.py`'s `apply_chat_skill_integration(..., attached_skill=...)`), falling back to slash detection if the attached namespace isn't actually installed. Frontend: picking a skill from the "/" menu or the "+" menu's new "Use a skill" picker attaches it as a colored, removable chip in the input (never plain "/name " text) and on the sent message's own bubble; index cap/ordering is the remaining item, tracked separately below.

Files: `gateway.py`, `agents/orchestrator.py`, `mcp/ecosystem_skill_tools.py`, `pipeline/stream_events.py`, `workers/kafka_consumer.py`, `routers/chat_router.py`, `db/models.py`, `db/migrate.py` (Part AD11), `ai-ui/src/components/Chat.jsx`, `ai-ui/src/components/EcosystemPlusMenu.jsx`.
Tests: targeted suites per item (ecosystem_skill_tools, stream_events, chat_auto_title_slash_strip, gateway_ecosystem_chat_gate_safety, EcosystemPlusMenu.test.jsx) — see the full report for exact pass counts; full-suite + CI comparison run once at the end of this round.
Design docs: this entry.

## 2026-09-28 — object storage hardening + admin_import.py, after a second real bug found during the fork-lock investigation

**Real bug, found while investigating the fork-lock deadlock (entry directly below)**: the 8 real starter-catalog items were created by an ad hoc host-side Python script (run directly on the dev machine, outside any container, against the dockerized Postgres via its published port). `store/ecosystem_object_storage.py`'s `LocalFilesystemEcosystemObjectStorage` defaults to a *relative* path (`storage/ecosystem_objects`), which resolves against whatever the calling process's cwd happens to be — for that host script, the host repo checkout; for the real `gate-worker`/`gateway` containers, `/app` (their fixed `WORKDIR`), which is where `docker-compose.yml`'s `ainxt_uploads` volume is actually mounted. Confirmed directly: the host directory held 2088 files, the container's mounted volume held 43, and the specific object key for one of the 8 items existed only on the host side. `store.get()` inside the gate-worker raises a clean, fast `ObjectNotFoundError` for it (verified via a direct `docker exec` repro) — a real, separate failure mode from the fork-lock hang, just never reached because every gate run for these 8 items was still stuck on the hang first.

**Fix**: `docker-compose.yml` now sets `ECOSYSTEM_OBJECT_STORAGE_LOCAL_DIR` explicitly to the real absolute mount path (`/app/storage/ecosystem_objects`) on both `gateway` and `gate-worker`, removing the cwd-dependency for the two real in-container consumers. `store/ecosystem_object_storage.py`'s new `assert_local_storage_root_is_mounted()` fails fast, before any real work, if (a) the configured/default local-storage dir isn't an absolute path, or (b) `/.dockerenv` isn't present (i.e. the calling process isn't actually inside a container) — a no-op for the `s3`/MinIO backend, which has no cwd-dependent path to get wrong. `scripts/ecosystem/admin_import.py` (new) is now the one supported way to bulk-import catalog items (the starter set today, the later External-sources phase) — it calls this guard first, wraps `create_service.create_via_import()` for a fixed batch, and is meant to be invoked via `docker exec ainxt-gateway python -m scripts.ecosystem.admin_import starter --org-id ... --created-by ...`, never a bare host script again. The 8 contaminated rows (created by the old host-side script) and the stray host `storage/ecosystem_objects/` directory (gitignored, confirmed untracked, 2088 files) were deleted, then re-imported for real via `docker exec ainxt-gateway python -m scripts.ecosystem.admin_import starter --org-id e2e-test-org --created-by <user_id>` against the rebuilt image (also carrying the fork-lock fix below). All 8 gate runs resolved within seconds each this time — no hangs, no fork-lock recurrence: 6 `pass`, 2 `warn` (real upstream content-truncation findings in the source repos at their pinned commits, not a platform bug — see `docs/ecosystem/catalog/starter-approved.md` for the per-skill table and finding detail).

**Disclosed, not fixed (pre-existing, out of scope)**: `scripts/ecosystem/seed_builtin_skills.py` and `scripts/ecosystem/backfill_legacy_items.py` are documented as runnable directly (`python scripts/ecosystem/....py`), with no guard against being run outside a container either — the same latent risk class, just never yet triggered for real. Not touched here (strict-scope rule; these predate this task and aren't part of the starter-catalog work).

Files: `docker-compose.yml` (`ECOSYSTEM_OBJECT_STORAGE_LOCAL_DIR` on `gateway`/`gate-worker`), `store/ecosystem_object_storage.py` (`assert_local_storage_root_is_mounted`), `scripts/ecosystem/admin_import.py` (new), `tests/store/test_ecosystem_object_storage.py` (+4).
Tests: `tests/store/test_ecosystem_object_storage.py`: 13 passed, 2 skipped (Tier-2 MinIO, no endpoint configured). Negative case (`python -m scripts.ecosystem.admin_import ...` run bare on the host) verified directly: raises immediately with a clear message, before any DB/network call.
Design docs: `TESTING_GUIDE.md` §6a/§6a.1.

## 2026-09-28 — real root cause of the gate-worker hangs: fork()+lock deadlock, not GIL/DNS/OOM

**This supersedes the theory in the entry directly below.** After redeploying the sweep-retry/backoff fix (below) and letting the 8 real starter imports retry against it, every single retry hit the exact same 450s `JobTimeoutException` again -- the sweeper was correctly detecting and re-enqueuing, but the underlying hang itself was never actually fixed, just retried repeatedly until `_MAX_SWEEP_ATTEMPTS` gave up.

**Root cause, confirmed with direct evidence, not theorized**: `docker exec`'d into the live container and inspected the two hung work-horse processes via `/proc/<pid>/wchan` and `/proc/<pid>/fd` -- both were blocked in `futex_do_wait` (a userspace lock wait, ruling out network/LLM/DNS hangs outright), and both had **identical open socket inode numbers** despite being forked from two different parent RQ worker processes -- meaning those sockets/connections were opened by their common ancestor (`workers/start_workers.py`'s top-level `--gate` process) before it forked. That process ran two background daemon threads in-process -- `_gate_heartbeat_thread` and `_gate_sweep_thread` (the sweeper this very fix was building on) -- both of which call `SessionLocal()`/query the DB on a 30s cadence, and `core/job_queue.py` additionally opens and `.ping()`s a real Redis connection eagerly at import time. `os.fork()` (which RQ calls internally per job, and which `multiprocessing.Process` also uses to spawn the worker subprocesses themselves) only duplicates the *calling* thread -- if a fork lands at the exact instant one of those background threads holds the DB connection pool's or Redis connection pool's own internal lock, the forked child inherits that lock already acquired *forever*, since the thread that would release it doesn't exist in the child. The very first DB call `run_gate()` makes deadlocks solid -- matching every observed symptom exactly: `"[EcosystemGate] starting gate_run_id=..."` logs, then nothing, `stage_timings` never populated (not even stage 1), and RQ's blunt 450s job timeout as the only thing that ever kills it. Reproduced deterministically in `tests/services/ecosystem/test_gate_health_service.py::test_child_process_can_use_the_db_after_fork_while_the_pools_own_lock_is_held` (holds the pool's own `util.queue.Queue.mutex`, forks, proves the child hangs without the fix below and succeeds with it -- verified both ways on real Linux, not just Windows/skipped).

A second, independent bug was found and fixed alongside this: the 8 real starter-catalog items were created by a host-side script (outside any container), so their object bytes landed in the host's `storage/ecosystem_objects/` directory, not the `ainxt_uploads` Docker volume the gate-worker container actually reads from (`store.get()` raises `ObjectNotFoundError` for all 8, confirmed by direct repro) -- see the "object storage hardening" entry below for the fix; the 8 items were deleted and will be re-imported through the new admin-import command once both fixes are deployed.

**Fix (architecture, not another retry knob)**:
1. `workers/start_workers.py`: removed `_gate_heartbeat_thread`/`_start_gate_heartbeat` and `_gate_sweep_thread`/`_start_gate_sweep` entirely from the `--gate` process. That process now does nothing but warm up gate modules and fork RQ work-horses -- no threads, no pre-fork DB/Redis connections of its own.
2. Liveness: `core/job_queue.py`'s new `get_queue_worker_liveness(queue_name)` reads RQ's *own* worker registry (`rq.Worker.all()`, which RQ itself keeps alive with its own heartbeat/TTL -- no thread of ours needed) instead of a hand-rolled Redis heartbeat key. `services/ecosystem/gate_health_service.py`'s `get_health()` now calls this; `record_heartbeat()`/`_HEARTBEAT_KEY` removed.
3. Sweeping: `_run_gate_sweeper_loop()` (renamed from `_gate_sweep_thread`, same logic) now runs as the entire body of its own dedicated process — a new, additive `docker-compose.yml` service, `gate-sweeper` (`python workers/start_workers.py --gate-sweeper`, same image, no Docker socket, no sandbox access) — never sharing a process with anything that forks. `--gate` and `--gate-sweeper` are deliberately mutually exclusive modes.
4. Defense in depth, in case anything else ever opens a connection pre-fork again: `db/database.py` and `core/kv/queue.py` each register a process-wide `os.register_at_fork(after_in_child=...)` hook (POSIX-only, guarded) that disposes/resets the SQLAlchemy engines' pools (`engine.dispose(close=False)`, SQLAlchemy's own documented pattern) and the shared Redis connection's pool (`ConnectionPool.reset()`, redis-py's own documented pattern) in every forked child, unconditionally. This covers *any* future fork in the process (RQ's per-job fork included) with one registration each, not just the two threads removed above.

Files: `workers/start_workers.py`, `core/job_queue.py` (`get_queue_worker_liveness`), `services/ecosystem/gate_health_service.py`, `db/database.py`, `core/kv/queue.py`, `docker-compose.yml` (new `gate-sweeper` service), `CLAUDE.md` (compose services list), `tests/services/ecosystem/test_gate_health_service.py` (+7: RQ-registry liveness healthy/unhealthy, real Worker register_birth/death round-trip, sweeper-loop-in-its-own-process re-enqueues, and the deterministic fork+lock deadlock reproduction, verified both failing-without-the-fix and passing-with-it on real Linux).
Tests: `tests/services/ecosystem/test_gate_health_service.py`: 15 passed (was 12; 3 heartbeat-based tests replaced, 4 new added net +3 after replacements — see file). Full suite re-run at the end of this round — see the final entry for this task.
Design docs: `LLD/gate.md` (liveness + sweeper process separation), `TESTING_GUIDE.md` (new `gate-sweeper` service, fork-lock regression test).

## 2026-09-28 — gate-worker resilience: sweep retry/backoff + max-attempts, after a real work-horse kill

**Real incident**: while running the starter-catalog's 8 real imports, 2 of the corresponding gate-worker RQ jobs died mid-run with "Work-horse terminated unexpectedly; waitpid returned None" -- RQ's own signature for a job whose forked child process disappeared, not a clean Python exception. `dmesg` on the host kernel showed **zero OOM-killer activity** at all, ruling out a genuine memory-exhaustion kill. Several OTHER unrelated gate runs independently hit RQ's own job-level 450s `JobTimeoutException` around the same time, all with tracebacks landing in unrelated SQLAlchemy mapper-configuration code -- the classic deferred-SIGALRM-delivery symptom, not evidence of where time was actually spent. `ethics_stage.py` is the only gate stage that makes a real model call (`models.model_router.model_router.generate()`); `gate_service.py`'s `_run_stage_with_timeout()` bounds it with a `ThreadPoolExecutor` + `future.result(timeout=60)`, but that mechanism's own docstring already discloses the real limitation: Python cannot forcibly kill a running thread, so an abandoned stage thread keeps running in the background past its own timeout and can later crash the process touching a resource the main thread has since moved past.

The existing stuck-run sweeper (`gate_health_service.sweep_stuck_gate_runs()`, task B-6) DID eventually catch and re-enqueue both orphaned runs, but with no attempt counter and no backoff -- a row whose underlying cause keeps recurring would be re-enqueued forever on a fixed cadence, with no signal to an admin that something was actually broken versus "still verifying, almost done."

**Fix**: `ecosystem_gate_runs.sweep_attempts` (new column, `db/migrate.py` Part AD10) tracks how many times a row has been swept. `_resweep_cooldown_for(attempts)` doubles the re-sweep cooldown per attempt (capped at 1 hour) instead of a flat 600s forever. Past `_MAX_SWEEP_ATTEMPTS` (3), the sweeper stops retrying and calls `_mark_permanently_failed()`, which resolves the run to a real `verdict="fail"` with a clear `GATE_WORKER_INTERRUPTED` finding (mirroring `run_gate()`'s own end-of-run shape exactly, so every existing UI/API consumer already knows how to render it) -- an admin sees an actionable failure, never an item silently stuck in "Verifying..." forever. `gate_service.py`'s `_run_stage_with_timeout()` docstring now explicitly states what it can and cannot protect against, pointing at this sweeper as the layer that actually handles an out-of-process kill.

Concurrency: `docker-compose.yml`'s existing `python workers/start_workers.py --gate --n 2` already runs exactly 2 gate-worker subprocesses, each an RQ `Worker` that processes one job at a time by default -- i.e. already at "1-2 concurrent gate jobs system-wide," matching the requested default; no change needed there. `sandbox_stage.py`'s Docker sandbox execution only ever runs when a skill declares a `test` entrypoint in its manifest (none of this round's imports do), so it wasn't a factor in this incident.

Files: `db/migrate.py` (Part AD10), `db/models.py` (`EcosystemGateRun.sweep_attempts`), `services/ecosystem/gate_health_service.py`, `services/ecosystem/gate_service.py` (docstring only), `tests/services/ecosystem/test_gate_health_service.py` (+4: killed-work-horse RQ status re-enqueues correctly, backoff grows exponentially, a row not yet due for its own backed-off cooldown is skipped, exceeding max attempts marks the run permanently failed with the real finding row).
Tests: `tests/services/ecosystem/test_gate_health_service.py`: 12 passed. Full `tests/services/ecosystem/` suite: 409 passed.
Migration applied to both `ainxt_test` and the real `ainxt_memory` database live (column confirmed present via `information_schema.columns` on the real DB). **Unrelated, pre-existing finding from the same real-DB migration run**: two later, unrelated migration parts (`conversations.user_id`/`conversations.rag_mode` column adds, `db/migrate.py`'s Part Q1/S26) hit `psycopg2.errors.QueryCanceled: canceling statement due to statement timeout` against the live `conversations` table -- reported here per file:line, not fixed (unrelated to the ecosystem initiative, and altering statement-timeout/table-migration strategy for a large, unrelated production table needs its own review).
Design docs: none dedicated -- see this entry.

## 2026-09-28 — compatibility tag ("works in chat" vs "needs file/terminal tools")

Explicit review request: every skill needs a visible signal for whether its own instructions can be followed purely through a chat conversation, or assume shell/git/file-edit access a chat-only surface can't give an agent.

`services/ecosystem/compatibility.py` (new): `classify_compatibility(instructions)` — a text-pattern heuristic (fenced shell/terminal code blocks, `git <verb>` mentions, file-edit imperatives, CLI/package-install phrases) returning `"chat"` or `"tool_dependent"`; disclosed as a heuristic, not a guarantee, same tradeoff every other classifier in this codebase already accepts. `default_surfaces_for(compatibility, has_tools=False)` and `enforce_compatibility_on_surfaces(compatibility, surfaces)` implement the actual policy: chat-compatible defaults to every surface; tool-dependent defaults to Cowork+Desktop (+Agent Studio if the item has tools) and can never be handed the `chat` surface even if a caller explicitly asks for it.

Wired into `services/ecosystem/create_service.py`'s `_create_item_and_version()` — the one choke point every creation path (write/upload/import) already passes through — computed from `manifest["instructions"]`, stored back onto the manifest itself (no schema migration; manifest is already an arbitrary JSONB blob every version reader already parses), and returned in the create/import result dict. `services/ecosystem/items_service.py`'s `_item_to_summary()` surfaces it as `compatibility` on `ItemSummaryModel`/`ItemDetailModel` (same model, list and detail). `packages/ecosystem-ui`: `Badges.tsx`'s new `CompatibilityBadge` shown on both `Card.tsx` and `Detail.tsx`'s header.

Files: `services/ecosystem/compatibility.py` (new), `services/ecosystem/create_service.py`, `services/ecosystem/items_service.py`, `routers/ecosystem_router.py`, `packages/ecosystem-ui/src/types.ts`, `packages/ecosystem-ui/src/components/Badges.tsx`, `Card.tsx`, `Detail.tsx`, plus fixture updates (`client/fixtures.ts`, `client/MockEcosystemClient.ts`, `Card.stories.tsx`, `detail/EditContent.test.tsx`) for the new required field.
Tests: `tests/services/ecosystem/test_compatibility.py` (new, 12), full `tests/services/ecosystem/` suite re-run: 392 passed (no regressions from the surfaces-defaulting change). `packages/ecosystem-ui`: `tsc --noEmit` clean, `vitest run`: 126/127 + 8/8 on a targeted re-run of the one test that failed in the full parallel run (confirmed pre-existing timing flake, unrelated file, untouched by this change) — effectively all green.
Design docs: none dedicated — this is a cross-cutting creation-path addition, not a new component; see this entry as the record.

## 2026-09-28 — well_known import adapter rewritten against the real Agent Skills Discovery format

**Finding, live**: `services/ecosystem/import_adapters/well_known.py`'s original index shape (`slug`/`download_url`/`sha256`/`license` fields per entry) was this module's own invented guess, disclosed as such in its own header comment at the time. Testing it live against the two real sites that actually publish a well-known skill index (docs.x.com, supabase.com) — per explicit review request — showed neither matches it. Both implement the real, published standard instead: `https://schemas.agentskills.io/discovery/0.2.0/schema.json` (`{"$schema", "skills": [{"name", "type": "skill-md"|"archive", "description", "url", "digest": "sha256:<hex>"}]}`).

**Fix**: rewritten against the real format. Modern index tried first; falls back to the legacy path (`/.well-known/skills/index.json` — also real and live, a genuinely different, simpler shape docs.x.com serves alongside the modern one: bare `name`/`description`/`files[]`, no url/digest at all). Relative `url` values resolved against the domain's own origin; redirects followed and re-validated through the SSRF guard on every hop (real skill archives are GitHub release assets, which always 302 through `objects.githubusercontent.com` — `connectors/net_relay.py`'s shared `relay_request()` doesn't follow redirects itself, and that widely-used relay function wasn't touched). `type: "archive"` now supported: safe `.tar.gz` extraction (SKILL.md must sit at the root; symlinks/hardlinks/devices never extracted; path-traversal member names rejected; per-file and total-uncompressed size caps enforced against the untrusted tar header). The digest is verified over the exact fetched bytes in both formats. The license is read from the fetched SKILL.md's own `license:` field — the real index format has no license field at all, unlike the original code's assumption — missing or non-MIT/Apache-2.0 excludes the skill under tier 1, same fail-closed rule as every other adapter.

Verified live after the fix: docs.x.com's one real skill correctly excluded (its real SKILL.md has no `license:` field); supabase.com's `supabase-postgres-best-practices` (a real "archive" entry, a GitHub release tarball) imported end-to-end for real — MIT, digest verified, real bundled reference files extracted; supabase.com's other entry correctly excluded (no license field); mintlify.com confirmed to have no index at all (followed its own redirect chain to an ordinary 404-shaped page).

Files: `services/ecosystem/import_adapters/well_known.py`, `tests/services/ecosystem/import_adapters/test_well_known.py` (rewritten, 7 → 17 tests, several using fixtures recorded from the real live responses).
Tests: `tests/services/ecosystem/import_adapters/` full suite: 49 passed. Live re-verification against docs.x.com/supabase.com/mintlify.com performed and matches the test suite's expectations.
Design docs: `LLD/external-import.md`.

## 2026-09-28 — starter-catalog license policy: field-absent no longer means excluded

**Finding, live**: running the just-landed `discover_skills_in_repo()` against a real, MIT-licensed 25-skill GitHub collection (found via a live, read-only topic search, per explicit review) returned zero allowed candidates — not because any skill was actually mis-licensed, but because none of the 25 `SKILL.md` files carried a per-skill `license:` frontmatter field, and the previous AND-only rule (repo license AND skill field must BOTH independently pass) treated a missing field as an automatic fail. Real-world publishers overwhelmingly rely on one repo-level LICENSE file rather than redundant per-file declarations, so this would have made the strict rule reject nearly everything real.

**Decision**: `discover_skills_in_repo`/`import_from_github_path` (only — `import_from_github`'s original root-only path is untouched, kept at its stricter AND-only rule since it's the older, already-shipped path with its own test coverage) switch to an inheritance chain, first-found-wins: the SKILL.md's own `license:` field, then the nearest LICENSE file in the skill's own folder or an enclosing folder, then the repo-root SPDX license. A present field is authoritative even when it's wrong. Real conflicts inside the skill's own folder still exclude it regardless: a LICENSE/COPYING/NOTICE file that concretely guesses to a different disallowed family, or a bundled file's own `SPDX-License-Identifier:` header naming one — both capped to bound extra API calls under GitHub's rate limit.

Files: `services/ecosystem/import_adapters/github_repo.py`, `tests/services/ecosystem/import_adapters/test_github_repo_discovery.py` (+4 tests, 2 rewritten to isolate the folder/repo-fallback signal now that a default "MIT" fixture field would otherwise short-circuit them).
Tests: `tests/services/ecosystem/import_adapters/` full suite: 39 passed.
Design docs: `LLD/external-import.md`.

## 2026-09-27 — chat-skill safety scoping + two live bugs found and fixed while verifying it

**Safety scoping (`d48aac2`)**: the CIL ambiguity-clarification gate's skill-invocation bypass and the `PIPELINE_V2` fast-path tail's skill injection (both landed the same day, `ed8f4c7`/`a7240ef`) originally matched on `^/\S+` alone — any slash-shaped text, not just a real installed skill. Per review, this was too broad: a typo, an unrelated slash reference, or free text starting with `/` for some other reason must still go through the CIL gate exactly as before. `mcp/ecosystem_skill_tools.py` gained `matches_installed_skill_slash_command(question, *, org_id, user_id, surface)` — fail-closed-to-`False` — checking real membership via `get_effective_capabilities()`/`build_slash_command_lookup()`, not just syntax. `gateway.py`'s `_is_real_skill_invocation` (replacing the old `_looks_like_skill_invocation`) is computed once and reused by both the CIL gate and the fast-path injection. Flag-off byte-identical behavior is guarded by a new source-level structural test (`tests/test_gateway_ecosystem_chat_gate_safety.py`) rather than a runtime one, since `gateway.py` can't be imported in CI (HSM boot at import time).

**Bug 1 — the fast-path chip wrapper hung every skill-invoking chat message forever (`0994f52`)**: found live while capturing item 2's screenshot. `_general_stream_with_skill_chip()`'s closure referenced the free variable `_underlying_general_stream`, which the very next statement reassigned to the wrapper itself — a classic late-binding closure bug. Once actually iterated, the wrapper's `async for` looked itself up and tried to iterate itself, hanging with no error and no timeout (confirmed via the container's own log: the skill resolved and injected correctly, then total silence for 100+ seconds, while background cron jobs kept firing fine — only that one request's task was stuck). Fixed by capturing the original generator under a never-reassigned name, `_fp_source_stream`. Regression test extracts the real, current closure via AST from `gateway.py`'s own source and drives it with a fake multi-item stream under `asyncio.wait_for`, proving every item forwards instead of hanging.

**Bug 2 — the "Using skill" chip never survived to the rendered message (`9e3484c`)**: a separate, pre-existing bug in `ai-ui/src/components/Chat.jsx` from the original chip-wiring round. The SSE loop's `skill_used` handler set `msg.skillUsed` via `updateMessages(newMessages.map(...))`, but every one of these calls maps over the SAME frozen `newMessages` snapshot captured before streaming began — intermediate React state updates never feed back into it. The final `__meta__`-triggered update does the same map over that stale snapshot and lists dozens of explicit fields to carry forward, but never `skillUsed` — silently discarding the chip on every single request, on both the orchestrator and fast-path lanes (both share this loop). Fixed by tracking `skillUsedMeta` as a local variable (same pattern as `modelLabel`/`toolEvents`) and including it in the final update object. Verified live via Playwright against the real running UI — screenshot at `docs/ecosystem/ui-parity-screenshots/chat-skill-chip-light.png`.

**Bonus — OS dark-mode media query broke Marketplace legibility (`45351c6`)**: found while scoping the item-6 screenshot set. `ai-ui/tailwind.config.js` never set `darkMode`, so Tailwind defaulted to the `'media'` strategy; a handful of pre-existing `dark:` utility classes elsewhere in the bundle then activated under `prefers-color-scheme: dark` even though ai-ui has no real dark theme (`packages/ecosystem-ui/src/theme.ts`'s own comment; `color-scheme: light` is hardcoded in `ai-ui/src/index.css`) — some text went light-gray on an unchanged white background, confirmed illegible in an existing screenshot from an earlier round (`impl-yours-dark.png`). Fixed with `darkMode: "class"`, which makes the existing `dark:` classes inert until something actually adds a `.dark` class to the tree (nothing does yet) — this removes the OS-driven breakage without adding or claiming a real dark theme. `packages/ecosystem-ui` itself was never the source (no Tailwind config, no `dark:` classes, and its own token system only changes via host-supplied tokens, never on its own).

Files: `gateway.py`, `mcp/ecosystem_skill_tools.py`, `ai-ui/src/components/Chat.jsx`, `ai-ui/tailwind.config.js`, `tests/test_gateway_ecosystem_chat_gate_safety.py` (new), `tests/services/ecosystem/test_ecosystem_skill_tools.py`.
Tests: 33 passed across `tests/test_gateway_ecosystem_chat_gate_safety.py` + `tests/services/ecosystem/test_ecosystem_skill_tools.py` + `tests/services/ecosystem/test_orchestrator_ecosystem_chat_skills.py` (isolated `ainxt_test` DB). Both live bugs additionally verified end-to-end against a real rebuilt gateway container and the real ai-ui dev server (real e2e test user, real Anthropic model call, real screenshots).
Design docs: `LLD/chat-runtime.md`, `TESTING_GUIDE.md`.

## 2026-09-27 — starter-catalog decision: GitHub import adapter, subdirectory discovery

Explicit decision (overriding `EXTERNAL_SOURCES_PLAN.md`'s original recommendation to keep the instance-side adapter root-only and centralize subdirectory-walking in a future catalog crawler): extend `github_repo.py` directly, since most real-world skill publishers ship one repo per collection rather than one repo per skill, and the starter catalog needs this now rather than after a whole new crawler subsystem exists.

`discover_skills_in_repo(repo, ref=None, path=None)` (`services/ecosystem/import_adapters/github_repo.py`) walks a repo's full git tree (GitHub's recursive trees API, optionally scoped under `path`) and returns one candidate per discovered `SKILL.md`, each with its own license evidence and allow/deny verdict — nothing is imported by this call, so an admin can review the full candidate list (including excluded ones and why) before importing anything. Per-skill license precedence: a `LICENSE`/`LICENSE.md`/`LICENSE.txt` file directly in the skill's own folder wins if present, otherwise the repo-level SPDX license is the fallback; either way the SKILL.md's own `license:` field must independently also be MIT/Apache-2.0. `import_from_github_path(repo, path, ref=None)` then imports one chosen candidate, pinned to the resolved commit sha, bundling every other file in that folder (not just SKILL.md, unlike the root-only `import_from_github`). Guards: `path` rejected outright on any `..`/absolute/backslash segment before any network call; every tree entry re-validated the same way regardless of source; a truncated tree response is a hard error, not a silent partial scan; discovery capped at 20,000 tree entries / 200 discovered `SKILL.md` files; bundled files capped at 64KB/file, 8MB/folder (mirroring `create_service.py`'s own upload-path constants). `import_from_github` (root-only) is untouched — both new functions are additive.

Not yet wired into `create_via_import()` or any router endpoint — that "browse and pick" admin flow is starter-catalog follow-up work, not part of this task.

Files: `services/ecosystem/import_adapters/github_repo.py`, `tests/services/ecosystem/import_adapters/test_github_repo_discovery.py` (new, 9 tests), `docs/ecosystem/EXTERNAL_SOURCES_PLAN.md`.
Tests, all real, no live network (`connectors.net_relay.relay_request` monkeypatched): new file's 9 tests + the existing `test_github_repo.py`'s 8 unmodified tests, run together: `tests/services/ecosystem/import_adapters/` 35 passed.
Design docs: `LLD/external-import.md`, `EXTERNAL_SOURCES_PLAN.md` §(GitHub repos row).

## 2026-09-27 — gate-worker cold-start warmup + build-info visibility

Two related "stale/slow infra, no visibility" fixes from the same round.

**Cold-start hang**: even with the same-day per-stage-timeout fix in place (`LLD/gate.md`'s own entry), a freshly-started gate-worker's *first* job could still hang for RQ's blunt job-level timeout — the real cold-import cost of `run_gate()`'s dependency graph (including `sandbox_stage.py`'s deliberately lazy `sandbox.ecosystem_gate_executor`/`docker` SDK import) was being paid *during* that first job's own timeout window, before any per-stage timeout ever got a chance to run. `workers/start_workers.py`'s new `_warmup_gate_modules()` pre-imports everything once, synchronously, before the worker (parent process, and each spawned subprocess) starts accepting jobs or reporting healthy via the heartbeat thread — measured ~44s cold on a dev machine, moved entirely to startup. `core/job_queue.py`'s job-level `timeout` (`enqueue_ecosystem_gate_job()`) was bumped `300 → 450`, since the old value was *less* than the sum of the per-stage timeouts (390s worst case) — a true last-resort backstop now, not something that could fire before the per-stage mechanism got a chance.

**Build-info visibility**: the user was testing that same day against a 15-hour-stale image with no way to tell from the running app. `GIT_COMMIT`/`BUILD_TIME` are now baked into the image as env vars at build time (`Dockerfile`'s runtime stage; `docker-compose.yml`'s `gateway` service `build.args`, defaulting to `"unknown"` if not set — `.git/` is excluded from the build context via `.dockerignore`, so this is the only way a running container can report what it's actually running). `core/build_info.py`'s `get_build_info()` reads them; `GET /ecosystem/config`'s new `build_info` field surfaces `{commit, built_at}` admin-only (`marketplace:provision`, same signal `caller_permissions.can_provision` already uses — `null` for anyone else, never sent). Shown in the Marketplace admin nav (`AdminScreen.tsx`, `data-testid="admin-build-info"`).

Files: `workers/start_workers.py`, `core/job_queue.py`, `core/build_info.py` (new), `docker-compose.yml`, `Dockerfile`, `services/ecosystem/config_service.py`, `routers/ecosystem_router.py`, `packages/ecosystem-ui/src/types.ts`, `packages/ecosystem-ui/src/components/admin/AdminScreen.tsx`.
Tests, all real: `tests/core/test_gate_worker_warmup.py` (new, 3 — including a genuinely cold subprocess timed end to end), `tests/services/ecosystem/test_config_service.py` (+2, build_info admin-gating), `AdminScreen.test.tsx` (+2). Full `services/ecosystem/gate` + related suites re-run together: 72 passed, 1 skipped (a test-order-dependent isolation check, correctly self-skips rather than false-failing when run after another test already imported the same module). Full `ecosystem-ui` package suite: 127/127. `tsc --noEmit` clean.
Design docs: `LLD/gate.md`, `TESTING_GUIDE.md` §§11-12.

## 2026-09-27 — chat titles stripped a leading "/skill-name"; "Using skill" chip frontend confirmed correct; uninstall-empty-state E2E rewritten for the real per-group rule

**Chat title bug**: a chat opened via `/skill-name rest of the message` auto-titled itself starting with the literal slash-command token, since `routers/chat_router.py`'s `auto_title_chat()` fed the raw first user message straight into its title-generation prompt. Added `_strip_leading_slash_command()` (a small, standalone regex helper, `^/\S+\s*`) and applied it to `first_q` before building the prompt, falling back to the original text if stripping leaves nothing (a bare `/skill-name` with no trailing text). 5 new unit tests in `tests/test_chat_auto_title_slash_strip.py`.

**"Using skill" chip**: traced the full chain (backend `SkillUsedMarker` → `gateway.py`'s `{"skill_used": {...}}` SSE frame → `Chat.jsx`'s `obj.skill_used !== undefined` handler setting `msg.skillUsed` → `<MessageMeta msg={msg}>` → `SkillUsedChip`) end to end — no bug found in this half. `MessageMeta.test.jsx` already covers the render half directly (given `msg.skillUsed`, the chip renders with the right name). Whether the BACKEND resolution itself fires for a real chat request is a separate, ongoing investigation (the coordinator's own — `gateway.py`'s `ecosystem_surface` derivation), not touched here.

**`uninstall-empty-state.spec.ts` rewritten**: the old version asserted uninstalling the caller's only item shows Yours' *global* empty state — false now that the 4 builtin skills auto-(re)provision for every org member the moment anything calls `config_service.get_effective_config()` (which `ensure_provisioned()` lives inside). Rewritten to the real rule: uninstalling the caller's own "Created by me" item empties only that group (no row remains under that group's label), "Org provisioned" keeps its builtins completely unaffected, and the global `yours-empty-state` never renders while any group still has rows. Passes for real against the live dev stack (8.5s).

Files: `routers/chat_router.py`, `tests/test_chat_auto_title_slash_strip.py` (new), `ai-ui/e2e/uninstall-empty-state.spec.ts`.

## 2026-09-27 — item 7: skill-usage proof — structured logs + a "Using skill: ‹name›" chat chip

Live-diagnosing whether a real `/name ...` chat invocation actually resolved and used a skill was impossible before this: `mcp/ecosystem_skill_tools.py`'s `apply_chat_skill_integration()` had zero logging on its success path (only a `logger.warning` on exception, which it's designed to swallow so an ecosystem lookup failure never breaks the live chat path). Checking `docker logs` for a real user's real attempt this session found nothing at all, either way — genuinely unknowable from logs, not just under-logged.

Added exactly 3 structured, content-free log lines (`core.logger.logger.info`, matching this codebase's own `TAG → key=value` convention): `ECOSYSTEM_SKILL_RESOLVED` (name/version/surface, before content is ever fetched), `ECOSYSTEM_SKILL_INJECTED_AS_USER_MESSAGE` (name only, right after `state.question`/`raw_question` are rewritten), and `ECOSYSTEM_SKILL_VIEW`/`ECOSYSTEM_READ_SKILL_FILE` (inside `skill_view()`/`read_skill_file()` themselves, name/version/[path]/surface) — **never the skill's own instructions body or file content**, per the explicit "no content, no secrets" requirement.

Separately, the chat UI had no visible indication a skill was ever applied to a turn. `apply_chat_skill_integration()` now also sets `state.metadata["ecosystem_skill_used"]`; `agents/orchestrator.py`'s `run()` yields a new typed sentinel (`pipeline.stream_events.SkillUsedMarker`, the same str-safe pattern as the existing `ToolMarker`/`ReasoningMarker`) when it's set; `gateway.py`'s Phase-5 SSE loop translates it to a real `{"skill_used": {"name", "display_name"}}` frame (unconditionally — unlike tool/reasoning events, not gated behind `_PIPELINE_V2_STREAM`, since this is a simple always-relevant signal); `ai-ui/src/components/Chat.jsx` stores it on the message; `MessageMeta.jsx` renders a small emerald "Using skill: ‹name›" chip (`SkillUsedChip`, `lucide-react`'s `Sparkles` — this file predates the Ecosystem initiative and stays on `lucide-react`, matching its own existing convention, not `@heroicons/react`).

Live-diagnosis finding, disclosed rather than guessed at: the specific real DB row backing the user's own earlier `/email-tone-polish` attempt showed a real, `enabled=true`, `surfaces` including `chat` install already existed for that account at the time — so the slash command most likely *did* resolve correctly; there was simply no log evidence either way until now, which is exactly the gap this closes.

**Tests, run for real against the isolated `ainxt_test` database**: `tests/services/ecosystem/test_ecosystem_skill_tools.py` gained 2 tests, both patching `core.logger.logger.info` directly (structlog, not stdlib — no existing `caplog` convention in this suite to follow) and asserting a distinctive marker string lands in the real return value/question but never in a captured log call. `ai-ui/src/components/MessageMeta.test.jsx` (new, 4 tests) covers the chip's render/no-render conditions. `tests/services/ecosystem/test_orchestrator_ecosystem_chat_skills.py`'s existing 6 tests re-run clean (22 passed total across both files) — the new yield only ever fires when a skill was actually resolved, so every pre-existing flag-off/no-match case is unaffected.

Files: `mcp/ecosystem_skill_tools.py`, `agents/orchestrator.py`, `pipeline/stream_events.py`, `gateway.py`, `ai-ui/src/components/Chat.jsx`, `ai-ui/src/components/MessageMeta.jsx`, `ai-ui/src/components/MessageMeta.test.jsx` (new).
Design docs: `docs/ecosystem/design/LLD/chat-runtime.md`.

## 2026-09-27 — items 6+8: gate stages get real timeouts + a live-progress Verification tab; the stuck-item incident's real root cause

**Root cause of "a skill I just added is stuck on Verifying for a long time"**: the `ainxt-gate-worker` Docker container had been running for 15 hours on an image that predates every gate-related fix from today (B-6's sweeper, the `ecosystem_gate_job_id()` colon→underscore fix, task C, task D) — confirmed via `docker exec ainxt-gate-worker` grepping for that code inside the running image and finding none of it. It could still process some jobs from the shared queue but had no sweeper to recover a stuck one, and two jobs it did pick up died with `rq.timeouts.JobTimeoutException` (RQ's own blunt 300s job-level default — the traceback is misleading, showing the deferred `SIGALRM` landing inside an unrelated `import httpx` frame rather than wherever the real 300s was actually spent, almost certainly the ethics stage's LLM call or the sandbox stage's Docker interaction, neither of which had a timeout of its own). The specific stuck item's namespace was also the literal string `"undefined/email-tone-polish"` — a separate, real frontend bug in the now-removed "Copy to my skills" one-click feature, not a gate-worker problem. The image-staleness half of this fix is a real `docker build`/restart, batched with every other fork's changes at the end of this round, not a code change.

**Fixed at the code level** (item 8): every gate stage now runs under a per-stage wall-clock timeout (`gate_service._run_stage_with_timeout()` — 30s for the four pure-Python stages, 60s for ethics, 180s for sandbox, looser than `sandbox/ecosystem_gate_executor.py`'s own inner 120s `container.wait()` cap specifically to also catch a stalled Docker image pull before that call). A timed-out stage resolves to `pending` (never `fail`) with a new `STAGE_TIMEOUT` finding, and the whole run's `finished_at` is deliberately left unset in that case — `gate_health_service.sweep_stuck_gate_runs()`'s **existing** recovery query (`pending` + `finished_at IS NULL` + started long enough ago) picks it up and re-enqueues it automatically, with its own existing cooldown; no second, parallel retry system was built. The existing `GET /ecosystem/admin/gate-health` endpoint (task B-6) was never actually consumed anywhere — `AdminGateFindings.tsx` now polls it every 30s and shows a warning banner when the worker looks unhealthy or the queue is backed up.

**Item 6** — the Verification tab now shows real, live per-stage progress instead of just the final resolved verdict: a new `ecosystem_gate_runs.stage_timings` JSONB column (`db/migrate.py`'s Part AD9) records each stage's status/duration as it actually finishes, written by both `run_gate()` and `run_fast_path_gate()`. `GET /ecosystem/items/{id}/gate-runs` now also returns `average_stage_durations_ms` (a recency-windowed mean over the last 20 resolved runs of the SAME path — fast path and full gate never mixed) so the tab can show a live ETA while a run is still in progress (polled every 2s while `finished_at` is null). The tab shows exactly the stages this run's own path has — fast path: manifest + static_safety; full gate: + license/supply_chain/ethics/mcp_connector, + sandbox only if the version has bundled files — plus a one-line explanation of which path this run took and why.

**Tests, run for real** against the newly-isolated `ainxt_test` database (see item 0's entry below): `tests/services/ecosystem/test_gate_stage_timeouts.py` (new, 3 tests) proves a slow stage resolves to `pending` with `STAGE_TIMEOUT` (not a crash), the existing sweeper picks it up with no new retry mechanism, and a timeout never produces `fail`. `packages/ecosystem-ui/src/components/detail/Verification.test.tsx` (new, 5 tests) proves the stage list matches fast-path vs. full-gate (with/without sandbox), the ETA renders while in progress, and the empty state still works. Full existing gate suite (90 tests across `test_gate_enqueue_resilience.py`/`test_gate_health_service.py`/`gate/`/`test_create_service.py`) re-run clean, 0 regressions. Full `ecosystem-ui` suite: 124/124 pass (includes other forks' concurrent work landing in the same round). `tsc --noEmit` clean, package build succeeds.

Files: `services/ecosystem/gate_service.py`, `services/ecosystem/gate_health_service.py` (read, unchanged — its existing sweeper is reused as-is), `db/migrate.py` (Part AD9), `db/models.py`, `routers/ecosystem_router.py`, `packages/ecosystem-ui/src/components/detail/Verification.tsx`, `AdminGateFindings.tsx`, `types.ts`, `EcosystemClient.ts`/`RealEcosystemClient.ts`/`MockEcosystemClient.ts`, `Detail.tsx` (passes `hasScripts` to `Verification`).
Design docs: `LLD/gate.md`.

## 2026-09-27 — item 2: "Copy to my skills" removed; Detail header is now Add / "Installed ▾" / Blocked; Contents parses SKILL.md frontmatter

**"Copy to my skills"** (commit `46b604e`) had a real, live-reproduced bug — a gate run in the shared dev DB carried the literal namespace `"undefined/email-tone-polish"`, from `handleCopyClick()`'s `${config.caller_default_namespace_prefix}/${nameSegment}` templating in a JS `undefined` at request time. Rather than chase that bug, removed the feature entirely per the user's own decision, following this project's reference mock (`docs/ecosystem/claude_ui_refs/ainxt_customize_mock.html`'s own `installedPop()`) instead: the header's single action slot is now **Add** (not installed), **"Installed ▾"** (installed — a popover: Manage in Yours, Enable/Disable, Versions & rollback, a divider, then Uninstall), or nothing (`blocked`). Uninstall is locked with an explanatory note (not hidden outright) for a `scope === "required"` install, matching the real server-side refusal already enforced in `installs_service.uninstall()` — a real gap this exposed: nothing on `ItemSummary`/`ItemDetail` exposed the install's own `scope` for the UI to check this against; added a new `install_scope` field (`items_service._item_to_summary()`, which already computed the caller's own install for `allowed_actions`, just never exposed this piece of it) — see `CONTRACTS.md` §9.

New `packages/ecosystem-ui/src/components/detail/InstalledMenu.tsx`, reusing `KebabMenu.tsx`'s `MenuItem` (now exported, gained `disabled`/`note` props) and `PopoverAnchor` for positioning/mutual-exclusion. "Manage in Yours" navigates to the catalog route with no new routing state needed — `CatalogScreen.tsx` already defaults to the "yours" view whenever the caller has any install of that type, which is always true for the very item being managed here.

**Contents tab frontmatter**: Preview mode dumped SKILL.md's raw text, `---\n...\n---` frontmatter block included, as one run-on line instead of parsing it. Added a tiny, deliberately PyYAML-free frontmatter parser (mirrors `AgentStudio/backend/skill_factory/pipeline.py`'s own `parse_frontmatter()` line-by-line convention — no existing client-side equivalent found to reuse) — Preview now shows a small metadata block (name/description/license from the parsed frontmatter, category from `item.category` itself, since `category` was never a valid SKILL.md frontmatter key) above the markdown BODY, frontmatter stripped. Code/raw view is unaffected — the exact original file, frontmatter included.

**Side-panel clipping**: the Detail page's flex row (`RiskSidePanel` + main content) had no wrap behavior — a fixed 220px side-panel column plus a `flex: 1` main column could overflow the row at narrower widths with no way to shrink, clipping instead. Added `flexWrap: "wrap"` plus sane `flex-basis`/`min-width` on both columns so the panel drops below the main content instead of clipping (no CSS file exists for this component to express a real breakpoint in).

Tests: `Detail.test.tsx` rewritten for the Add/Installed▾/Blocked states, the Installed▾ popover's exact contents, the Required-lock, and each menu action's real handler call (15 tests, all real); `Contents.test.tsx` (+2, frontmatter metadata block + Code view keeping the raw file). `tsc --noEmit` clean (ignoring pre-existing, unrelated errors from a concurrent in-flight fork's own `GateRunsResponse` type change); both the package and `ai-ui` production builds succeed.

Files: `packages/ecosystem-ui/src/components/Detail.tsx`, `Detail.test.tsx`, `detail/InstalledMenu.tsx` (new), `detail/Contents.tsx`, `detail/Contents.test.tsx`, `KebabMenu.tsx`, `client/fixtures.ts`, `client/MockEcosystemClient.ts`, `Card.stories.tsx`, `detail/EditContent.test.tsx`, `types.ts`; `services/ecosystem/items_service.py`; `routers/ecosystem_router.py`.
